"I apologize, but you should not have accepted the Poison King and the Demon Physician. Especially the Poison King—he is the most dangerous person in the martial world. There is no telling what he might do after entering our Alliance. More than anything, I am worried for the Alliance Lord's safety."

He tried to change the Alliance Lord's mind even now, putting his concern for the Lord at the forefront.

But it was a futile effort, for he had no idea what Baek Jagang was currently thinking.

"When they come, watch them thoroughly."

If they were really coming, Baek Jagang wouldn't have let them. Let the Poison King into the Sa Do Maeng? Absolutely impossible. He wouldn't be comfortable drinking even a cup of water after that day.

That was something Hyeok Sagun could also figure out. That was why Baek Jagang had made the first move.

"But why is the Young Cult Leader so stubborn?"

A sharp gaze probing Hyeok Sagun flew toward him.

‘You didn't really scheme this, did you?’

He had always been a suspicious man. If he trusted Hyeok Sagun blindly, it would feel unnatural instead. It was a suspicion to block suspicion.

"He seems to be stalling for time."

"For what?"

"I shall find out."

No matter how clever Hyeok Sagun was, he couldn't contend with Baek Jagang, who had weathered countless battles. Baek Jagang shook him relentlessly to prevent him from thinking stray thoughts.

This time, he unleashed a cold killing intent, revealing his anger toward Geom Muguk.

"Calling the Demon Physician and the Poison King to verify is truly an impudent trick. Is it not an insult to this Alliance's honor?"

"You speak rightly. Moreover, even if he promises the Gangseo region matter in writing, he may not keep that promise."

Hyeok Sagun pointed out new problems, trying somehow to change his mind, but it wasn't easy.

"The Young Cult Leader is a man full of arrogance. He'll keep his promise somehow, if only for his pride. Geom Ujin must be fuming."

Hyeok Sagun forced himself to hold back the sigh he wanted to let out.

If the Poison King and the Demon Physician came and revealed that the Chief Strategist's illness was due to poisoning, he was finished. He was the one who had obtained poison from Hwang Seokgyeong and administered it.

Until now, several things had hindered his meteoric rise. Blocking his path, tripping him up. Each time, he had overcome them with Hwang Seokgyeong's help.

But this time was different. The scale of the obstacle was catastrophic. It was greater than all the bad things that had happened so far combined.

"Then I shall take my leave."

He bowed politely and turned to leave, but Baek Jagang spoke from behind.

"Investigate it well. We can't afford to be manipulated by that young brat, can we?"

"Do not worry, Alliance Lord."

Hyeok Sagun bowed his head with a reliable expression, then turned away again.

‘Because of that young brat, the Alliance Lord is going to die.’

* * *

That night, Hyeok Sagun appeared at the place where he had previously met Tu Wang.

Far off in the field, Hwang Seokgyeong's figure could be seen.

Today as well, he had arrived first and was waiting. Hwang Seokgyeong was never late. Upon seeing him, the suffocating feeling he had felt all day while looking at Geom Muguk seemed to melt away.

Only now did he understand. That suffocation earlier had been an instinctive signal that he needed someone's help. Only someone like Hwang Seokgyeong could place the desired order with that innkeeper.

Hyeok Sagun cut straight to the chase.

"The Young Cult Leader has called the Demon Physician and the Poison King."

Tu Wang's expression hardened. He immediately realized why they were coming.

"It's because of the Chief Strategist."

"That's right. That brat of a Young Cult Leader is no ordinary foe."

Tu Wang first asked Hyeok Sagun for his opinion. Even when the decision was made, he always asked the strategist first.

"What would you have us do?"

Hyeok Sagun had already prepared his heart on the way here.

"Is there a way to stop the Poison King and the Demon Physician from coming?"

Tu Wang pondered for a moment, then shook his head.

They couldn't recklessly kill the Poison King and the Demon Physician, and even if it were a situation where they had to, if the opponent was the Poison King, it wouldn't be easy. If they didn't kill him in one blow, their side would be annihilated. That was the Poison King.

"Then there is only one way. Although the timing has moved up greatly from what we planned, haven't we made many preparations for this moment?"

This decision would change his and Hwang Seokgyeong's fate. It would change the fate of their subordinates, the fate of the Sa Do Maeng, and further, the fate of the demonic faction, and the fate of the entire martial world.

Hyeok Sagun asked with a grave gaze.

"Are you prepared?"

Compared to the solemn Hyeok Sagun, Tu Wang looked at ease. He had lived his life thinking of it as a battle. Because he had lived with the mindset that losing meant death at every moment, he could answer at any time.

Under the bright moonlight, Tu Wang answered as if he had been waiting.

"I am always prepared."

* * *

At the same time, another meeting was taking place elsewhere.

Geom Muguk stood in the field where he had met the Sa Do Maeng Lord earlier.

Baek Jagang entered there.

"I thought you would come here. First, allow me to apologize."

"Lying to me is fine."

"No, it's not because of that. Because of my lie, the Alliance Lord has been put in danger. They will think that if the Demon Physician and the Poison King investigate together, the poisoning will definitely be revealed. In the end, they will try to settle this before those two arrive."

"How will they settle it?"

"They won't simply flee."

Words that should not have left Geom Muguk's mouth spilled out.

"They will try to kill the Alliance Lord."

Even at the words that they would kill him, Baek Jagang showed no particular reaction.

"And they will pin that crime on me. They will never miss that opportunity."

Geom Muguk continued speaking in a calm tone.

"Originally, this would have happened years later, or perhaps a decade later. However, the timing changed because your disciple and I became friends."

They were words that should have been 'because I appeared.' But Geom Muguk said it was because he had become friends with Bisain. He tried to show off their friendship at every opportunity.

Dreaming of unifying the martial world was something his father alone was enough for. Simply stopping his father was already a sufficiently difficult task.

"You will step away from this fight."

Of course, since it was the Sa Do Maeng's affair, he should follow the Alliance Lord's will.

But because he knew the opponent was Tu Wang, he couldn't do that.

He thought that if they fought one-on-one now, the Sa Do Maeng Lord would win against Tu Wang.

The problem was that Tu Wang would use any means necessary to win. Tu Wang was someone obsessed with victory, not one who pursued fairness.

"Are you worried about me?"

"If you get hurt, I won't be able to face your father."

"That won't happen. I've properly learned qinggong, so I'm quite confident in running away."

Baek Jagang figured there must be a reason Geom Muguk wanted to join this fight.

"Why do you want to join this fight?"

Geom Muguk answered honestly.

"It's because of the Young Alliance Lord. He would want to join this fight more than I. Since he hasn't fully recovered from his injuries, I will watch over him."

No goosebumps rose on Baek Jagang's ears.

He suddenly thought,

of a martial world where the Demonic Cult Leader and the Sa Do Maeng Lord are friends.

But not a single scene came to mind. Such a martial world did not exist for him.

Then, Geom Muguk's words from before came to mind.

—Whether it be my demonic path or the Young Alliance Lord's path, I believe there must be a new ideal in our era.

"I'll say it again, that friendship won't come to pass."

Then Geom Muguk said something startling.

"I am also someone who believes in the friendship between you, Alliance Lord, and my father."

"What? Your father?"

At times like this, it was bewildering. The look in his eyes as he spoke of a friendship between the Heavenly Demon and the Sa Do Maeng Lord was not that of an innocent, naive bumpkin dreaming pure dreams. That clever, sly young brat was speaking from the heart.

"Haven't you properly spoken with my father? From what I see, the two of you seem like you'd get along well."

"While aiming to kill each other, you mean?"

Baek Jagang felt his fondness for Geom Muguk deepen each time they met. He felt an inexplicable sense of kinship and was curious what this lad would say next.

And more than anything, it was fun being with him. Even now, weren't they talking about a friendship between the Heavenly Demon and the Sa Do Maeng Lord that no martial artist in the world had ever imagined?

‘If his father is like this...’

Perhaps? he thought, then immediately shook his head. Just remembering Geom Ujin's eyes looking down on him made his blood boil; friends, indeed!

"Please allow me to fight alongside the Young Alliance Lord!"

"So be it."

It was a permission that could never have happened under normal circumstances. Baek Jagang was not the type to let an outsider intervene in the Sa Do Maeng's affairs.

But just this once, he decided to acknowledge their friendship.

"Enjoy the friendship while it lasts, for it will soon shatter."

"Thank you."

The path to becoming the Sa Do Maeng Lord had not been smooth for Baek Jagang.

To prevent his heart from weakening, he had not even kept a family. He had believed that was right back then, but now that he had grown old, there were times when he occasionally envisioned a different life.

Especially now, looking at Geom Ujin's son, those feelings grew stronger.

‘Bisain, you're going to have a hard time from now on.’

You can't lose to Geom Ujin's son, now can you?

Geom Muguk and Baek Jagang looked up at the moon together—the same moon Tu Wang and Hyeok Sagun had gazed upon.

"After this matter is settled, may I ask a single favor?"

"What favor?"

"I will tell you after it ends."

In all his life, who would have dared tell Baek Jagang they would state their condition later? That was why this moment was still fresh, and why he could nod out of curiosity rather than annoyance.

"They will pour out all the power they've gathered until now."

"To face me..."

Baek Jagang spoke, his narrow eyes brimming with bright moonlight.

"They should have gathered a lot more."

Absolute Regression — Episode 366

Chapter 366: Because You Looked Lonely.
---------------------------------------
When Geom Mugeuk returned to the safe house, Bi Sain was in the courtyard, circulating his qi and regulating his breath.

“You’re back?”

Bi Sain greeted Geom Mugeuk with a peaceful expression.

Geom Mugeuk studied him closely, then asked with a suspicious look.

“Who are you?”

Thinking this was just another prank, Bi Sain gave no reply.

“I asked who you are. My friend, the Young Alliance Leader, is not a man who wears such a peaceful expression. When my friend is in a good mood, he looks like he’s handing a challenge letter to his sworn enemy. Get out of my friend’s body this instant!”

Bi Sain shook his head with a look of disbelief.

“Can’t you go even one day without joking around?”

“I have to joke as much as I can while I’m still even a year younger. Look at your master and my father. How stiff and boring are they? We’ll end up like that when we get old too.”

“You’ll still be joking around even when you reach that age, won’t you?”

Unable to deny it, Geom Mugeuk grinned.

“How did things go?”

“The discussion went well. Nothing much happened, except that they’re going to ambush the Alliance Leader.”

“What!”

Knowing that Geom Mugeuk was not the sort to joke about such matters, Bi Sain quickly asked,

“What happened?”

Geom Mugeuk told Bi Sain everything that had occurred.

“I drove him into a corner that way. Now the only method left for them is to kill the Alliance Leader and seize the Sado Alliance. I’m sorry. There was no other way to draw them out.”

Bi Sain might well have grown angry, saying he had placed the Alliance Leader in danger, but instead he silently nodded.

“Why aren’t you angry?”

“Didn’t you say there was no other way?”

Geom Mugeuk could clearly feel that his relationship with Bi Sain was different from before. The man who had once stood far away was now standing right before him.

“They won’t dare attack while he is in the Alliance Leader’s Hall.”

The Alliance Leader’s Hall itself was a fortress, and countless hidden experts guarded its surroundings. One or two might be able to enter by following Hyeok Sagun, but from the start, it was impossible for a large number of enemies to infiltrate.

“They’ll aim for him when he leaves the Alliance. The Deputy Strategist is the first to know the Alliance Leader’s schedule.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded, as if he had been thinking the same thing.

“The reason I said the Demon Physician and the Poison King would arrive in ten days was because I wanted you to take part in this fight.”

A tremor passed over Bi Sain’s face. Because his face was so hideous and frightening, that tremor left an even more powerful impression.

“I know you’re thinking of me, but there’s no need.”

Bi Sain spoke words Geom Mugeuk had not expected.

“Plan without taking me into account.”

“Will that be all right?”

Bi Sain nodded.

Do not grow accustomed to it.

After hearing those words from his master, Bi Sain had been able to steady his heart. He had realized that now was not the time to fight, but the time to change his bandages. It was not the time to boil over, but the time to cool down.

Geom Mugeuk could feel it. Bi Sain had grown a great deal.

“You’ve changed a lot.”

“Thanks to someone.”

Their gazes intertwined gently in the air.

“Then rest.”

Geom Mugeuk turned to leave, but Bi Sain spoke from behind him.

“Thank you.”

It was only a single phrase, but it contained Bi Sain’s sincerity.

“Then don’t just settle it with words. Grant me one request.”

It was the same request he had made of Baek Jagang.

“Tell me.”

“I’ll tell you once this matter is over.”

Then came an astonishing promise from Bi Sain.

“Whatever it is, I’ll grant it without fail.”

To say he would grant anything without even hearing what it was—and for those words to come from the mouth of a man who would certainly keep his promise—was greater and more valuable than any expression of gratitude.

It was also the moment when the basket filled with gratitude was emptied.

“What if I ask for your position? How can you say you’ll grant anything?”

As Bi Sain turned and entered the building, he said,

“Then I’ll challenge you for the position of Young Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk shouted after him.

“One look at my father and those words will vanish from your mouth!”

* * *

The next day, Geom Mugeuk went to the Alliance Leader’s Hall again.

As he walked down the corridor, he stopped before the steel wolf that stood there again today.

“Do you like that wolf?”

At the unexpected question, Geom Mugeuk looked in surprise at Ingung, the captain of the guards who had been guiding him.

On the way here today, too, he had thrown all manner of questions at Ingung. When did he sleep while serving as a guard? How much stealth technique did one have to learn to reach such a level? Who controlled the poison needle holes beneath the Alliance Leader’s Hall? Of course, Ingung had not answered a single word.

And yet now Ingung had spoken first.

“Do you know? Since we first met, this is the first time you’ve spoken to me.”

“You seemed to be looking closely at that wolf.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned back to the wolf. It would have been like him to joke, So you spoke not because of me, but because of the wolf, but instead he spoke calmly, his eyes deepened.

“It looked lonely, somehow.”

That was why he liked it. It seemed like his father, and also like the Geugak Soma sitting alone in a pure white room, and also like the Leader of the Sado Alliance walking through a windswept field, and also like this captain of the guards silently guiding him. And it also seemed like himself in the life before his regression.

Ingung’s gaze also turned to the wolf. It was an answer he had not expected. He had passed through this place countless times, but not once had he ever thought such a thing. The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult was certainly different from the martial artists he had seen until now. Then again, that was probably why he was standing in this corridor.

Geom Mugeuk spoke again, unexpectedly.

“I feel as if this wolf will come to life someday.”

Those words, too, were sincere. Because that was the feeling he had. He did not know what it was, but there was a strange pull that drew him in.

Ingung looked at Geom Mugeuk. Among his own subordinates, not one believed that legend.

‘You believe what even we do not.’

Geom Mugeuk stroked the wolf’s head once.

“You there, don’t doze off. Guard this place properly!”

Guided by Ingung once more, he resumed walking.

Before the doors of the Alliance Leader’s Hall opened, Geom Mugeuk said to Ingung,

“He is strong.”

Whenever Geom Mugeuk met the Leader of the Sado Alliance, Ingung had always been with them.

Having heard every conversation while concealed, he knew what Geom Mugeuk meant.

“Understood.”

Ingung answered politely.

It was something the captain of the guards, at least, had to know, and so Geom Mugeuk had reminded him of the Fighting King’s strength.

The doors opened, and Geom Mugeuk went inside.

Baek Jagang, the Leader of the Sado Alliance, sat in the Taishi chair with his eyes closed, lost in thought. Geom Mugeuk knew better than anyone how important such moments of contemplation were for experts of the Alliance Leader’s caliber, so he walked forward while making as little presence as possible.

Only when Geom Mugeuk had approached the foot of the Taishi chair did Baek Jagang finally speak.

“Was your plan to come and go so freely, then kill me?”

It was Baek Jagang’s joke about how soundlessly he had approached.

“If one wishes to kill the Leader of the Sado Alliance, shouldn’t one put in at least this much effort? Ah, but I suppose I must train my movement technique further. I think I’ll need to approach at least ten more steps in secrecy to seat my friend in that position.”

Baek Jagang’s eyes snapped open. As expected, banter was only amusing when the other person said it so freely, without watching his mood.

“Have you made a plan?”

“As you know, there are problems with the strategists in our Alliance. What do you think?”

“It is not as if I have any particular thoughts.”

Contrary to his words, Geom Mugeuk had already formed a plan.

“They won’t move while you remain in the Alliance, so there must be a natural reason for you to leave. Only then will they not suspect anything.”

Baek Jagang nodded, and Geom Mugeuk continued.

“You will go out to welcome the Demon Physician and the Poison King. The Chief Strategist, who has fallen ill, is currently being hidden outside, is he not?”

During their last meeting, Geom Mugeuk had asked Baek Jagang to take measures, saying Hyeok Sagun might kill him in order to eliminate evidence. Thus, the Alliance Leader had taken the Chief Strategist to a secret location.

“Use the pretext that you do not want to allow the Poison King and the Demon Physician into the Sado Alliance, and go out to meet them. Tell Hyeok Sagun that you intend to take them directly to the Chief Strategist. For them, there could be no better opportunity. They may not believe that I killed the Alliance Leader alone, but if they are told that the Poison King poisoned you to death, they will believe it.”

Baek Jagang was impressed. He thought that perhaps Geom Mugeuk had been thinking this far ahead from the very beginning, when he first lied that the Poison King and the Demon Physician were coming in order to corner Hyeok Sagun.

“Let us do that.”

Baek Jagang accepted Geom Mugeuk’s plan.

“What did Sain say about this matter?”

“The Young Alliance Leader said that he need not fight, so I should make the plan without minding him.”

Baek Jagang felt that the words he had conveyed had properly reached his disciple.

“Even so, I will bring the Young Alliance Leader out with me.”

“Why?”

“Because this is the Young Alliance Leader’s fight as well. If he misses this chance, he will regret this day for the rest of his life. I do not wish to leave my friend with such regret. Do not worry. I will protect him well.”

No goosebumps rose along Baek Jagang’s ears.

There were people for whom this sense worked, and people for whom it did not. He found himself thinking that it would have been better if Geom Mugeuk were someone for whom it did not work.

In nearly every relationship where this sense had worked, what remained in the end had been disappointment. It was also the greatest reason he did not trust people.

‘Can you become the exception?’

The thought likely arose because of all the unexpected things this young Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult had shown him thus far. But surely that could not be.

“Do not worry about your friend. Worry about yourself. Do you know how many poison needle holes are beneath your feet right now?”

With an exaggerated gasp, Geom Mugeuk made a fuss.

“I suppose I’d better not look at the floor at all! Fortunately, I like looking at the sky.”

* * *

Several days passed in that way, and the day before the Alliance Leader was to leave the Alliance arrived.

“Good grief. Tomorrow, another wretched fight begins.”

At Instructor Cha’s complaint, Hwang Seokgyeong smiled as he moved training equipment with him. Tomorrow was the day a new group would enter the Samaeng Hall. Whenever their short leave ended, this was the complaint Instructor Cha always made, like an annual ritual.

“Think of them as the foundation stones that will protect the Alliance.”

“They may be precious foundation stones to you, popular as you are. To me, they’re wretched. I wonder what sort of brat will make my blood boil this time.”

After setting down the load he had brought, Hwang Seokgyeong said to Instructor Cha,

“By the way, I won’t be able to come tomorrow.”

“You? Why not? Are you ill?”

Instructor Cha was startled. The most diligent person in the Samaeng Hall was Hwang Seokgyeong. Even on ordinary days, he never missed a single day, so to hear that he could not come on the first day of receiving a new group was bound to shock him.

“There is somewhere I must go.”

“Where?”

Hwang Seokgyeong only smiled and did not answer.

“I’ll lock the storeroom door and leave, so you go on ahead.”

“You will?”

Instructor Cha, who had been walking away, turned back and asked,

“If there’s anything I can help with, tell me.”

Looking at him, Hwang Seokgyeong smiled brightly.

“It is all right. It is something I must do myself.”

Something only I can do.

After parting with Instructor Cha in that way, Hwang Seokgyeong returned to the storeroom from which they had taken the training equipment.

Entering the storeroom, he lifted away the dust-covered bundles piled in a corner. They were unused tools.

When he operated an intricate mechanism hidden in the wall, a door leading underground opened.

As Hwang Seokgyeong entered, the door closed automatically.

There, fragments of night pearls were embedded in the walls, faintly illuminating the view. It was a small secret chamber.

A treasured garment hung on the wall directly ahead. If they learned what it was, nine martial artists out of ten would scream.

Immortal.

The Immortal Divine Armor was his most cherished treasured garment and the protective armor he wore only for important battles.

After completely removing his clothes, he donned Immortal. Made by weaving together various special fabrics and heavenly silkworm thread, it clung tightly to his body as if wrapping around it. Thin yet powerful, it was an absolute divine artifact of the martial world that could not be obtained even for a thousand gold pieces.

That was not all. Hwang Seokgyeong fastened guards to protect his wrists and shins.

Twin Souls.

These protective guards named Twin Souls could not compare to the Immortal Divine Armor, but they were still rare artifacts of the jianghu that could not be obtained easily.

Next, he wrapped around himself a leather belt for holding daggers. When he opened the box placed beside him, it was filled with daggers. Like the divine artifacts before them, this leather belt and the daggers were no common objects.

Hwang Seokgyeong slowly and carefully placed them into the leather belt.

His movements as he prepared for battle were calm and solemn.

The last thing he put on was a pair of gloves.

War God.

Also made from special materials, these gloves were so light it felt as if he were not wearing them at all. They protected his hands not only from sword qi but even from sword aura, and they were rare treasures that drew the power of his fists to the utmost limit.

It was the first time. The first time he was going out to fight while wearing all of these things.

After putting his outer clothes back on, Hwang Seokgyeong left the secret chamber and went back up.

He piled the original bundles back over the entrance to the secret chamber. And there, he also set down his own sword. Today would be the last time he entered this place.

When he finally laid down his sword, he at last changed from Hwang Seokgyeong, instructor of the Samaeng Hall, into the Fighting King on the eve of the decisive battle.

Absolute Regression, Chapter 367

Chapter 367: It's Okay to Be a Little Less Cool.
------------------------------------------------
Late that night, Bi Sa-in was pacing alone in the courtyard of the safe house.

Tomorrow was finally the day the Alliance Leader would leave the alliance.

He had said he would sit it out, but—

‘Should I have said I would go?’

The reason he had said he would not was because he worried that, with his body not fully healed, he would only become a burden.

But if, by some chance, something were to happen to the Alliance Leader, he would spend the rest of his life regretting that he had not been there.

Surprisingly, he was not worried about Geom Mugeuk. Even if he ran away, he would manage just fine on his own. He was the sort of man who, even if he fell into water, would persuade the fish and make his way out.

‘Will the Alliance Leader understand my feelings later? What if he misunderstands and thinks I didn’t come because I was afraid?’

Soon, Bi Sa-in shook his head. To be worrying about himself in a situation like this.

Just then, someone walked out from behind him and spoke.

“Your heart is troubled, so you cannot sleep, can you?”

When he turned, it was Goeak.

“You said it was tomorrow?”

“Yes.”

Bi Sa-in wanted to ask Goeak to go and help the Alliance Leader. He even wanted to say, This is an order from the Young Alliance Leader, so go.

But he could not. He himself was not going to a dangerous place where they might die, so how could he tell Goeak to go? If Goeak had intended to help, he would have spoken first. He would have said that he would go and lend his aid.

“Do not worry too much. You know what kind of person the Alliance Leader is, don’t you?”

He knew. He knew better than anyone. That was why he was worried. The Alliance Leader was not a man who would ever back down. Ordinarily, he was cold and realistic, but when it came to battle, he was a man of fire. He was the greatest true man Bi Sa-in knew.

And, as always, there was someone else who solved his worries.

“If you’re worried, let’s go together!”

Startled, he turned and saw Geom Mugeuk entering.

“Why are you here?”

“Why else? I came to take you with me.”

As he said that, he held something out. In Geom Mugeuk’s hand was a suit of protective armor.

It was the very armor that had saved his life before, thanks to wearing two layers of it. The one that had been soaked in blood was now clean.

“I came earlier and washed it by the stream. I’m going to see my master for the first time in ages—how could I go reeking of blood?”

It truly had been washed clean, and it was completely dry.

“I was afraid it would smell if it wasn’t dry enough, so I put it on myself and dried it by circulating my heat-yang energy.”

“What?”

“I made quite a scene by myself in the middle of the night.”

Bi Sa-in pictured Geom Mugeuk standing by the stream, washing that protective armor, then putting it on and drying it with heat-yang energy.

Astonishment, bewilderment, gratitude. Several emotions surged over him all at once, and Bi Sa-in stood there blankly for a moment.

Truly, this Young Cult Leader was a man impossible to predict by even an inch. And the emotion that adorned the end of all those feelings was joy.

A bright smile spread across Bi Sa-in’s fierce face.

“Fine. Let’s go.”

Going was the right choice. Seeing how happy he felt, he knew it was right.

“That’s right. This is a matter of the Sado Alliance. You should go.”

Bi Sa-in nodded vigorously.

Once the fog in his heart cleared, a man stood there. A disciple who wanted to protect his master, a man who was unafraid even of losing his life.

Yes, let’s do it. Let’s give it a try. If I die, then I’ll die there.

“Help me, my friend.”

At the added word friend, Geom Mugeuk broke into a broad smile.

“Of course. Am I not your one true friend? Never forget that I am the only friend who risked his life to help you when you were in trouble! And the friend who will later roam the beaches with you is also me. As for the young ladies, I’ll win them all over for you!”

Bi Sa-in knew that, unexpectedly, Geom Mugeuk was weak to being credited for his deeds. He found being thanked burdensome, and he disliked boasting.

He thought that perhaps, within the fog hidden behind all that chatter, there might be a man standing there who was even quieter and more taciturn than himself.

Bi Sa-in put on one suit of protective armor and held the other out to Geom Mugeuk.

“You wear one.”

“I’m already wearing mine. So wear two layers again this time.”

The top-grade Heavenly Silkworm Silk that had been tied around the hilt of the Black Demon Sword was already wrapped around the vital parts of Geom Mugeuk’s body.

At that moment, Goeak, who had been listening to the two of them, cut in.

“There may be poison applied inside the protective armor.”

It was a joke, but one with a barb. Advice telling him not to trust Geom Mugeuk completely.

“With you here, Senior, at the very least the Young Alliance Leader won’t be poisoned to death.”

Geom Mugeuk sincerely hoped the relationship between these two would develop well. Bi Sa-in, it’s up to you.

Bi Sa-in said to Goeak,

“Will you help me and go with me tomorrow?”

Goeak answered without hesitation.

“If the Young Alliance Leader goes, then I must go as well.”

He had not stepped forward of his own accord for the Alliance Leader, but at the very least, he had the heart to move for Bi Sa-in.

Bi Sa-in realized something. If he had forced Goeak earlier and told him to go, such a willing answer would not have come now. Because he had held back then, he had gained something now. He must not forget it. When our hearts are urgent, we easily forget the distance between ourselves and the other person.

As for the one who had the greatest influence on this realization, how very busy he was. Geom Mugeuk bid them farewell.

“Then I’ll see you tomorrow morning. I’ll come to pick you up.”

“Where are you going at this hour?”

After saying something inscrutable, Geom Mugeuk vanished in an instant.

“You’re not the only one I need to put protective armor on.”

* * *

Late that night, Baek Jagang was training in his own practice field, his upper body bare.

His opponent was an iron figure made by mixing in ten-thousand-year cold iron.

The iron figure became Geom Ujin, then Hwang Seokgyeong, and then himself.

He could have cut it down in a single stroke, but only crisp sounds continued to ring from the iron figure.

After swinging his sword for a while, Baek Jagang approached the iron figure.

Slowly, he ran his hand over the marks that had just been carved into it. So many lines had been drawn across its surface that the lines seemed to have become a plane.

And yet Baek Jagang knew exactly which marks among them had been left by him just now.

It was a realm higher than cutting ten-thousand-year cold iron, and the ones enjoying this great realm were his fingers, tracing the sword lines.

The lines were telling him that he had by no means easily inherited the seat of Alliance Leader. That he, too, was a man who had walked a road of blood to reach the grand seat.

At that moment, the voice of the captain of the guards came from outside.

“The Young Cult Leader has arrived.”

* * *

Geom Mugeuk was standing before the steel wolf again today.

“What brings you here at this late hour?”

“I couldn’t sleep, so I came to see you.”

If it were anyone else, Baek Jagang might have thought they were simply nervous, but not Geom Mugeuk. He must have a reason for coming.

“Why are you not inside?”

“Ah, because I like it here.”

Baek Jagang stood beside Geom Mugeuk.

“They say you said this wolf would come alive?”

It seemed the captain of the guards had relayed those words.

“For some reason, I think it would be nice if it did come alive.”

“Are you wishing for a crisis to befall this alliance?”

“Ah, I suppose I was. No wonder! I had such a strong feeling it would come alive.”

This was why conversation with Geom Mugeuk was amusing. He said things no one else would dare say so casually.

“You said it would be a formidable foe?”

Geom Mugeuk spoke into the air.

“Are you not telling the Alliance Leader too much of what I say? Are there no secrets between us?”

Only after joking like that did the playfulness vanish from Geom Mugeuk’s face.

“Yes. Among the enemies the Alliance Leader has encountered, he will probably be the strongest.”

“As strong as your father?”

“Not quite that strong.”

Before a relaxed smile could form on Baek Jagang’s lips, Geom Mugeuk spoke first.

“But he is not an enemy you can afford to let your guard down against.”

He knew such words might wound the pride of the Sado Alliance Leader, but even so, he gave the warning.

“Are you afraid I will die?”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk answered.

“I am.”

There was no reaction at Baek Jagang’s ear. This Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult was sincerely worried that he might die. How on earth was he supposed to take that?

“There must be treasured armor for the Alliance Leader. I ask that you wear it when you leave the alliance.”

“Why treasured armor?”

“The other side will be wearing it as well.”

Geom Mugeuk already knew from his life before regression that the Fighting King wore protective armor. Not only protective armor—if it could help him win a fight, he did not hesitate to use all manner of rare artifacts and divine objects.

The reason this was known was because the Fighting King was not at all ashamed of it. The only thing he was ashamed of was defeat.

A man whose skill was already as strong as the Demon Venerable’s, and who even used the greatest artifacts on top of that—this was why Geom Mugeuk was worried for the Sado Alliance Leader.

“I felt this before as well, but you seem to know quite a lot about him.”

What should he say?

“Yes. I know information about him.”

The longer one spoke, the more openings appeared, so he said nothing more. He had already told Baek Jagang before that similar conspiracies had taken place in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, the Murim Alliance, and the Pungcheon Cult beyond the borders, so Baek Jagang would likely assume he had learned it in the process of resolving those incidents.

“Please do not misunderstand. I am not saying this because I look down on you, Alliance Leader.”

“I know that. But let me ask you this.”

Baek Jagang stared intently at Geom Mugeuk and asked,

“If it were your father, would he wear it?”

After briefly thinking of his father, Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“He would not.”

Baek Jagang emitted a cold aura.

“Then why are you telling me to wear it?”

“Because I would have told my father to wear it as well.”

If the situation were the same, he would have advised his father too. Though his father would absolutely not have worn it.

“I know. I know the pride and self-respect you possess as a martial artist, Alliance Leader. Even if your opponent were to come wrapped in every rare treasure in the world, you would face him with a single sword. I know very well that both my father and you are such men. That is why he is the Cult Leader, and why you are the Alliance Leader.”

“Knowing that, why tell me to wear it?”

“Because I will wear it. I will go out wearing the best protective armor, layered over itself. Even if I become Cult Leader, I will do the same.”

Baek Jagang stared into Geom Mugeuk’s eyes, which did not waver by even an inch. He was curious why this young Young Cult Leader, who would shoulder the next generation, was saying such things.

“The honor of a Cult Leader, as I see it, does not lie in the elegance of winning under unfavorable conditions.”

“Then where does your honor lie?”

“It lies in the result of killing the ones who must be killed. That is why it is all right to be a little less impressive. I do not need to unfold some heroic tale. I simply want to return safely and see my people. I want to return safely, drink with them, and talk with them. I will not say a single word about an enemy who is already dead. I will take off the bloodstained protective armor, then laugh and make merry. People will think in their hearts, Yes, if it is the Cult Leader, he can surely kill those who must be killed. That is where my honor lies.”

It was something Baek Jagang had never once considered. Whenever he was with Geom Mugeuk, things he himself did not possess kept unfolding before him.

Everything Geom Mugeuk had said earlier had been for this conclusion.

“I hope that you are not hurt by even a hair’s breadth, Alliance Leader. I hope that becomes your honor.”

From the moment Geom Mugeuk began speaking until he finished, not a single hair rose by Baek Jagang’s ear.

* * *

The next day, a carriage carrying the Sado Alliance Leader was racing along.

The small number of martial artists running as they escorted the carriage were the Sado Alliance Leader’s guards. Only the most skilled among the guards had accompanied this secret journey.

Inside the carriage were Baek Jagang and the deputy strategist, Hyeok Sagun.

“Are you nervous?”

At Baek Jagang’s question, Hyeok Sagun, who had been looking out the window of the moving carriage, turned his head.

“No.”

“You look nervous, unlike usual.”

Of course, Hyeok Sagun was nervous.

His fate would be decided today.

He believed Hwang Seokgyeong would win, but that place would become an extremely dangerous battlefield. There was no telling what variables might threaten him.

“May I say one thing regarding this matter?”

“Go ahead.”

“To be honest, I felt disappointed. I wished that if you were going to trust me, you would have trusted me completely.”

“You were disappointed because I did not tell you where the chief strategist’s residence was.”

“Yes.”

Hyeok Sagun soon lowered his head and apologized.

“I apologize for my disloyal words, Alliance Leader.”

“No. You are human as well. It is only natural you might feel that way.”

Hyeok Sagun was doing his utmost to hide himself. The Alliance Leader was a sharp man. He was someone who could see through people with those small eyes.

That was why, today, he hid his excitement beneath disappointment. Because they had to reach the place where Hwang Seokgyeong was waiting.

How long had they traveled like that?

When the carriage slowly began to reduce its speed, Baek Jagang unexpectedly spoke.

“Today is the day the Sado Academy accepts new recruits, is it not?”

“That is correct. It is today.”

When Baek Jagang brought up the Sado Academy, Hyeok Sagun grew tense inwardly.

“I heard Instructor Hwang took leave today?”

“Yes. I heard it was the first time he had ever done so. But how did you know?”

“Is he not famous for his diligence? Do you know what happened?”

“I heard he had an urgent personal matter.”

“I think I know where that man went.”

At some point, the carriage had come to a stop.

Baek Jagang’s gaze turned out the window.

“That man is over there.”

Up ahead, the Fighting King stood blocking the road.

Revealing his aura in full, the Fighting King exuded a tremendous presence. The aura he was displaying now alone was enough to explain why Geom Mugeuk had been so worried.

The guard warriors, who had already received Ingung’s instructions, showed no agitation whatsoever.

Baek Jagang slowly stepped down from the carriage. The Fighting King’s fighting spirit came whirling in like blades, but Baek Jagang spoke with utmost composure.

“This is rather too flashy a vacation for you, is it not?”

Chapter 368: Hand It Over Peacefully, or Painfully.
---------------------------------------------------
Baek Jagang felt Tu Wang's fighting spirit surging through his entire body.

Penetrating his flesh, this sharp fighting spirit tore at him as if demanding he rush out immediately, and the very first thought that rose from it was this.

‘This is someone who has truly fought before!’

Baek Jagang's chest grew hot, and his fists clenched of their own accord. His opponent was certainly someone who stirred the primal destructive urge of a martial artist—the desire to sweep everything away in a single throw.

The emotion Tu Wang felt was similar, yet different. From just those comfortable eyes of his opponent, and a single remark asking if his vacation wasn't too splendid, every hair on his body stood on end at once.

‘One wrong move, and I die!’

This heart-dropping tension was precisely the emotion Tu Wang loved most.

He recalled the image of the Sado Alliance Lord fallen as a corpse.

Just imagining that moment sent thrilling shivers through his entire body. It would bring him a joy like nothing else in his lifetime. A joy greater than the combined pleasure of every victory he had won in battle up until now.

‘I will definitely win.’

The scorching fighting spirit and heat generated by the two swept through their surroundings.

Feeling the swirling energy, Hyeok Sagun belatedly stepped down from the carriage. Things were proceeding according to plan, yet strangely, he felt a foreboding premonition.

It was understandable that the Sado Alliance Lord's reaction was calm. He was an extraordinary person, after all.

But the reaction of the escort warriors weighed on his mind. Even though Hwang Seokgyeong blocked the path, they gazed calmly at Tu Wang as if they had anticipated this very moment.

Hyeok Sagun feigned ignorance and spoke to Tu Wang.

“Instructor Hwang, is the Alliance Lord not asking you something? By what right do you dare stand before the Alliance Lord and block his way?”

My lord, the day has finally come. May you achieve your grand endeavor!

Presently, Tu Wang suppressed his fighting spirit and calmly opened his mouth.

“You said my vacation was splendid? Very well. When this vacation ends, I shall return to the most splendid position in the Sado Alliance.”

When his opponent revealed a clear will to kill him, Baek Jagang smiled instead.

“My seat is not quite as splendid as you imagine.”

“I shall experience that emptiness for myself.”

Tu Wang lightly struck his right fist against his left palm.

Pa-ang!

At the signal of that great sonic boom, loud enough to burst eardrums, the hidden experts began to reveal themselves.

The first to appear was a middle-aged expert wearing a blade curved like a crescent moon.

Sinwoldo (New Moon Blade).

He was someone who belonged to the Seven Great Masters of the Sado Alliance. Following Gwangseom, who had earlier tried to kill Bisain, this was the moment another of the Seven Great Masters of the Sado Alliance appeared as an enemy.

“It has been a while, Alliance Lord.”

Sinwoldo politely greeted Baek Jagang.

“Have you been well?”

“Thanks to your excellent leadership of the Sado martial world, I have been well.”

“That is good.”

“I apologize in advance for matters coming to this.”

“It is fine. Such things can happen.”

Baek Jagang did not press him on why he had sided with Tu Wang. He must have been offered a condition sufficient to move him. It was only natural to act according to one's interests—such was Baek Jagang's belief.

The second to appear was an old man whose face was full of wrinkles. So wrinkled was he that he appeared to be over a hundred years old, though in truth he was not that aged.

Cheongsalgwi (Blue Killing Ghost).

He was a former supreme expert who belonged to neither the orthodox nor the unorthodox, a legendary figure publicly pursued by both orthodox and unorthodox alike. He was a villain of such ill repute, yet he had survived and revealed himself here today.

Cheongsalgwi's appearance was even more astonishing than Sinwoldo's. His internal energy was far deeper than that of the top experts of the current generation, making him an even more troublesome opponent.

“I saw you when you were young. Do you remember?”

Cheongsalgwi asked Baek Jagang, feigning familiarity.

Baek Jagang shook his head.

“Well, you were only so small back then.”

He lowered his palm as if measuring the height of a small child around the waist, then the hand abruptly twisted and unleashed a palm force.

Sssshhhreak!

Kwaaaang!

The one who blocked the incoming palm force, undulating with blue energy, was Ingung, the captain of the escort guards.

Overwhelmed in internal energy, he slid backward. Other guards supported his back and channeled their internal energy to him. When two more men behind them added their strength, they finally stopped sliding.

Cheongsalgwi smiled, revealing his few remaining teeth.

“Oops, my hand slipped.”

Ingung and the guards quietly stepped aside.

Baek Jagang, standing behind them, watched the newly appearing figure without a word.

This time, a hunchbacked old man with a cane slowly walked out.

Dokpaeja (Poison Tyrant).

He was a poison expert who had once made his name notorious. He had vanished after being publicly hunted for using mass-slaughter poison, forbidden in the martial world, but today he revealed himself here. It was this very Dokpaeja who had crafted the poison used to secretly afflict the Supreme Commander.

At Dokpaeja's appearance, Ingung's expression hardened. Dokpaeja was a more threatening figure than any of the enemies who had appeared before.

“Antidotes!”

At Ingung's command, the escort warriors simultaneously drew anti-poison pills from their bosoms and placed them in their mouths.

Dokpaeja giggled mockingly.

“How long do you think you can endure with those measly anti-poison pills of yours?”

The anti-poison pills had their limits in blocking poison.

If a chaotic battle broke out and Dokpaeja employed a hidden poison strike, the Alliance Lord himself would be endangered, not to mention their own safety.

Tu Wang, Sinwoldo, Cheongsalgwi, and Dokpaeja.

But they were not the only ones prepared.

From all directions, over fifty warriors revealed themselves. Surrounding the area with blazing eyes, they were the warriors later called the Turang, the Fighting Wolves, who followed Tu Wang.

They were also extremely belligerent experts, battle-crazed and unafraid of death. If the experts who appeared earlier were tigers, they were hunting hounds.

Seeing them, Hyeok Sagun smiled unconsciously. These were people who had long prepared for this day. Of course, he would have prepared more under normal circumstances, but this was enough.

‘No matter how strong the Alliance Lord is, he cannot endure.’

Pang! Pa-ang!

After once more striking his fist against his palm to alert the surroundings, Tu Wang issued an order to all.

“Kill all the guards first, then join the battle.”

The Turang all drew their weapons at once, radiating killing intent.

Tu Wang was not ashamed of fighting together. If he were someone to be ashamed of such a thing, Dokpaeja would not be here.

Baek Jagang could now understand why Geonmugeuk had pleaded with him yesterday.

Since the opponent was trying to kill him by any means necessary without discrimination, it had been a request not to place meaning on winning through a fair fight.

The escort warriors who had taken the anti-poison pills drew their swords and blocked the left, right, and rear of Baek Jagang, leaving only his front open.

In the hair-trigger situation where enemies steadily pressed in, someone shouted from afar.

“Wait just a moment!”

The shout from afar flew clearly to where they were.

“You there, the tall one sixth from the left! Put down your concealed weapon and wait just a moment!”

His eyesight was so keen that indeed, the sixth Turang from the left had been drawing a concealed weapon. He was tall as well.

Everyone looked toward the voice to see a carriage rushing toward them from afar.

Hyeok Sagun's expression soured. He had realized who owned that mischievous voice.

‘The Young Lord!’

Sure enough, the one driving the carriage from the coachman's seat was Geonmugeuk.

Tu Wang raised his hand to stop the Turang from charging. It was an order to stand and watch.

The carriage rushed up and stopped before Baek Jagang.

“Fortunately, you haven't started yet.”

Sinwoldo, Cheongsalgwi, and Dokpaeja looked at Tu Wang with puzzled expressions. To their silent question of what this situation was, Tu Wang spoke to them.

“That one is the Young Lord of the Demon Cult.”

As if they had already heard that Geonmugeuk had involved himself in this matter, the three nodded and directed renewed gazes at him.

“He is younger than I thought.”

Geonmugeuk recognized them. He was not someone who would fail to know who the hunchbacked poison expert or the expert with the curved blade was.

“You are scarier people than I thought.”

Far from showing courtesy, Geonmugeuk's attitude was so excessively relaxed that it seemed like a joke, making them all frown. Of course, they did not know that the joke had not even begun.

The carriage door opened, and Bisain stepped down first.

Then Geonmugeuk introduced him in a loud voice, like someone introducing a martial artist at a tournament.

“Here is the successor of the Sado Alliance, who has returned alive like an immortal after escaping the brink of death! He may be ashamed of me, but he is certainly my friend as well!”

Bisain was no longer fazed by this level of joking.

Bisain immediately greeted Baek Jagang in a trembling voice.

“It has been a while, Master.”

The gaze with which the master looked at his disciple was incomparably gentle.

“Are you in good health?”

“Thanks to your concern, I am well.”

Seeing the situation unfolding, Bisain thought that he had truly done well to come.

Next, Goak descended from the carriage.

“Here is Goak, one of the Seven Great Masters of the Sado Alliance, who has recently tasted the bitterness of life through a friend's betrayal!”

He says something like that in this situation? Ah, this guy is truly insane! Goak also pretended not to hear and greeted Baek Jagang politely with cupped fists.

“Have you been well?”

“It has been a while.”

“The Young Lord cares deeply for the Alliance Lord.”

These words held the meaning that they had come to this place for the Young Lord. Bisain was grateful to Goak for speaking thus on his behalf.

Bisain recalled how Geonmugeuk treated him. If he could do even half of that, he could build a good relationship with Goak.

Next, Goak's gaze turned to Sinwoldo.

“Why are you...?”

Goak could not understand. Sinwoldo was the one among the Seven Great Masters of the Sado Alliance least interested in the affairs of the martial world. Yet he had revealed himself here.

“Things happened to turn out this way.”

“I never imagined the day would come when I would fight you.”

“The Sado Alliance is the Sado Alliance, after all.”

“Indeed. Since it has come to this, let us fight well without regrets.”

“Let us.”

The most surprised and bewildered person at the appearance of these three was Hyeok Sagun.

The original plan had been to naturally step back and retreat once the pincer attack began.

The guards would be too busy protecting the Alliance Lord to pay any attention to him.

But with their appearance, the plan had encountered a problem. He had to retreat, or go behind Hwang Seokgyeong over there.

Would that Geonmugeuk really let him go?

On the other hand, Tu Wang was not shaken in the slightest even with a variable arising. He would smash through even variables—such was his mindset.

“Kill them all!”

At that moment, yet others appeared.

“Stop!”

Thirteen experts descended at that spot with dazzling movement arts. They were the Sado Sipsamrang.

Illang shouted at the Turang.

“Your opponents are over here.”

Bisain's eyes met theirs. The gazes of the Sado Sipsamrang looking at him were filled with fervor.

‘We greet the Young Lord!’

Bisain's eyes also burned with fervor. Yes, if they overcame this crisis! He was truly confident that he could do well going forward. Yes, they would win! They would definitely win!

They were not the only reinforcements.

Another cold voice rang out.

“Who is the one who framed me?”

The man with such a frigid atmosphere that he seemed as though he would not bleed a single drop even if stabbed was Beoncheon, the commander of the Geukdo Corps.

Behind Beoncheon, the elite warriors of the Sado Alliance's finest Geukdo Corps revealed themselves simultaneously.

“How dare you attempt to assassinate the Alliance Lord! Not a single one of you will leave here alive!”

The Turang turned to confront the Sado Sipsamrang and the Geukdo Corps. Beoncheon repeated what Tu Wang had said earlier.

“Slaughter all of these bastards and aid the Alliance Lord!”

At the resounding shout, the elites of the Geukdo Corps drew their swords in unison. With the situation turned thus, momentum and morale rose even higher on the Sado Alliance Lord's side.

Right then, Geonmugeuk saw.

Dokpaeja slyly trying to slip his hand into the pouch at his waist.

Geonmugeuk intuited it. That he intended to scatter mass-slaughter poison and eliminate the Geukdo Corps warriors first.

Having been with the Poison King for a long time, he understood the psychology and movements of poison users better than anyone. That was why he had been watching only Dokpaeja, the most dangerous being present.

There was no way all those many warriors of the Geukdo Corps possessed high-grade anti-poison pills. Their mid-grade anti-poison pills would not be able to block Dokpaeja's poison.

“Wait a moment, Elder Dokpaeja!”

Geonmugeuk quickly shouted.

“Before we fight, let me ask just one thing.”

Dokpaeja's hand, which had been heading toward his pouch, paused briefly.

“Did you, our elder, use poison on the Supreme Commander? Was it not a high-level poison that even evaded the eyes of the Sado Alliance's Divine Physician?”

“Yes, it is a poison this old man made.”

“Is there an antidote? The Poison King of our cult once told me: the true essence of poison arts lies not in administering poison, but in curing it.”

Put that way, it was the sort of mood where even an antidote that did not exist would have to exist.

The Poison Tyrant took a small pouch from his robe. Fortunately, he had an antidote.

“Here it is.”

“Give it to me.”

“Why should I?”

“Because if you give it to me now, you’ll die gently. If you don’t, you’ll die in agony.”

The Poison Tyrant let out a derisive sneer.

“Yes, I’ve heard the rumors. They say the reckless young cult leader is causing quite a stir in the Central Plains.”

The Poison Tyrant glanced at the Battle King. It was a look asking whether it would be acceptable to kill the young leader of the Demon Cult here today. The Battle King gave a slight nod. In any case, the young leader of the Demon Cult had to be dealt with as having fought the Evil Dao Alliance Leader until both sides suffered grievous wounds. If they made it seem that the Poison King had helped, everyone would believe it.

The Poison Tyrant held out the pouch.

“If you’re confident you can take it, come and take it yourself.”

At the Poison Tyrant’s provocation, Geom Mugeuk walked toward him.

Knowing well what kind of poison master the Poison Tyrant was, Guard Captain Ingung turned to Baek Jagang with an expression that asked if they should not stop him even now.

Baek Jagang silently watched Geom Mugeuk. He could guess why Geom Mugeuk was facing the Poison Tyrant. To obtain the antidote? It seemed that way, but no.

It was because the Poison Tyrant was the most dangerous one. Because if they were to return safely, the first person who had to be killed was the Poison Tyrant. Because he himself had also been thinking that if he were to kill them, he would have to kill the Poison Tyrant first.

Baek Jagang recalled what Geom Mugeuk had said yesterday.

—The honor I believe in lies in the result of killing those who must be killed.

Can you truly kill the one who must be killed and return safely?

The others, too, watched with tense eyes.

From Bisa-in, who knew Geom Mugeuk well, to Goeak, the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Dao, and even Ingung. What each of them had experienced was different, but all of them had witnessed Geom Mugeuk’s astonishing side. Would that astonishment and uniqueness work against the Poison Tyrant as well?

Geom Mugeuk walked fearlessly right up to the Poison Tyrant.

The Poison Tyrant considered this courage to be recklessness.

‘He must believe I won’t be able to kill him because he’s the young leader of the Demon Cult.’

Otherwise, no one in the martial world could walk so confidently toward him, the Poison Tyrant. No one could look at him with such relaxed eyes.

‘You foolish demonic bastard. Your arrogance will lead you to hell.’

Just as Geom Mugeuk was about to take the pouch in his hand.

Puh-eok!

Poison smoke burst out around the two of them.

“No!”

Bisa-in shouted.

He tried to rush in and save him, but poison smoke had already billowed thickly around Geom Mugeuk. He had never imagined he would be taken so helplessly. If it was you, you should have had some way out, shouldn’t you? He had thought Geom Mugeuk would kill him before he could set it off. Because it was Geom Mugeuk.

It was right then.

Puk! Puk!

Within the poison smoke, sword light flashed, and the sound of flesh being torn rang out in succession.

Hwiiiiing.

A whirlwind rose around the two of them, drawing in the poisons nearby.

Fwoooosh.

Flames caught in the poison smoke and began to burn.

The fire whirled, wrapping around the two of them. Everyone watched this astonishing sight with stunned expressions.

And so, the poison smoke was completely burned away, rising into the sky with the wind before disappearing.

There, Geom Mugeuk and the Poison Tyrant still stood.

The Poison Tyrant was still holding the antidote pouch out in front of him.

When Geom Mugeuk took the pouch, the Poison Tyrant tried to say something. At that very instant!

Puaaaak!

Blood spurted from his neck and heart, and the Poison Tyrant collapsed backward.

Everyone there stared at Geom Mugeuk in shock. To gather the poison and burn it away with scorching yang energy—what a profound technique was that? No, before that, how had he endured the Poison Tyrant’s poison?

Geom Mugeuk took out two poison-warding beads he had been holding in his mouth, making his cheeks bulge like a toad’s. He would have been fine without the poison-warding beads, but he had held them in his mouth because he did not want to reveal in front of everyone that he was impervious to all poisons.

“Did you think you could disregard the highest-grade poison-warding beads used by the young leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult? You gave me the antidote, so as promised, I let you die gently.”

Baek Jagang and the Battle King were half in doubt. From the situation just now, they thought the Poison Tyrant must have used a poison that even the highest-grade poison-warding beads could not resist. In truth, the Poison Tyrant had used an extreme poison that no poison-warding bead could withstand, but there was no way for them to know that.

In any case, the martial artists on the Evil Dao Alliance Leader’s side were able to understand one fact. Before that terrible poison could be unleashed upon them, Geom Mugeuk had saved their lives.

Geom Mugeuk tucked the antidote for the Chief Strategist into his robe and said to them,

“Now then, take out those poison-warding beads you’re holding in your mouths, and let’s fight comfortably.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 369

Chapter 369: How Stubbornly He Must Have Insisted.
--------------------------------------------------
The person most moved at that moment was Bisain.

Had Geom Mugeuk not killed the Poison Tyrant, there was no telling how many warriors of the Sado Alliance would have died.

And above all, he was glad that Geom Mugeuk was safe. To think the day would come when he would be glad the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult was unharmed.

The guard captain, Ingung, spat out the poison-warding bead and looked at Baek Jagang.

‘The Alliance Leader believed the young cult leader would succeed.’

As expected, his eye for people was different from his own, Ingung marveled. In truth, however, Baek Jagang had not been certain either.

He had merely felt an inexplicable sense of expectation. A baseless expectation that this young cult leader, who kept showing him a world he himself did not possess, would not die so pointlessly.

The Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Path also looked at Geom Mugeuk with great gratitude. In the past, during the fight against Seok Gwanchu and Baek Manggi, many of the Thirteen Wolves had been killed or injured, and afterward, in the process of Bisain becoming successor, a generational shift had taken place. Inevitably, their strength had weakened compared to when they had first met Geom Mugeuk. Thus, had the Poison Tyrant’s poison been released, they too would have suffered greatly.

The First Wolf’s gaze turned toward Bisain. There had been a time when he too had disliked Geom Mugeuk and had been wary of Bisain becoming close to him, but now he could say it with certainty.

— I believe the Young Alliance Leader’s choice was right.

The juniors would gradually grow under Bisain, and if they survived today’s battle, the warriors who represented the Sado Alliance would once again be the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Path.

At the First Wolf’s sound transmission, Bisain answered with a glad heart.

— You have no idea how much strength it gives me that you came, First Wolf.

— From now on as well, I will devote this one body entirely to serving you.

The warriors of the Extreme Saber Corps felt the same. In truth, they would have suffered the greatest losses.

On top of that, their commander, Beoncheon, had one more reason to feel goodwill. He had heard from the Alliance Leader that if Geom Mugeuk had not intervened this time, he would have been made to shoulder all the blame. For that reason, his goodwill toward Geom Mugeuk was even greater.

If the warriors of the Sado Alliance were this shocked, how could their enemies not be?

The morale of the Fighting Wolves was completely broken, and the Crescent Moon Saber and the Blue Killing Ghost, who had appeared together with the Poison Tyrant, exchanged glances.

The Poison Tyrant they knew was an old man who had weathered every hardship under heaven.

‘And yet he killed him this easily?’

The Poison Tyrant was someone even they found difficult to deal with. Who here could face him comfortably?

The Crescent Moon Saber and the Blue Killing Ghost naturally looked toward the Fighting King.

The Fighting King showed not the slightest agitation at the Poison Tyrant’s death. Lingering attachment to one already dead would not help in battle in the least.

The Fighting King was calmly studying Geom Mugeuk. His first impression was similar to what the Poison Tyrant had felt. He sensed a boundless composure that seemed to come from the confidence of being the Heavenly Demon’s blood kin.

But now that it had been revealed that his composure was not of that sort, he peered deeply into what lay beneath it.

And then the Fighting King could smell it.

‘The scent of blood.’

In the Fighting King’s mind arose the image of Geom Mugeuk standing alone on a battlefield. Standing alone in a field of battle, a single sword hanging at his side.

He could feel that they were of the same breed. He could tell that youth and playfulness were concealing his true nature.

‘He is someone who must be killed today.’

The Fighting King instinctively knew that today’s battle would not be easy. What would it take to win?

In the meantime, Geom Mugeuk had approached Hyeok Sagun.

Hyeok Sagun was still in shock over the Poison Tyrant’s death. After all, the person he had trusted the most inwardly had been the Poison Tyrant.

“By the way, why are you still here? Shouldn’t you be over on that side?”

“Are you trying to slander me again?”

Hyeok Sagun had sensed that Geom Mugeuk would not leave him be.

He immediately began by needling him. Geom Mugeuk had tattled on him to Beoncheon, the commander of the Extreme Saber Corps.

“The person who framed you was this Deputy Strategist.”

“Do not believe the young cult leader’s words. It is a false accusation.”

Only after speaking did Hyeok Sagun realize it had been an inappropriate answer. He had reported to the Alliance Leader that evidence had emerged of Beoncheon colluding with the Demonic Cult, and yet now he was calling it a false accusation. What he should have said to Beoncheon was this:

Evidence has emerged that you joined hands with the Demonic Cult.

This was the first time one person had made him commit so many verbal mistakes.

‘This is all because of him.’

The reason his long-laid plans were all collapsing and falling apart. Even at this moment, the bastard was shaking him.

“Aren’t you afraid?”

“Of what?”

“You must have come this far with that man’s help. But once that man becomes leader of the Sado Alliance, do you think he’ll let you live?”

“What are you talking about? What absurd false charge are you trying to pin on me now?”

“Let’s say you became the Alliance Leader. Then would you want a strategist who forced his way up with someone else’s help? Or would you want a truly capable Chief Strategist?”

“!”

How could Hyeok Sagun not have thought of this? But he had deliberately buried it deep within his heart. The greater his yearning for success, the deeper the pit in which he buried his worries.

He trusted Hwang Seokgyeong. He would be the Alliance Leader, and Hyeok Sagun would be Chief Strategist. He had dreamed only of a shining future where they stood together by the window of the Alliance Leader’s hall, sharing memories of the past.

And now Geom Mugeuk had dug up the very thing he had buried deep inside his heart. He learned for the first time that the things one believed to have buried the deepest would pop out when prodded like this.

‘Do not drive a wedge between my lord and me!’

Hyeok Sagun nearly said that. It was something he must not say. For the moment, the fact that he was on the same side as Hwang Seokgyeong, who stood over there, could not be discovered.

“Do not try to drive a wedge between the Alliance Leader and me.”

That was the proper answer.

Once again, Geom Mugeuk struck at the heart of the matter.

“You are deceiving yourself far too much just to take the mere twenty or so steps from here to where that man is standing.”

“!”

For an instant, Hyeok Sagun could not answer.

Geom Mugeuk gave him no time to rest, shaking him relentlessly. Geom Mugeuk shouted to the Fighting King.

“If you don’t withdraw, I’ll kill your strategist here.”

But the Fighting King did not retreat a single step.

“See that man’s reaction? And you still say this isn’t being discarded after the hunt?”

Hyeok Sagun was inwardly flustered. He recalled what Geom Mugeuk had said earlier. Would you want a strategist who forced his way up? Or a truly capable strategist?

“Now then, confess clearly what you’ve been hiding as your final move, and come back to this side.”

At that, the Fighting King, who had been watching, stepped forward.

“Do not trouble yourself trying to hide your identity. The Alliance Leader already knows. He is mocking you.”

Hyeok Sagun looked at Baek Jagang in disbelief. If Hwang Seokgyeong said such a thing, Baek Jagang should naturally react to him, but Baek Jagang did not even look at him. With that, he knew.

‘He really did know.’

It was the moment when the true nature of the unease and discord he had felt the entire way here was clearly revealed. He did not even ask why Hyeok Sagun had betrayed him.

Bisain did not spare him a single glance either. Since he had tried to kill him, he should have at least hurled abuse, but instead he ignored him completely.

Beoncheon and the Thirteen Wolves of the Evil Path, following their master’s will, did not look at him at all.

Finally, he looked at Geom Mugeuk. Geom Mugeuk gave him one last remark.

“Look carefully. There may be a great cauldron hanging behind him, waiting to boil you.”

It would have been better not to hear it.

The Fighting King said to Hyeok Sagun,

“Come here.”

Was it all right to go like this? Would someone ambush and kill him?

He worried over such things, but he had no other choice.

Hyeok Sagun slowly walked toward the Fighting King.

He did not feel good. He had intended to reveal his betrayal in a far more dramatic and magnificent fashion.

He had planned to look down at the dying Baek Jagang and say this:

— I betrayed you.

Then the Alliance Leader would sigh like this.

— I cannot believe it. You were the traitor?

It would be the Fighting King who killed the Alliance Leader, but he had harbored the desire to be the final protagonist. He had imagined his final moment with the Alliance Leader countless times.

And yet, with his identity exposed in such a pathetically dull manner, he was walking toward the Fighting King.

He would rather someone had hurled curses at him, but no one paid him any mind.

Hyeok Sagun felt it in his bones: being ignored was more unpleasant than being hated.

The one who completed this worst of endings was the Fighting King.

The Fighting King gave him no meaningful words.

You have worked hard all this time. A new history will begin now. Thanks to you, we came this far.

Hyeok Sagun desperately hoped for just one such word of praise. The only thing that could sweep away this thorough disregard was a single word from the Fighting King.

“Stay behind me.”

But that one sentence was all.

The Fighting King’s gaze turned back to Baek Jagang.

Hyeok Sagun felt it. At this moment, he was nothing more than an existence that would get in the way of the fight.

Even as he understood the Fighting King, he could not help feeling hurt. Without realizing it, Hyeok Sagun clenched his fist and walked behind him. In the cauldron hanging within his heart, water began to boil.

The Fighting King asked Baek Jagang,

“Since when did you know?”

“A few days ago.”

“There should have been no evidence.”

Baek Jagang nodded. He was right. Had Geom Mugeuk not appeared, he would not have thought Hyeok Sagun had betrayed him until the very last moment.

Who would dare imagine that someone had recruited his strategist, intended to make him Chief Strategist, kill the Young Alliance Leader, and even aim for the position of Sado Alliance Leader?

Baek Jagang’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

“He insisted stubbornly. So stubbornly that I believed the words of the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult over those of our own Deputy Strategist.”

He had called it stubborn, but in truth, the word should have been charming. The moments he spent with Geom Mugeuk had been fresh and amusing. That was why, in the end, they had managed to come this far.

At this moment, how could Geom Mugeuk possibly miss the chance to be glib?

“Ah! There truly was an effort that brought blood to the eyes. Even the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult is working this hard, so what are you doing here instead of accepting the new recruits you’re supposed to take in? Did you get tired of giving others a fresh start and decide to make one yourself?”

The Fighting King sensed that this was the sort of thing one was deceived by. His opponent was looking at him from the battlefield while covered in blood. Because the Poison Tyrant had failed to see that, he had died.

Into the heavy silence, Goeak walked forward.

“It seems the conversation to be shared by mouth has been had to some extent.”

Goeak pointed to one person.

“You. Let us have a look at each other.”

His opponent was none other than the Crescent Moon Saber, one of the Seven Great Masters of the Evil Path.

“If we have to fight anyway, fighting first would be more dignified in many ways.”

It meant that as time passed, the battle would descend into a melee. The Crescent Moon Saber accepted the proposal to fight now rather than then.

“You are right.”

As the two men stepped forward, the warriors who had been facing each other also withdrew, naturally creating space for the two to fight.

Because it was a fight between the Seven Great Masters of the Evil Path, everyone held their breath.

Bisain was worried about Goeak. He had come here today for his sake while his body had still not fully recovered.

— Senior, please be careful.

At Bisain’s concern, Goeak, with his back turned, nodded once.

The Crescent Moon Saber drew a blade curved like a crescent moon. Emitting a sharp killing edge, he launched the first attack.

The crescent moon, beautiful when floating in the sky, became savage when it descended to earth.

Shik, shik, shik, shik, shiiiik!

In an instant, dozens of lines of saber light were drawn, and Goeak found a path between the lines that divided life from death. Since he had mastered the body art, the Iron Man of Severing Strikes, he had to close the distance.

Because they knew each other’s abilities well, the fight became fierce in an instant. Saber qi flew in, and the astral qi that shot from Goeak’s entire body swept through the surroundings.

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwang!

Even watching was perilous. They fought without the slightest regard for those around them.

Those watching released sword qi to block the saber qi that spilled backward, and at times they threw themselves aside to dodge.

Swaaaaaaak!

Crescent-shaped saber qi was released in succession. It was a powerful attack that would make bone and flesh vanish without a trace from the slightest graze.

This time, screams erupted here and there. A Fighting Wolf who failed to dodge in time was swept away by the saber qi and sent flying.

Those watching retreated. Those with relatively weaker martial arts moved even farther away.

Amid that chaos, Baek Jagang and the Fighting King were facing each other. They did not watch the fight. They stared only into each other’s eyes. Saber qi brushed past the side of their faces, but neither moved a single step. They were still, as though standing in a different space.

From the moment Baek Jagang learned that his opponent had brought the Poison Tyrant, he no longer regarded him as a warrior, but as an assassin. If he was someone for whom only victory mattered, then Baek Jagang intended to deal with him accordingly. His opponent was someone who could leap into this fight at any time and launch a joint attack.

‘Try moving once.’

Because Baek Jagang was restraining him so powerfully, Geom Mugeuk could watch the fight between the two with an easy heart.

He placed himself within their battle. He became the Crescent Moon Saber, and he became Goeak.

The martial art the Crescent Moon Saber had mastered was saber technique while his own was sword technique, and Goeak’s was body art while his own was fist technique, but Geom Mugeuk perfectly overlaid his own martial arts onto their movements. If it were me, like this. If I had been there, like that.

For Geom Mugeuk, experiencing such a thing while watching a life-and-death battle between the Seven Great Masters of the Evil Path was of great help.

A single instant’s mistake would lead to defeat. They were truly engaged in a contest so close that the loser would feel wronged.

The only difference was the reason the two had entered the fight.

And it seemed Goeak’s reason was more special, and stronger.

Piercing through the flashing saber light, Goeak’s elbow struck the Crescent Moon Saber’s shoulder.

A momentary stagger. That was the end. If he were someone who missed such an opportunity, Goeak would never have risen to the position of one of the Seven Great Masters of the Evil Path.

Puh-eok! Kwajik.

Goeak slammed the Crescent Moon Saber’s chest with his own back and shoulder. The instant the Crescent Moon Saber was pushed back and vomited blood, Goeak had already sealed his paralysis point and wrapped an arm around his neck from behind.

“Do you have any last words?”

“I feel filthy.”

“Isn’t living filthily and dying filthily the way of us on the Evil Path?”

Kwaduk!

The Crescent Moon Saber’s neck bone snapped at a right angle, and he died instantly.

Goeak slowly turned and walked back. Everyone watched him in silence.

Because he had overexerted his internal energy, blood was flowing down from the corner of Goeak’s mouth.

Bisain ran out to meet him.

“Are you all right?”

Goeak nodded with a pale face.

“A fight like this was not bad either.”

It meant that he had fought only for himself all his life, and for the first time, he had fought for someone else.

“I will not forget this moment.”

At least in this moment, Bisain was Geom Mugeuk, and Goeak was Geugak Soma.

Absolute Regression, Chapter 370

Chapter 370: Raise Your Hand If You Think You're Ugly!
------------------------------------------------------
“Begin by circulating your qi.”

Bisa-in was worried about Goeak. His complexion was so pale that he looked as if he might collapse at any moment.

“Don’t worry. I’ve come through worse situations than this without incident.”

Before sitting down, Goeak looked at Sinwoldo’s corpse.

Overlapping his corpse, Goeak saw the corpse of Gwangseom. He also saw the figure of the Heavenly Water Sword, one of the Flame-Scorching Twin Swords. Who would have thought the day would come when he would kill the Seven Great Masters of the Unorthodox Path with his own hands?

When one’s life changes drastically, it is like an assassin’s dagger. It never gives warning.

Even while carrying out various tasks for the Unorthodox Alliance, Goeak had always kept one step back. He had been ready to pull out at the first sign of trouble. Yet that had been him.

‘I’ve come too far in.’

But as he had told Bisa-in earlier, he did not dislike the feeling. It was certainly awkward to be doing something for another person, though.

Goeak sent a voice transmission to one person.

—Is this what it normally feels like?

The person he addressed was Geom Mugeuk. He had asked the question abruptly, without any context, but Geom Mugeuk answered without hesitation.

—It starts from here.

Had he answered that way because he knew what Goeak was asking?

—Then what happens later?

—You’ll regret it. Why did I get so deeply involved and put myself through all this trouble? Perhaps you might even die because of those feelings that don’t suit you.

Geom Mugeuk knew exactly what question he had been asked and answered accordingly.

—But it will be different when you die.

—How?
