There was no one among the Twelve Zodiac Kings who used such martial arts. Unless perhaps the Phantom King were alive.

‘Then who in the world was it?’

In any case, he could know one thing for certain. The force behind this incident had clearly been watching the Fighting King. Perhaps that was only natural. Their attempts against the main cult, the Martial Alliance, and even the Feng Tian Sect had all failed before this.

‘It is someone who knows the Fighting King’s personality. They watched until the very end and rescued him at the final moment.’

He could not tell who it was. At this point in time, he had no way of knowing what circumstances they were in.

Since the Fighting King had escaped in the middle of the fight, one might have expected him to be flustered and left with an unpleasant feeling, but Baek Jagang showed none of that. His face displayed a confidence that said, If he appears again, I will simply kill him then.

“He bears deep resentment toward you, so be careful.”

“It seems he was fated to die by my hand.”

At that moment, the Poison King sent a voice transmission to Geom Mugeuk.

—That will not happen.

—Pardon?

—I do not know where the other side of that hole that swallowed him leads, but he would have been better off dying here.

Geom Mugeuk understood. The Poison King had already poisoned him. And with a dreadful, terrifying poison at that.

—I thought he would die to the Sado Alliance Leader before the poison spread.

It seemed the Poison King truly could not forgive the Fighting King. He had given a symbolic punishment to the man who had used poison by putting children forward, but in the end, it had become a real punishment.

If the Poison King said he would die, then he would surely die. Geom Mugeuk believed in the Poison King’s skill. He also believed in the karmic retribution for the crime of using a poison that should not have been used, in a way it should not have been used.

—As expected! Our Poison King is a man of foresight.

At that moment, someone shouted from the side.

“Come at me! I’ll kill at least one of you before I die!”

With his master gone, the flustered Sahon swung his sword in a final desperate struggle.

It was right then.

Someone approached silently from behind him and wrapped around his neck.

Crack.

Before he could resist, Sahon’s neck snapped.

The person who revealed himself behind Sahon as he collapsed like a bundle of straw was Goeak. After recovering from his injuries through circulating his qi and regulating his breath at the place where the fight had first broken out, he had arrived belatedly.

“Are you all right?”

At Bi Sain’s concern, Goeak nodded.

“I am fine.”

“That is a relief.”

Bi Sain looked at him with sincere feeling. From now on, when it came to human relationships, he would no longer glance over things carelessly or make rough assumptions as he had before.

Bi Sain had desperately wanted this moment. He had resolved that if he escaped the crisis, he would build all of his relationships better.

When the moment came to put it into practice, he would not make the mistake of losing to annoyance or laziness. From now on, I am Geom Mugeuk. I will follow even half of his effort.

Left alone, Hyeok Sagun’s only way to escape the pain was to bite off his tongue and kill himself, but he could not bring himself to do it. He believed the Fist King would come to save him. No, he had a feeling he would not come, but dying was far too frightening.

Bi Sain said to him coldly.

“You’ll have to pay the price for dreaming an empty dream.”

Hyeok Sagun was dragged away by the warriors of the Extreme Blade Corps. The children were also transported to the Alliance.

And so the long battle with the Fist King came to an end.

Baek Jagang said to Geom Mugeuk.

“First, let us return to the Alliance together. Even if we part ways afterward, we should at least share a farewell drink.”

He could not simply send away Geom Mugeuk, who had saved them. Geom Mugeuk readily followed along as well.

“Yes, let’s go. Please serve me the finest wine in the Sado Alliance.”

When Baek Jagang stretched out his hand, the gauntlet Fighting God, which had fallen to the ground, flew into his grasp.

Baek Jagang handed Fighting God to Geom Mugeuk.

“This is about the only thing worth using. You take it.”

He said it as though it were nothing, but Fighting God was a gauntlet that stood at the very pinnacle of all gauntlets. It was a treasure so precious that its value could not even be measured, truly an enormous gift.

He did not feel it was a waste at all. If not for Geom Mugeuk, it was terrifying to imagine how things might have ended.

“Why are you giving this to me?”

“You came a long way, so sell it and use it for travel expenses. Why? You don’t want it?”

“As if that could be true!”

Geom Mugeuk quickly tucked Fighting God away.

“I’ll ride back in a golden carriage.”

Baek Jagang then spoke to the Poison King.

“Demon Venerable, you should come to the Alliance with us as well.”

“I have matters to attend to, so I must take my leave here.”

The Poison King bid farewell to Baek Jagang.

Baek Jagang did not insist any further. The Poison King and the poison men entering the Sado Alliance was not something he wished for either.

Geom Mugeuk sent a sound transmission to the Poison King.

—Please go to the safe house! I’ll come see you soon. Don’t leave first; let’s go back together!

He could wait at the safe house where Jo Chunbae’s family was resting, and then they could return together.

—Let’s dig up some poisonous herbs on the way back.

—Why didn’t you bring your personal gatherer? I mean that precious person for whom you even prepare separate meals you don’t prepare for the Young Cult Leader.

—That person is busy these days.

The Poison King led his subordinates away from that place. Watching his back, Geom Mugeuk wanted to follow him.

Please wait just a little. I’ll finish things here and come soon.

After parting with the Poison King like that, Geom Mugeuk headed toward the Sado Alliance with Baek Jagang.

As they walked together, Baek Jagang asked as though something had suddenly occurred to him.

“You said that when this matter was over, you had a favor to ask of me, didn’t you?”

Baek Jagang was curious what that favor could possibly be. Bi Sain, walking with them, was equally curious. Geom Mugeuk had said he had a favor to ask of him as well.

“Do you want a martial arts manual or treasure stored in our Alliance’s vault? Or do you want a spiritual medicine?”

“No, that’s all right. What I want is for you, Alliance Leader, to go somewhere with me.”

“Where?”

Then, from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth came words Baek Jagang had never imagined.

“I’d like to treat you to a drink at my regular tavern in front of our cult.”

Chapter 375: No Looking Down on Me Just Because the Mystique Is Gone
--------------------------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk was seeing it for the first time.

The sight of Baek Jagang's eyes widening. Of course, even widened, they were still small.

'Is that the best you can do, Alliance Lord?'

Whether it was Bi Sain or the Sado Alliance Lord. They were people whose outstanding individual personalities felt remarkably cool. Because those small eyes only grew more charming the more one looked at them.

"Did you say that regular tavern is in front of the Divine Cult?"

"It is in Magachon."

"You wish to take me there?"

"Yes."

Baek Jagang, who had been staring at Geom Mugeuk for a moment, looked at Bi Sain.

Before the Alliance Lord could say anything, Bi Sain sighed and spoke.

"He's serious. He was already this kind of crazy person."

Still, Bi Sain had used the word "person" instead of "bastard."

Baek Jagang asked again.

"Was this what you wanted to ask from the beginning?"

"Yes."

"Perhaps was this your goal? To lure me to the Divine Cult and kill me?"

Geom Mugeuk smiled. In Baek Jagang's mind, he was already surrounded by the Palma Jon at the tavern in front of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, being attacked by a combined assault.

"No. I have absolutely no intention of harming the Alliance Lord."

A shiver did not run down Baek Jagang's neck.

Yes, if that had been the purpose, the Poison King would not have detoxified Hwangcheon earlier.

"What is the reason for taking me all the way to the Divine Cult?"

"Did I not tell you? Because I wish to treat you to a drink there."

"Could you not simply buy the drink here?"

"The owner's cooking there is very good. The tavern atmosphere is good as well."

"Speak honestly. What is this for?"

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk answered.

"Because I wish for you to meet my father."

No matter how unofficial the matter, if the Sado Alliance Lord has come all the way to the main cult's entrance, would the Heavenly Demon simply remain idle? His father would undoubtedly come out to the tavern to meet the Sado Alliance Lord.

"With your father? Why?"

"There are two reasons. First, because of this recent conspiracy. They targeted the Sado Alliance's Young Alliance Lord and the Martial Alliance Lord's grandson, as well as our cult's Soul-Devouring Demon Lord. With such individuals active in the shadows, I believe it would be meaningful for the two of you to meet."

Baek Jagang nodded. It was a valid point, but there was no need to meet in person. They could exchange letters or share intelligence at a military officers' level. He had a feeling that the reason Geom Mugeuk wished to take him there was the second reason.

"The second reason?"

Then came Geom Mugeuk's true feelings.

"I hope that when the two of you meet, you become close. I somehow feel you would get along well."

Because he wished for his father and the Sado Alliance Lord to move away from the dream of Murim unification. Countless lives hung upon a single toast between the two.

Baek Jagang looked at Geom Mugeuk with an expression that said he simply could not understand.

"You say that reason is better than a Murim secret treasure or a thousand-year spirit medicine?"

"Yes, it is better. Of course, I know all too well that this is not a matter to be decided lightly."

He had brought it up lightly, but it was by no means a light matter. It was a matter of the Sado Alliance Lord moving to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. Just considering the escort alone—should he bring only a small number of experts? Or a large-scale force?

"Are you saying this with your father's permission?"

"To be honest, I have not yet received it. However, this is not something that requires permission. It is a matter of me personally treating the Alliance Lord to a drink."

If this matter were handled officially, his father would not permit it. He disliked the Sado Alliance and the Sado Alliance Lord alike. Though the same could surely be said of the Martial Alliance Lord.

In any case, even without permission, he could make the meeting happen.

Baek Jagang began to walk again.

Walking briefly lost in thought, he stopped.

"Very well. Let us suppose I go. Would the Martial Alliance simply stand by? They would think the Divine Cult and the main alliance are conspiring together."

"I will take responsibility and ensure there is no misunderstanding."

"How?"

"I will go to the Martial Alliance Lord and tell him directly. That I arranged this meeting for such and such reasons. Please do not misunderstand."

Baek Jagang knew that Geom Mugeuk was speaking from the heart.

'Perhaps if it's this fellow?'

He thought that perhaps Geom Mugeuk could calm the Martial Alliance.

Before he knew it, he was thinking such thoughts. He had been a man who prided himself on scoffing at the idea of being captivated by a young fellow.

Just then, the carriage for the Alliance Lord arrived.

"I shall see you at the alliance later."

As if he had things to ponder alone, Baek Jagang left first with his escorts without taking the two. The martial artists of the Geukdo Legion escorted the children and followed, and Gwiaekdo left the place promising to meet again.

Now only Geom Mugeuk, Bi Sain, and the Sado Thirteen Wolves remained.

The two walked slowly, and the Sado Thirteen Wolves scattered far in all directions, escorting the two as they walked.

To Bi Sain, the events of fighting the Tu Wang felt like a dream. But as if telling him not to even think of that past villain, Geom Mugeuk threw a new problem at him.

Since he had not known Geom Mugeuk for merely a day or two, it was not strange at all that he had made such a request to the Alliance Lord. However, there was one thing that worried him.

"What are you going to do if the two fight when they meet? No one would be able to stop them. Are you not afraid?"

Instead of answering, Geom Mugeuk asked,

"Do you trust your master?"

"Of course I trust him."

"I trust my father as well. Therefore, there will be no fight."

He was saying to trust the adults. But were they ordinary adults?

Even if a fight broke out, it would be fine. Rather, Geom Mugeuk even wished that the two would exchange blows. Because the bond that formed between martial artists through fighting could not be ignored.

As Bi Sain nodded silently, Geom Mugeuk asked with suspicious eyes,

"Why are you accepting my words so readily? It's unsettling."

"It is my period of acceptance."

"Is there such a period?"

"There is. And it is all something I learned from you. That it is not knowing nothing which harms one's pride, but not making the effort to correct it."

Bi Sain had changed greatly, as could be seen from how readily he admitted this. Perhaps because he was still young, Bi Sain had a stronger desire for learning and change than anyone Geom Mugeuk had ever met.

As if he had just thought of something, Bi Sain asked again,

"By the way, what was it you wanted to ask of me? Were you going to ask me to come to the tavern as well?"

"No. If the Alliance Lord permits it, you will follow along anyway. Why would I bother asking?"

Geom Mugeuk's request was as unexpected as the one he had made to Baek Jagang.

"Please let me take just one thing I want from the Sado Alliance treasury."

Bi Sain opened his eyes wide and stopped walking.

"Why so surprised? For saving your life, is it not natural to request at least that much?"

"Of course."

"Were you disappointed? That I would show such greed? That I should have made a request to deepen our friendship instead?"

"That is not it."

"Rather than nursing lingering regrets, I intend to become an incarnation of greed for a brief moment right now."

Bi Sain walked in silence for a moment. Then he stopped and revealed his true feelings.

"Honestly, rather than disappointed, I was bewildered. I thought you would ask for something else. If you were me, how would you react in this situation?"

Geom Mugeuk smiled subtly, thought for a moment, then gave his answer.

"I would say this: Very well, I shall give you a gift from the secret vault. Is there anything else you want besides that?"

That he would ask what more he wanted?

"If you answered that you wanted more treasure?"

"Very well, I shall give you that as well. Is there anything else you want? I would keep asking like this. I would keep asking until the answer I want comes out. If you have a conscience, you will speak. Until then, I would give you even ten treasures."

"Is there a reason you must do so?"

"Because we are friends."

Bi Sain's eyes narrowed as he asked suspiciously,

"Are you aiming for ten treasures of the main alliance right now?"

"You noticed?"

Bi Sain's rough face, which had formed a dumbfounded expression, relaxed slightly.

"Did I not tell you? I am not such a great person. I am greedy and look after my own interests first. Since I am that kind of person, do not hold any preconceptions or illusions."

Bi Sain could feel it. It was as if Geom Mugeuk was saying this to him:

"Shatter the illusions between people, friend. Only then will it last."

In fact, Bi Sain could not know, but there was a separate reason Geom Mugeuk wanted to enter the treasury.

It was because of the Bigwe.

Because of the hope that perhaps there might be an artifact in the Sado Alliance treasury that the Bigwe could absorb. Because if not for this opportunity, there would never be a chance to enter the Sado Alliance treasury.

"You are truly a man one can never figure out."

"You will know in just a little while. That I am a person easier to understand than you. Do not ignore me then just because the sense of mystery has faded."

A brief gust of wind blew, not cold but warm.

"The weather has warmed greatly. At this rate, spring will arrive before we know it."

As he spoke, Geom Mugeuk looked up at the sky. Bi Sain looked up at the sky as well, following Geom Mugeuk. Even though he tried to look often, he kept forgetting to look up.

"What kind of place is your regular tavern?"

"A place with good liquor, a good owner, and good patrons. You would like it as well. Perhaps you might even come to drink there every day."

"Is it not too far to go for a drink?"

"Train your lightness skill diligently."

Not only the two of them, but also the Sado Thirteen Wolves who accompanied them stood there looking up at the sky for a long while.

* * *

After returning to the Sado Alliance, the two went straight to the Alliance Lord Pavilion.

"I shall go receive the Alliance Lord's permission, so wait here."

No matter how much he was the Young Alliance Lord, he could not freely enter the Sado Alliance treasury.

"Please get permission even if you have to force it."

Bi Sain answered as he walked toward the Alliance Lord Pavilion.

"At times like this, one should say not to overdo it."

"I want to go in so badly! I want to obtain it so badly! Please! Put all your friendship to use!"

Bi Sain, who had been walking, pretended to cover his ears.

After sending Bi Sain off, Geom Mugeuk looked at the steel wolf erected in the corridor. It looked exactly like the blue wolf that had appeared when Baek Jagang displayed his martial arts.

As he was looking at it briefly, someone revealed themselves by undoing their concealment.

"You are looking at the wolf again."

He was none other than Ingung, captain of the Sado Alliance guard.

"I am strangely drawn to this fellow."

To the extent that he wanted to say the thing he desired was this wolf statue. Of course, that was impossible.

"I am grateful for this time."

Ingung expressed his gratitude to Geom Mugeuk. He had revealed himself to offer this greeting.

"Not at all. You must have had a hard time, Captain."

Though it had not shown outwardly, they had fought the most arduous battle this time. Because they had been prepared to throw themselves in while concealed if the Alliance Lord fell into danger. The mental exhaustion had been enormous.

Therefore, those who felt the greatest gratitude toward Geom Mugeuk and the Poison King were Ingung and the guards. Because they were ones who existed solely to save the Alliance Lord.

Just then, at the far end of the corridor, the door to the Alliance Lord Pavilion opened and Bi Sain walked out. He had come out faster than expected. Either permission had been granted readily, or he had been refused at once—it had to be one of the two.

"Then I shall take my leave."

Ingung concealed himself silently.

Bi Sain walked up to Geom Mugeuk and spoke confidently.

"Let us go. Permission granted."

"As expected!"

"If you say even one word, I will cancel it."

Geom Mugeuk covered his mouth with his hand and laughed.

The two left the Alliance Lord Pavilion. Walking side by side through the inner courtyard, Geom Mugeuk asked cautiously,

"Were you not scolded?"

"I was scolded."

"Give me a blindfold. I shall cover my eyes tight and go."

"What would you do if I pushed you into a trap like that?"

"You would push?"

"If I push, it would be off a cliff. A cliff about ten thousand zhang high."

The two arrived at the treasury.

Along the way, they passed through several heavily guarded gates. Only after passing hidden experts, perilous mechanisms, and mysterious formations could they finally reach the treasury.

He had not blindfolded him along the way. Bi Sain knew well that for an expert of Geom Mugeuk's level, covering his eyes was meaningless.

"I am not trying to boast, but even I have never freely chosen from here before."

“I truly am grateful. I know I was given this opportunity because you held me in high regard. I doubt I’ll ever forget this gratitude for as long as I live. I probably won’t be able to sleep for a while. If I have children someday, I’ll be sure to tell them. You know your father’s friend, Alliance Leader Bi? When he was young, you see……”

“Enough! I’m sorry. I did show off a little.”

With an expression that seemed to say, Good grief, I should never have opened my mouth, Bi Sain stepped inside first.

All manner of treasures were there.

Peerless secret manuals that would send bloody storms sweeping through the martial world, famed swords whose names anyone would know, protective armor close to immortal in quality. It was truly filled with dazzling treasures.

The two walked together, slowly looking over the items on display.

“Once you become Alliance Leader, all of this will be yours, won’t it? I suppose I should try harder to stay in your good graces from now on.”

“If someone heard you, they’d think the Divine Cult had no treasury of its own. You must have an even greater one. If I asked you to trade it for ours, would you or wouldn’t you?”

“……I wouldn’t.”

“And yet you make such a fuss!”

As he looked around, Geom Mugeuk felt a hot, sweltering heat.

‘This place is preparing for war too.’

It was the same as the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. Weapons, protective armor, and elixirs to be distributed to martial artists once war broke out were neatly arranged. The Sado Alliance was clearly preparing for that day as well.

“The Alliance Leader must have been truly grateful to you. He is not someone who would ever allow an outsider into this place.”

“It must be because of you.”

“Why because of me?”

“Because he wants to keep the promise he made with me.”

Was that truly the reason?

Bi Sain could not be certain. He still did not fully understand the Alliance Leader’s heart. Even so, at the very least, he wished that was the reason. And Geom Mugeuk was the only person who understood that wish. He no longer doubted him. He knew such words were Geom Mugeuk’s consideration.

Geom Mugeuk passed by the secret manuals, famed swords, protective armor, and elixirs without pause.

Geom Mugeuk did not focus on his own greed. He focused on Bigwe’s desire.

‘Bigwe, this is our one and only chance, so hurry and choose.’

But even as they passed all sorts of rare and precious objects, Bigwe showed no response.

“Is there anywhere else?”

“There’s an area where we keep things less valuable than these.”

“Let’s see that too.”

“Are you looking for something in particular?”

“I’m looking for something that strongly draws my heart. It isn’t here.”

And so they looked around the other area as well. Compared to the treasury they had been shown first, the items were indeed less precious.

“If it were me, I would have only shown you this place.”

Joking like that, Geom Mugeuk calmly looked through the second area as well. Here, there were more ordinary jewels and ornaments than weapons, treasure garments, or elixirs.

The farther they went into the corners, the less valuable the stored items became. In that way, the two of them walked almost all the way to the end.

Just as he was about to give up, thinking the Sado Alliance likely did not have it—

Wooong!

Bigwe reacted.

Chapter 376: The Banquet Is Canceled.
-------------------------------------
It was a small lump of iron.

It was not round like the black crystal of the Pungcheon Cult that Bigwe had first absorbed. This one was a fang-shaped ornament that looked as though it might have fallen from some iron beast. It was too heavy to wear around one’s neck, so it could only be set up and used as decoration.

“I’ll take this.”

Bisain looked at Geom Mugeuk in surprise.

“Are you serious?”

“I am.”

“May I have a look?”

“As much as you like.”

Bisain examined the ornament closely. No matter how he looked at it, it was just a fang made of iron. But there was no way Geom Mugeuk had chosen it at random.

“Why do you want this?”

“I was simply drawn to it.”

“You truly are impossible to understand.”

It was not as though this had happened only once or twice since meeting Geom Mugeuk, but this was the most incomprehensible case yet.

“Did I not receive the Sun-Moon Sword from you, and the Fighting God from the Alliance Leader? This is enough for me.”

“And yet you worry your mystique will disappear. The question of why you chose this alone will last me a lifetime.”

Geom Mugeuk and Bisain left the treasury together.

“I truly never dreamed I would go through such things with you.”

And was it only Bisain? Even Geom Mugeuk himself had never imagined he would live this kind of life after regressing. Whenever he had grown weary wandering the Central Plains alone in search of materials for the great art, he had soothed himself by thinking of the life he would have after his regression. How could he have imagined that he would walk side by side out of the Sado Alliance’s treasury with the Young Alliance Leader of the Sado Alliance?

“There will be many more things we never expected. Let us live our whole lives being surprised like this.”

Though younger than him, Geom Mugeuk was someone Bisain could always rely on.

There was one thing he wanted to ask such a Geom Mugeuk.

“Are you not afraid?”

“Of what?”

“Those who are plotting such schemes against us. We do not know what else they might do while hiding in the darkness, do we?”

Since Bisain believed that the King of Fighters had escaped alive, perhaps this fear was only natural.

Now that the frantic battle had ended and he had caught his breath, Bisain realized just how perilous this whole affair had been. If not for Geom Mugeuk, he would have died several times over by now.

Young Cult Leader, just what kind of thoughts do you have?

Geom Mugeuk answered calmly.

“What is there to fear? I am sure I am living more diligently than they are. If I still fall victim after doing the very best I can, then there is nothing to be done about it, is there? I should gladly die.”

“!”

They were words that would become a milestone for Bisain’s life from this day forward.

Even as he gave him such grateful words, Geom Mugeuk instead expressed his own thanks.

“Thank you for keeping your promise.”

When he thought of all the gratitude that had filled the basket of accounts, this moment only made him feel terribly sorry and ashamed.

Bisain strode ahead and said,

“Just do not be late for the evening banquet.”

* * *

Geom Mugeuk returned to the guest hall provided by the Sado Alliance.

After entering the room, he locked the door and closed the windows as well.

He placed Bigwe and the iron fang he had brought from the treasury on the table. Since he had already experienced it absorbing the black crystal once, he would not be surprised by what was about to happen. In any case, it seemed you absorbed things even when they were shaped differently.

“You were hungry, weren’t you? Now, eat up!”

But Bigwe did not move at all.

“Eat. Why aren’t you eating?”

It had definitely vibrated, but Bigwe did not absorb the iron ornament.

“Did your face change to the other side while I wasn’t looking?”

He changed its direction and placed the fang ornament on another side, but it still did not absorb it.

“What is this? Was it not this one? Was it the one beside it?”

Of course, that could not be. It had clearly reacted to this.

He tried this and that, but nothing worked.

In the end, Geom Mugeuk gave up.

“You must be full.”

Bigwe merely wore the bright smile he had drawn on it.

* * *

That evening, guided by In-gung, Geom Mugeuk walked down the corridor leading to the Alliance Leader Hall.

When In-gung naturally stopped in one spot and looked back, sure enough, Geom Mugeuk had come to a halt before the steel wolf.

“After today, you will not be able to see it again, so please take a moment to say farewell. I will be waiting at the Alliance Leader Hall.”

He felt sorry that he had not given Geom Mugeuk proper time, despite how much he liked that wolf statue. Of course, it was also consideration born of goodwill for his having saved the Alliance Leader.

“Thank you. I will come soon.”

Once In-gung disappeared, Geom Mugeuk bade the wolf farewell.

“I won’t be able to see you anymore either.”

He did not know why this wolf kept drawing his steps.

Was it because he felt that his past life before regression resembled this lonely wolf? Perhaps it was.

“I suppose I will have no reason to see you again. Be well.”

After stroking the wolf’s head, Geom Mugeuk was about to turn away when he flinched and stopped. A thought had suddenly struck him like lightning.

Geom Mugeuk took the fang ornament from his robes.

“Surely not? No, it can’t be.”

Geom Mugeuk placed the fang into the steel wolf’s open mouth.

Even when he fit it into the place where a fang should go, nothing changed. The other side was the same.

“I knew it.”

He was about to leave, but since he had already tried, he placed it among the upper teeth as well.

At that very moment, blue radiance burst from the wolf’s two eyes.

Swaaaaaaak!

In that instant, it felt as though the wolf had come to life.

Geom Mugeuk drew up his inner force and retreated. Fortunately, the wolf did not attack him.

Instead, with a clank, something rolled out from inside the wolf’s mouth.

When Geom Mugeuk saw what it was, he was startled.

What had come out of its mouth was a bead tinged with blue light. If the one absorbed earlier had been a black crystal, then this was a blue crystal. Even its size was exactly the same.

This fang had been the key to retrieving the bead hidden inside the steel wolf’s body. Only now did he understand why he had been so drawn to this wolf statue. It had not been him, but Bigwe that had led him here. Or else the energy of the black crystal it had absorbed earlier had drawn him.

Wooooong.

Bigwe, inside his robes, vibrated in response.

He took out Bigwe and placed the blue crystal on top of it.

Sssrk.

As if it had been waiting, Bigwe began to absorb the blue crystal. The hard bead seeped into Bigwe like liquid.

After absorbing all of the blue crystal, Bigwe vibrated once.

Sssssss!

Something tinged with blue energy began to emerge from Bigwe.

It was the same as with the black crystal. It was not smoke, not liquid, not inner force, and not light. That indescribable something passed through Geom Mugeuk’s hand and arm, then spread throughout his entire body.

This time as well, the Heavenly Demon Protection Art did not activate. Rather, a refreshing sensation spread through his body. Geom Mugeuk’s instincts were certain of this: whatever this energy was, it would certainly not harm him.

The moment the energy spread from head to toe, radiance burst from Geom Mugeuk’s body. It was a light so intense he could not open his eyes.

When he opened his eyes again after having closed them, that blue energy had already been completely absorbed into his body. Through Bigwe, he had absorbed a second energy.

Even so, there was no change in his body. The two energies did not meet and mingle either. They merely lay quietly asleep, each deep within his body.

He had the feeling that this was because he had not yet absorbed all the energies needed to bring about some change.

Bigwe spat out the blue crystal after absorbing all its energy. It had now become an ordinary stone. After putting Bigwe, the stone, and even the fang he had inserted into the wolf statue away in his robes, he thanked the wolf statue this time.

“At the very least, I will not let what you held within you be used in vain. Thank you, Blue Wolf.”

The wolf stood proudly as usual, as though it had never emitted blue energy from its eyes.

Geom Mugeuk slowly moved his steps toward the Alliance Leader Hall.

* * *

Seated in the grand chair, Baek Jagang was thinking of the Heavenly Demon, Geom Ujin. That gaze, as though looking down on him, was still vivid in his mind. He had met him twice, and both times, the gaze had been the same.

Would a third time be any different?

In the past, he would have refused flatly. The reason he was hesitating like this was not because of the current conspiracy. Nor was it because he wished to grow close to Geom Ujin. It was because of Geom Mugeuk.

“He raised a son like that? That Geom Ujin?”

He could not believe it. He wanted to meet him in person and confirm it. That such a son had been born and raised like a mutation, not because he had been influenced by Geom Ujin. If Geom Ujin had truly raised him that way? That would genuinely infuriate him.

“What do you think?”

Bisain, who stood below the grand chair, answered calmly.

“I would like you to go.”

“Your reason?”

“Because I trust you, Alliance Leader.”

“Because you trust me?”

“Because you are the only person in this world I trust.”

Baek Jagang looked down at his disciple with an unexpected expression. He had never been one to say such things, but he had changed.

“After meeting the Young Cult Leader, I have thought often that I must change.”

“Because you can only win if you change?”

He thought that would naturally be the case, but Bisain held a different thought.

“No, I do not intend to win.”

“Not that you lack the confidence to win?”

“I have changed the dream itself. Not a dream of winning, but a dream of walking together. Perhaps, for me, that will be an even more difficult battle than one of victory or defeat.”

Just as it was harder for a martial artist not to draw his sword than to draw it.

“It will be very difficult beside someone that exceptional. I will be compared to him, my pride will be wounded, and I will feel plenty of self-loathing. But I still want to go. Rather than being satisfied as the head of a snake, I want to entwine myself with a dragon and dream of ascending to the heavens. Even if I fall and die in the process, I will have seen the sky before I die, so I will have no regrets.”

Bisain said all he wished to say without shrinking back at all. This was also the first time he had spoken so much before the Alliance Leader.

Baek Jagang was inwardly satisfied.

His disciple’s words were right. Right now, whether he won or lost was not important. What mattered was momentum.

Rather than boasting that he would definitely win just to curry favor with him, it was better to see him express his own convictions like this.

He had lived his whole life without children. Because of that, there were times in his old age when he occasionally felt regret.

But there stands a child.

That was how he felt. His disciple, like a son to him, was saying that he would walk a path Baek Jagang had never taken. Yes, no matter how large a snake became, it was still only a snake.

At that moment, news came that Geom Mugeuk had arrived, and soon after, the doors of the Alliance Leader Hall opened and Geom Mugeuk entered.

When he arrived before the grand chair, Baek Jagang asked first,

“Did you rest well?”

“Thanks to you, I rested comfortably.”

He must have heard what Geom Mugeuk had chosen from the treasury, but he did not ask about it.

Once he had given permission, that was the end of it. That heart which did not look back resembled his father’s a great deal. That was why he hoped the two might get along well.

Instead, he asked something else.

“What kind of person do you think I am?”

At the sudden question, silence flowed for a moment. Bisain was also curious what answer Geom Mugeuk would give.

After being lost in thought for a moment, Geom Mugeuk broke the silence.

“It has not been long since I met you, so honestly, I do not yet know what kind of person you are. However, the fact that you proudly lead the orthodox path of the unorthodox while competing with my father is enough to show that you must be an extraordinary person.”

It was a humble answer, yet it elevated both his father and Baek Jagang.

“And since you are the person this friend respects most, you must be someone worthy of my respect as well.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze, which had briefly turned toward Bisain, fixed on Baek Jagang.

“However, what I just said was an answer formed through others. I would like to give you the answer I feel purely for myself. I want to know more about what kind of person you are, Alliance Leader. So please come to my tavern and have a drink.”

Baek Jagang looked down at Geom Mugeuk and fell into thought. If he went, he would meet Geom Ujin. This decision could change not only him, but even the fate of the Sado Alliance.

At last, Baek Jagang made up his mind.

“Tonight’s banquet is canceled.”

Then he added a startling reason.

“We will drink at that tavern you frequent.”

At Baek Jagang’s refreshing decision, a bright smile spread across Geom Mugeuk’s face.

“Thank you very much, Alliance Leader.”

“Instead, as you said, you must resolve the matter of the Martial Alliance yourself. After that, we will set a specific schedule. However, if the Martial Alliance does not accept it, this decision is canceled.”

“Understood. I will contact you as soon as it is resolved.”

Geom Mugeuk knew. He knew just how difficult a decision this had been for the leader of the Sado Alliance. Would anyone leap into enemy territory knowing how the situation might unfold? He had just wagered his life.

“I still do not know exactly what kind of person you are, Alliance Leader, but I must say this.”

“What is it?”

“You are magnificent.”

Since goosebumps did not rise at his ears, it seemed the Young Cult Leader genuinely thought him magnificent.

“I have no need for flattery.”

With a smile, Geom Mugeuk bowed politely.

“Then please take care until we meet again.”

Geom Mugeuk left the Alliance Leader Hall.

The leader of the Sado Alliance was someone he had not known well before his regression, but it turned out he was such a straightforward man. I like him.

After waiting for a short while in front of the Alliance Leader Hall, Bisain came out after him.

“Are you leaving right away?”

At Bisain’s question, Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“Where will you go now?”

“I must stop by the safe house and bring someone back to the main cult. I also need to meet the Poison King. After stopping by the main cult, I will go to the Martial Alliance again. If things go well there, I will send word immediately.”

“You are truly busy.”

He was a man whose specialty was suddenly appearing and suddenly leaving, but this time, Bisain felt regret at parting with Geom Mugeuk.

“If you go, you will meet Squad Leader Jin as well.”

Jin Hagun had been disappointed that he could not follow them to the Sado Alliance, and now Bisain had ended up in the opposite position.

“Come with me.”

“Do you think the Martial Alliance Leader would be pleased to see the three of us together? If I go for no reason, I will only irritate him. Why are you looking at me like that?”

Then came Geom Mugeuk’s surprise question.

“When will you dance?”

Bisain’s flustered face flushed hot.

“Surely the Young Alliance Leader of the Sado Alliance would not go back on words spoken by his own mouth?”

“I will keep it. I said I will keep it!”

Now even his ears had turned red. In any case, when he was with this Geom Mugeuk, he could not let his guard down for even a moment.

Fortunately, Illang came and saved him. Illang handed over the sword box he had been keeping.

Bisain then passed it back to Geom Mugeuk.

“Here is the Sun-Moon Sword you left in my care.”

“Thank you for the gift.”

Just as Geom Mugeuk’s lips twitched, about to ask about the dance again.

“I am currently in possession of two swords.”

“Then I will hold back. I must.”

This playful and persistent man would tease him over this for a long time to come. In the end, he would have to dance someday.

After decorating their final banter with talk of dancing, the two parted in good spirits.

“I will absolutely persuade the Martial Alliance Leader, so let us meet at our main cult.”

Chapter 377: Flattery in Peaceful Ears.
---------------------------------------
“What are you looking at so intently?”

Mrs. Yun, Jo Chunbae’s mother-in-law, approached a young man who was staring at something in a corner of the inner house courtyard.

The young man was watching a small bug crawling in the flower bed. He was someone she had never seen during her stay here.

Since the young man remained silent, only watching the bug, Mrs. Yun offered an apology.

“Did this old woman disturb you? I am sorry, sir.”

Mrs. Yun treated everyone working at this inner house with the utmost respect. Not only the martial artists but also the servants and maids—she treated them all with courtesy. It was not out of fear, but out of gratitude.

She had lived a life of nothing but hardship, yet lately she had been enjoying such comfort. Every meal, they served her all kinds of precious dishes and provided new clothes to change into. For the first time in her life, she wore silk garments. She had slept in late and even taken afternoon naps, something she had never done before. So how could she not be grateful to the people here?

Just as Mrs. Yun was about to turn around, the young man spoke.

“It is called a dragon poison bug. It is a bug you can only see in this region.”

“Ah, I see.”

Mrs. Yun went to his side and squatted down with him.

He looked young in appearance, but his demeanor and voice seemed mature.

When the bug crawled away and disappeared into the grass, Mrs. Yun spoke while looking at the young man’s waist.

“Your coin pouches are quite cute.”

The young man wore several pouches, each embroidered with one of the animals of the twelve zodiac signs. Only merchants in the streets wore so many pouches like this. Of course, since he was someone she had met here, he probably was not a merchant.

Then the young man said something unexpected.

“I have tried your son-in-law’s cooking before.”

Mrs. Yun was startled.

“How?”

“I also live near that place.”

“Ah, so that is how it was. Please visit often in the future too. Our son-in-law Jo is truly kind and diligent.”

At the words that he was a guest of her son-in-law’s tavern, Mrs. Yun was overjoyed.

“Are you hungry? There is food in the kitchen; shall I bring you some?”

She assumed that because he was so young, he must be doing chores here. Even if he was a martial artist, he was probably a low-level one.

“I am fine.”

“No, you are at an age where you need to eat well. Please wait a moment.”

Mrs. Yun ran toward the building.

Watching her, the young man muttered as if to himself.

“I have already passed that age.”

Shortly after, Mrs. Yun came out with food on a tray.

But the young man was no longer there.

“Where could he have gone in that short time? He should have waited just a bit.”

She felt deeply disappointed.

At that moment, Jo Chunbae walked out.

“Our son-in-law Jo, aren’t you hungry? Shall I prepare a meal?”

She was a mother-in-law who tried to feed him whenever she saw him.

“I am fine. Besides, it is I who should be preparing meals for you.”

“No, you have spent your whole life busy taking care of others. You should rest properly at a time like this.”

How could this be only about the mother-in-law? She had suffered far more.

While staying at the inner house, Jo Chunbae had gone through several stages of psychological change.

At first, he had been bewildered but happy. He felt regret that his children, who had married and lived far away, could not be here together.

But as the days passed, his heart grew uneasy. Was it all right to live in such luxury? Was it all right to leave the tavern empty for so long?

Then, eventually, he had begun resting deeply with a resigned “whatever will be, will be” attitude. Above all, it was enough that his mother-in-law, wife, and his younger sister-in-law and her husband were living comfortably. Besides, it was not as if he could leave just because he wanted to.

“But what is that food for?”

“There was a young man here I took a liking to, so I was going to give it to him, but he left in that short time.”

“Who?”

“There was a cute and handsome young man.”

Jo Chunbae tilted his head. There had been no such young man?

“Ah, he said he had been to your shop too.”

“What? My tavern? That can’t be.”

Just then, the young man returned. In his hands was a large bundle of nameless medicinal herbs.

“Your elder’s knees seemed to be in quite bad shape.”

“When you get old, there is no place that does not hurt. But how did you know my knees hurt?”

Her knees were chronically afflicted.

“If you boil these and drink them, walking should become a bit easier.”

“Why would you go to such trouble… Thank you, truly thank you.”

Mrs. Yun was so moved she did not know what to do.

“So our warrior is not just handsome in face. Here, put that down and eat this.”

Mrs. Yun treated the young man as if he were her own grandchild.

Watching that scene, Jo Chunbae turned pale, eyes wide and mouth agape. He was so shocked he could not speak.

Right now, his mother-in-law was patting the Poison King on the back, telling him to eat plenty. She said that even these days, there were such grateful young men.

“Aaargh! You mustn’t!”

Jo Chunbae shouted.

The Poison King and Mrs. Yun looked at Jo Chunbae.

“Why are you like this?”

At Mrs. Yun’s question, Jo Chunbae bowed his head to the Poison King.

“Oh! Poison King-nim!”

He was the Poison King who had even left his name on the tavern wall—how could he not recognize him?

Belatedly realizing the young man’s exalted status, Mrs. Yun was flustered.

“Oh! Forgive me for failing to recognize your noble presence. Please kill me!”

The Poison King stopped Mrs. Yun from prostrating on the floor and handed her the herbs.

“I cannot accept such precious things.”

“I am indebted to your son-in-law, so I give them. Please do not mind it too much.”

Having said that, the Poison King immediately entered the building.

When he had arrived here yesterday, he heard from the steward of the inner house that Jo Chunbae’s family had come. He had deliberately not revealed that he had come so that they could stay comfortably. He had likely given them the best room, but if they knew he had arrived, Jo Chunbae would try to give up the room. The room was one thing, but they would also feel heavily burdened.

So he had spent two days quietly in the guest hall with his subordinates without announcing his presence.

And because the Poison King knew that Geom Mugik cherished Jo Chunbae, he had established Jo Chunbae’s prestige in front of his mother-in-law.

“Thank you, truly thank you, Poison King-nim!”

When that fearsome Poison King gave her the herbs and left, Jo Chunbae was deeply moved.

“Son-in-law Jo, I wonder if I made some mistake.”

“No. If you had, would he have given you such precious things?”

“He is truly a wonderful person.”

She bowed toward the building the Poison King had entered.

“What luxury this is, thanks to you.”

“I will boil the herbs for you.”

Jo Chunbae’s heart pounded as he received the herbs. Herbs given directly by the Poison King? His hands trembled. He was careful lest even a single leaf fall.

He wanted to see one person. Because all this happiness and joy was a gift from him.

“Young Cult Leader, please return safely. When you return, I will—”

Right at that moment.

“What will you do for me?”

Jo Chunbae turned around in surprise and saw Geom Mugik entering there. Geom Mugik, who had departed for the Four Devils Alliance, had rushed here like the wind at full speed.

“Young Cult Leader!”

At his shout, the Poison King, who had entered the building, opened the window and showed himself.

“You came faster than I thought?”

“I rushed here like the wind because I wanted to see the Poison King.”

“You mean you wanted to see the tavern keeper over there.”

Geom Mugik looked at Jo Chunbae and smiled.

“Of course, I also wanted to see our tavern keeper.”

Seeing that Geom Mugik had returned safely, Jo Chunbae was overjoyed.

And relief washed over him. It was the feeling of finally stepping onto solid ground after drifting through a dreamlike illusion.

“I do not know how I should repay this grace.”

“There will be a way.”

“Yes?”

“Later, a precious guest will come to the tavern, so please show your skills then.”

At Geom Mugik’s meaningful gaze, Jo Chunbae blinked with a bewildered expression. He had not said such things even when the Demon Venerable came. Just who was coming that he would say such things?

In the meantime, Mrs. Yun and all of Jo Chunbae’s family came out to welcome Geom Mugik.

“Thanks to you, our family has made memories we will never forget even if we die. Thank you, Young Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugik took Mrs. Yun’s hand and spoke with warm eyes.

“Please stay healthy, Elder.”

Since it was decided they would leave the inner house the next day, Jo Chunbae prepared that evening’s meal himself. He had wanted to personally serve food to those he was indebted to.

He served alcohol and dishes not only to Geom Mugik, the Poison King, and the Poison Children, but also to the inner house’s martial artists, male servants, and female servants.

While Jo Chunbae displayed his skills, his wife expressed her gratitude one by one to those who had cared for them.

The Poison King was absentmindedly lost in his own world while eating.

Sangseon, who was sitting beside him, poured wine for Geom Mugik instead.

“Thank you in many ways.”

Since meeting Geom Mugik, the Poison King was changing, and that change was what Sangseon had long wished for. Being here instead of the Thousand Poison Forest, gathering herbs with Ma Bul, and eating together were things that would have been impossible in the past.

“It is I who should be grateful. Because you came with us this time, we overcame great danger.”

“When you return, please visit the Thousand Poison Forest.”

“Next time. I plan to greet my father and then immediately depart from the cult to visit the Martial Arts Alliance.”

“This time, you will turn the Martial Arts Alliance upside down, I suppose.”

Geom Mugik answered with a smile at Sangseon’s joke.

“How could that be? It is a simple matter of meeting the Martial Arts Alliance Leader and obtaining his understanding on one matter.”

Then the Poison King abruptly spoke. He had been listening to everything even though they thought he was not.

“Will it really be simple?”

At the Poison King’s words, Geom Mugik emptied the wine cup before him. He did not know either. How would Jin Paecheon take it? This was something that neither he nor the Martial Arts Alliance Leader had ever experienced before.

When the pleasant dinner gathering ended, Geom Mugik spoke to Jo Chunbae.

“Now, let us return.”

It was the moment the most splendid vacation in Jo Chunbae’s life ended.

* * *

Geom Mugik, having returned to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, stopped by the Heavenly Demon Hall first.

“Father, I have returned.”

His father received him in the seat he always occupied.

“I heard you swept through the Four Devils Alliance and came back?”

“It is a misunderstanding. I did not even step forward; I only watched. Though you must have received all the reports from the Tongcheon Pavilion.”

Geom Mugik told his father about the events at the Four Devils Alliance.

He had done so in the past as well, but he tried to speak to his father directly like this as much as possible. It did not matter if it was content his father already knew. Just speaking honestly like this—it was not a report, but a conversation.

“I believe there are still forces behind it remaining.”

It would be enough if only Father did not let his guard down. Even if everyone in the world let their guard down, as long as Father and I did not let ours down, it would be enough. Then we could stop them.

“They may pull another trick, so Father and I must watch closely.”

He also emphasized that they must not let go of the string of tension.

“Defense alone is not the answer.”

It meant he would find them first and eliminate them; that was Father’s way. He had likely already given the order to the Chief Strategist, and it was already in progress.

“If that is possible, that would be the best method.”

But judging by their meticulousness so far, they would not be easy to find.

“Oh, and I invited the Four Devils Alliance Leader to the Pungnyu Tavern.”

He mentioned it casually on purpose, as if it were no big deal. His father gave no reaction. Unable to bear looking at his father’s face, Geom Mugik lowered his head and muttered.

“I know that you dislike the Four Devils Alliance Leader, but once you actually meet him, he might unexpectedly be a fine person, might he not? Moreover…”

Since there was still no reaction, he cautiously raised his head and looked at his father.

His father’s gaze said it all.

You know what you’re doing, don’t you?

He could have stopped by the Martial Arts Alliance on the way back and taken care of that matter first.

But he thought it was important to tell Father even now. He had to get permission, had to gauge his mood like this, and if he said no, he would persuade and negotiate.

Because there was a big difference between telling him like this even now and saying “It already happened.”

The corners of his father’s mouth slowly rose with an expression that said he saw right through him. It was his father’s scornful smile, one he had not seen in a long time. Seeing it, he finally felt relief.

Ah, I’m home.

His father had likely already seen right through why he had invited the Four Devils Alliance Leader.

As expected, his father did not ask about the reason.

“He won’t just stay put at the Martial Arts Alliance, will he?”

“That is why I plan to go meet the Martial Arts Alliance Leader personally.”

“Do you think Jin Paecheon will accept it just because you do?”

Just as the Poison King had, his father also did not take this matter lightly.

Of course, Geom Mugik had one thing he was sure of.

“Because I have allies on the Martial Arts Alliance side as well. There are two of them there.”

* * *

“Master!”

The Fist Demon was looking up at the cliff he meant to bring down.

“Have you come?”

I wanted to bring Bisain along and joke with him. Look, I’d say, your face is cute compared to our master’s.

“I’ve only just returned after finishing matters with the Sado Alliance. I paid my respects to Father and came straight here to you, Master.”

After making a show of it, I stood beside him and looked up at the cliff.

“By any chance, is this cliff still growing?”

At the look in the Fist Demon’s eyes asking what I meant, I looked back at the cliff and answered.

“Somehow, it seems bigger and higher than before.”

At that, a strange light passed through the Fist Demon’s eyes.

“Or could it be that you have grown?”

I could guess what he meant, but I pretended not to know and asked.

“Shouldn’t the more one grows, the more trifling one’s opponent seems?”

“That is only true of those at the bottom. The first-rate see their opponents more accurately the more they grow.”

That must also be why true masters were humble.

Had my martial realm risen as I achieved great completion in the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art? The cliff truly felt more majestic.

“Then to you, Master, this cliff must look even more tremendous.”

“The fact that flattery is reaching my peaceful ears tells me someone really has returned.”

“Would only flattery have returned? I also have a gift here.”

The Fist Demon recognized the gauntlets I handed him.

“It’s the Fighting God!”

“You recognize it.”

“No one who practices fist arts could fail to know this item. Where did you get it?”

“I received it as a gift from the leader of the Sado Alliance.”

The Fist Demon looked dumbfounded, then soon nodded with an expression that said, If it’s you, that could happen.

“Why give to me what was given to you?”

“Would the Fighting God want to be on my hands? Or would it want yours, Master?”

Since he had always lived with bare fists, I had thought he might say he did not need it, but he accepted it without hesitation.

“Yes. If I am to bring down that cliff, I will need it.”

Yes, as if I were the only one growing. From the way he accepted it gladly without putting his pride first, I could tell. The Fist Demon was growing too.

And I could also understand why the Fighting God was called the finest among gauntlets. Its elasticity was so excellent that it fit even the Fist Demon’s enormous hands perfectly.

“I think I understand now the saying that every treasure has its rightful owner. It truly suits you.”

The Fist Demon answered my thanks by clenching those great fists in front of me.

Yes, everyone had to become stronger like this. If they schemed in the darkness over there, then we would grow stronger like this under the sunlight. Let’s see who wins.

* * *

After parting from the Fist Demon, I entrusted the Sun-Moon Sword to the guards and immediately left the main cult. Since I had to leave the cult right away anyway, I decided I would greet the other Demon Supremes after I returned from the Murim Alliance.

“You’re going to leave us behind again, aren’t you?”

Jeokyeon, the captain of the guards, had now reached a state of complete detachment.

“I think I’ll have to this time too.”

He tried to persuade me with self-deprecating eyes that said, We really are useless bastards, but it did not work on me.

In the end, Jeokyeon shouted.

“Then please remember this, at least. If anything happens to you, Young Cult Leader, we all die too.”

Yes, if something happened to me, they would all die anyway.

“If you want to follow me, get stronger! Stronger! Stronger!”

As I passed through Maga Village, I saw the Pungryu Tavern. Having opened again after a long while, it was filled with people from the marketplace gathered around and talking with Jo Chunbae. How many things must he have wanted to say?

‘Owner, don’t do it yet. There is still a boast left that is truly worth making.’

I used Swift Step and raced toward the Murim Alliance.

Even as I flowed through the world like light, I could see it. The flowers by the roadside were beginning to show their buds.

Spring was coming.

Chapter 378: We're Living in the Same Murim.
--------------------------------------------
“Miss Jin, please accept my sincere feelings.”

The young man confessed as if pleading, his eyes earnest.

Compared to the lovestruck young man, Jin Haryeong’s reaction was calm. It was something like a side effect from when Hubei’s First Beauty had led the gathering of rising talents.

“Young Master Im.”

“Miss Jin.”

How much do you even know about me to confess? We have things we must do. I put work before love. She had tried various methods, but this one was the quickest and most certain.

“There’s someone I like.”

The young man was startled. When she said this, the reactions were generally divided into two types: those who gave up and turned away, and those who, like this man, could not abandon their lingering attachment to the very end.

“Who is it?”

“What if you knew? Would you go to that person and try to stop him?”

Here, too, they split into two types: those who belatedly realized they had gone too far and backed down, and those who, like this man, tried to see it through to the end.

“If I could, yes. Even if I had to do that, I’d want to show you how much I like you, Miss Jin.”

Perhaps someone had advised this man that if he wanted to win a woman, he had to charge forward with spirit. Perhaps that method had even succeeded once or twice in his life. But at least, it would not work on her.

“What if someone came now and tried to stop Young Master Im from liking what he likes?”

“I would not let it pass.”

“I feel the same way.”

Jin Haryeong’s gaze turned cold. If he did not back down here, she would have to release her aura as well.

Fortunately, this was as far as the young man went. He lowered his head and apologized.

“Please forgive my rudeness. I’m not usually this kind of person.”

Only then did Jin Haryeong’s gaze soften.

“I’ll see you at the next gathering.”

After giving a deep bow, the young man left.

Then Chuho, Jin Haryeong’s escort warrior, who had been standing some distance behind her, walked over and said,

“Shall I take measures so he can’t attend the gatherings?”

He had been so rude, and yet he claimed he was not that kind of person? Unlike Chuho, who wanted him dealt with sharply, Jin Haryeong was lenient.

“Leave him be. If we did that, his life would needlessly become gloomy and dark.”

Having received many confessions, she knew how to handle the other party.

“Do you think taking back feelings for someone is something you can do at will? We should give him time too. Whether he comes or doesn’t come should be his choice.”

At some point, Chuho had begun to feel that Jin Haryeong had become much more mature. And he knew when that point had been.

“But do you truly have someone you like?”

Jin Haryeong looked at Chuho with an expression that asked why he was asking that.

“You always refuse them with those words. I wondered whether you were saying it as an excuse, or perhaps…”

“I do.”

When Chuho feigned surprise, she asked in return,

“You asked even though you knew, didn’t you? Why pretend not to know between us?”

“My apologies. I simply thought you might want to talk to someone about that person, my lady.”

Chuho knew. He knew that Geom Mugeuk had taken a place in Jin Haryeong’s heart.

She had revealed those feelings to no one. It would be better if she simply blurted out a confession like that fellow earlier; at least then her heart might feel lighter.

Chuho was worried. Worried that if this went on, she might even fall ill from pent-up emotion. That was why he hoped she would at least confide in him.

“Are you worried about me?”

“No.”

“It’s not to the point where you need to worry. I just miss him sometimes. On days like this, I miss him once more. Very occasionally, he appears in my dreams. That’s about it.”

And on days like today, without a single cloud in the sky.

—See that color of the sky over there? That’s the color of my sword aura.

She thought of his words too.

Jin Haryeong recalled what Geom Mugeuk had said before he left.

—Near our cult’s main headquarters, there’s a tavern with good wine and a good owner. I’ll invite you there next time.

Liar.

Then again, was it because Geom Mugeuk was a liar? The problem was probably the places where he and she had been born.

Jin Haryeong said it was not to the point of worrying, but.

‘You like him as a man, that is.’

But Chuho did not say those words aloud. The two of them could never be together. Jin Haryeong must know that well too.

“Then one day, he’ll suddenly show up and startle me, won’t he?”

It was at that very moment.

Someone spoke from behind them.

“I suppose that day is today.”

The two of them turned around in shock, and Geom Mugeuk was standing some distance away.

“Have you been well?”

Jin Haryeong asked with a startled face,

“How long have you been here?”

“Just now.”

Fortunately, it did not seem as though he had heard the conversation she had shared with Chuho.

She turned to Chuho and said,

“Find out where my older brother is right now.”

“Understood.”

Chuho immediately obeyed. He knew it was an order that also carried a request for him to step away for a moment. Normally, he should not leave his post, but Geom Mugeuk was an exception. In any case, if Geom Mugeuk harbored ill intentions, Chuho knew well that his presence would be of no help. The fact that he had failed to notice Geom Mugeuk standing behind them just now was proof enough.

“Shall we have a drink?”

“Sounds good.”

Jin Haryeong’s mood improved. There was a distinct fluttering in her heart that she felt only when she met Geom Mugeuk.

The two went to the inn where they had first met before and ordered noodles and wine.

Geom Mugeuk did not hurry. Persuading the Murim Alliance Leader was important, but his relationship with her was just as important.

Eating with her, drinking with her, bantering with her. This was his life. It was as important as killing his enemies.

“How have you been?”

At Jin Haryeong’s question, Geom Mugeuk made a fuss.

“Don’t even ask. You wouldn’t believe how tremendous the things that happened were.”

“Tell me. All of it.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at her. He had expected her to say, What a bluff! But she truly seemed ready to listen to everything.

“I heard from my brother. That you went to the Sado Alliance with the young alliance leader of the Sado Alliance. You have no idea how alert our alliance was over that.”

That was why he had come. The Murim Alliance was watching every move of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Sado Alliance with keen attention.

“You didn’t know? When you move now, the murim begins to move with you.”

“They’re worried a loach might muddy the water.”

Now Jin Haryeong knew.

What her grandfather and the Murim Alliance were worried about was whether the wish-fulfilling jewel this dragon before her would grasp when he ascended would be the blue of that clear sky, or a jewel drenched in blood.

“Is everything all right on the Sado Alliance side?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded once.

In that moment, a thought came to her.

‘Well, of course. You went there.’

To her, Geom Mugeuk was that kind of person.

“I’m the granddaughter of the Murim Alliance Leader in name and fact, yet I’m relieved to hear that the Sado Alliance is all right!”

At her banter, Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“At the same time, it’s also a friend’s matter.”

“A friend?”

“A friend of a friend is a friend.”

“My grandfather would faint if he heard that. If even the young alliance leader of the Sado Alliance became your friend. You alone are enough.”

She liked talking with Geom Mugeuk no matter the subject. Whether they spoke of the fate of the murim, or of how the noodles were especially salty today. He seemed like someone who would understand and accept whatever she said. Someone who would show her a new perspective.

“By the way, where is Ian?”

“She’s busy these days. She’s out on business.”

Ian had left to recruit Seojin, the younger sibling of Gwiryeongja, as a captain of the Ghost Shadow Squad. For the first time in her life, she was alone in the Central Plains, carving out her own fate.

“I miss Ian.”

“Then see her.”

“That’s easy to say.”

“What’s difficult about it? If you miss her, come visit anytime. Or we can come.”

“That may be true in your murim. It doesn’t work in mine.”

“We live in the same murim.”

Jin Haryeong silently looked at Geom Mugeuk.

Were they truly in the same murim? Even now, to her, Geom Mugeuk was someone like a dream, appearing suddenly and vanishing like smoke.

“Actually, I came because I have a favor to ask you and Captain Jin.”

“What favor?”

“There’s something I must see the alliance leader about and persuade him to do. In case I fail to persuade him myself, I need a second and third contingency.”

“I’m the second contingency?”

“Captain Jin is the third.”

“What on earth is the favor?”

“Let’s meet your brother and talk all at once.”

Jin Haryeong met dozens of new rising talents every month, but it was always stifling and tedious. Yet less than a quarter hour had passed since she met Geom Mugeuk, and her heart had already begun to race.

Just then, seeing Chuho enter the inn, Geom Mugeuk rose first from his seat.

“Now then, shall we go meet the third contingency?”

* * *

Jin Hagun was training in the practice yard.

The practice yard was heated fiercely by his energy.

These days, he had been pushing himself to his limits.

He felt anxious that as he was now, he would not be able to face Geom Mugeuk. And was it only Geom Mugeuk? In the eyes of Bisain, his realistic rival, he had certainly felt a fire as well.

Training was not the only thing that had changed.

As he had told Geom Mugeuk, he also tried to observe people more carefully when dealing with them. Things he would have once let pass with a “I suppose that’s how it is,” he now thought over once more. Instead of judging by his own standards, he tried to consider the other person’s position.

It had happened not long ago.

One of the warriors of the Demon Extermination Squad requested leave. It overlapped with a mission, but Jin Hagun gladly permitted it. In the past, that would have been the end of it.

Jin Hagun called him aside and asked the reason for his leave. As it turned out, there was conflict within his household over the selection of a successor. Since the Demon Extermination Squad was made up mostly of scions from prestigious families, such problems commonly arose.

Jin Hagun went one step further than simply asking the reason.

—Do you perhaps need my help? If you do, tell me anytime.

The man refused the help, but he was deeply moved that Jin Hagun had said those words.

Because he had never said such a thing before.

Of course, the feeling had always been there. They were comrades who had crossed battlefields together, so how could he not feel that way? But he had never said it directly.

Through this opportunity, Jin Hagun realized anew just how great the power of words was between people.

—I thought you naturally knew how I felt.

Words like that, spoken after everything was over, were excuses.

—What need is there for words between us?

No. Only when you said it directly did the other person know.

And now that he had tried it, he understood. For someone with his personality, who always tried to keep the words he had spoken, even speaking was not easy.

What if his subordinate had truly asked for help? What if he mentioned someone in his family and asked Jin Hagun to pressure that person into backing down?

One had to be prepared to go that far before speaking in the first place. Only then could one answer without fluster, Very well, leave it to me.

Suddenly, Geom Mugeuk came to mind.

That man said all sorts of things. Was he prepared to take responsibility for every word he uttered?

Seeing as he could not imagine Geom Mugeuk avoiding responsibility with some absurd excuse.

‘You live in quite a tiring way too.’

Having tried it himself, Jin Hagun now realized how difficult it was. He even thought that perhaps his own bluntness had been a form of escape from reality.

At that moment, along with the news that guests had arrived, two people entered the place. They were Geom Mugeuk and Jin Haryeong.

“It’s been a while.”

Since Jin Hagun had just been thinking of Geom Mugeuk, how surprised must he have been?

“We haven’t seen each other for a while, and you’ve already become awkward again. It’s me, your friend.”

He was so glad to see him that, instead, he could not show his joy well.

“Why have you come here?”

“So brusque! Why else? I came because I missed you.”

Then Jin Haryeong, who was beside him, tattled.

“That’s a lie. He says he came because he needs our help.”

Jin Hagun was already worried about his younger sister, who had become excited, but he was just as stirred up himself.

“What is it?”

“I invited the Sado Alliance Leader to my regular tavern in front of our cult.”

For a moment, Jin Hagun thought the invited person was Bisain. He mistakenly thought Geom Mugeuk had come to say, I invited Bisain, so you should come too.

“I won’t go.”

Realizing that Jin Hagun had misheard, Geom Mugeuk and Jin Haryeong stared blankly at him. Under their gazes, Jin Hagun flinched and asked again.

“Who did you say you invited?”

“The Sado Alliance Leader.”

Only then did Jin Hagun’s eyes widen.

He clearly had limits. Just as he had misheard a moment ago, the idea of inviting the Sado Alliance Leader to a tavern in front of the Murim Alliance headquarters had not even existed in his mind.

But Geom Mugeuk always leapt far beyond those boundaries. He leapt right over them and, as expected, gave a reason Jin Hagun did not have.

“I simply wanted to treat him to a drink.”

If it had been anyone else, he would have thought it an absurd lie, but by now he knew full well what kind of person Geom Mugeuk was. When he went to the Sado Alliance this time, he must have left an impression deep enough to invite even its alliance leader.

And it was not as if Jin Haryeong had not been surprised.

“What kind of joke is this, after meeting for the first time in so long?”

“It’s not a joke.”

Yes, she knew it was not a joke. Because the thought rising in her mind was that it was truly just like him.

Jin Hagun asked again.

“Did the Sado Alliance Leader accept the invitation?”

“He said he would, if the Murim Alliance Leader takes this meeting in good faith.”

“Good faith? How can anyone take it in good faith when the Demon Cult and the Sado Alliance are holding secret talks?”

“Secret talks? Have you ever seen secret talks where everything is laid bare like this?”

“Whatever it is! The Alliance Leader will never permit it.”

He knew better than anyone how wary his grandfather was of the Demon Cult and the Sado Alliance, and how much he disliked them. The only reason he had formed any friendship with Geom Mugeuk at all was because Geom Mugeuk had deep ties with his grandson and granddaughter, and had later even saved their lives. It was truly an exceptional case.

“That’s why I came to ask for help. I want you two to persuade him with me.”

“Wouldn’t it have been better to simply invite him without telling us in the first place?”

At Jin Hagun’s probing question, Jin Haryeong looked at Geom Mugeuk with the same thought in mind.

“I couldn’t do that.”

“Why?”

“Because you and I are friends, aren’t we?”

In truth, the reason he intended to invite Baek Jagang was to prevent a war that might occur in the future. But if, because of this invitation, the Murim Alliance believed that the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Sado Alliance had joined hands behind their backs, then the invitation would bring more harm than good.

“Even if it hadn’t been a condition set by the Sado Alliance Leader, I would have come to ask permission.”

A thought suddenly occurred to Jin Hagun. What if it were him? Whatever the intention, if it was something that absolutely had to be done?

Would he not have simply gone ahead without creating unnecessary variables, then later said, “I’m sorry. It just ended up that way as I was doing it”?

When that thought surfaced, his face grew slightly hot. It was because he felt as though Geom Mugeuk was swimming through the sea called friendship, while he himself had only dipped his ankles in.

“I want the two of you to help me persuade the Alliance Leader.”

Jin Hagun firmly shook his head.

“Absolutely not!”

Jin Haryeong was the same. She shook her head.

“I can’t either. You’re saying that because you don’t know our grandfather well. If we bring that up, we’ll be driven out of the Alliance.”

“If you’re driven out, our cult will take you in!”

“Then Elder Brother won’t even get to become the successor.”

“How about using this opportunity to free yourself from your lust for power?”

“Excellent words, heir of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult!”

At that, Geom Mugeuk let out an exaggerated sigh.

“Ah! I never thought I’d be abandoned by my friends!”

The moment he heard those words, a doubt bloomed in Jin Hagun’s heart.

‘Did he really not expect this? Someone as clever as him?’

It suddenly occurred to him that perhaps Geom Mugeuk had not simply come to ask his grandfather’s permission.

‘Surely not?’

The one who spoke aloud the thought that had risen in his mind was his younger sister, who was even quicker on the uptake than he was.

“Don’t tell me you’re thinking of inviting Grandfather too?”

Chapter 379: Is Your Gaze Still the Same?
-----------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk asked Jin Haryeong instead of answering.

“Which do you think will be harder? Getting permission or inviting the Martial Alliance Lord?”

She could not answer that comparison, but one thing was certain.

“Both are impossible. There’s no way Grandfather would ever sit down and drink with the Demon Cult Leader or the Unorthodox Alliance Lord.”

Geom Mugeuk lowered his head and let his shoulders droop.

“Fine, then. I suppose I’ll go see the Alliance Lord to carry out an impossible mission. All alone, lonely and desolate.”

When neither of them stopped him even after he walked all the way to the entrance, Geom Mugeuk returned to his original seat.

“You two are too much. Is this how you treat a friend?”

At that moment, Jin Hagun asked,

“Why did you invite the Unorthodox Alliance Lord? You couldn’t have summoned him all the way to the Demon Cult merely to treat him to drinks. No, even if that had been your intention, the Unorthodox Alliance Lord would never have agreed. How did you persuade him?”

Geom Mugeuk answered honestly.

“Because I hope there will be no war.”

Of course, the two of them could not understand the deeper meaning contained in those words. He could not tell them that his father, the Unorthodox Alliance Lord, dreamed of unifying the martial world.

“You think there’ll be no war if the Unorthodox Alliance Lord drinks in front of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?”

“I believe there will be.”

Jin Haryeong believed him, too. This absurd answer.

Not because Geom Mugeuk’s reply had been so firm.

The reason was simple. If he had not saved her life, she would no longer be in this world.

“Were you serious earlier? About accepting me if I’m expelled from the Alliance?”

“I’ll even hold a welcoming ceremony for you.”

Jin Haryeong imagined the scene for a moment. Herself, expelled from the Alliance, standing in the rain at the main gate of the Demon Cult with a bundle of luggage on her back, together with her escort, Chu Ho.

Then Geom Mugeuk would open the gate and come out, making some exaggerated remark.

―From now on, you’re my maid.

He was exactly the sort of person who would play such a prank without hesitation. Yes, her faith in Geom Mugeuk did not stem solely from owing him her life.

“Fine. I’ll help.”

The fact that her heart felt lighter made her think she had made the right decision.

“Don’t get involved, Big Brother. If you do, you really will lose your position as heir.”

Jin Hagun looked at his younger sister.

‘How can she be so free?’

There was such an obvious limit within his own heart. A boundary had been drawn around him.

It was a line he had never crossed even once. He had never imagined a life in which he did not become the heir. The same was true of a life in which he opposed his grandfather.

Yet his younger sister crossed that line with ease, leaping over it again and again. In that respect, Jin Haryeong and Geom Mugeuk resembled each other.

Jin Hagun quietly examined his own heart. What was it that he truly wanted? Would crossing that line be mere recklessness? Or would it be a new challenge? He could not know until he crossed it, and so Jin Hagun was torn.

“I told you not to worry about it, Big Brother. You understand his situation too, don’t you?”

Instead of saying that he understood, Geom Mugeuk looked at Jin Hagun. His eyes seemed to hope that Jin Hagun would cross his limits.

‘You’re not going to take responsibility for my life, so why are you looking at me like that?’

Jin Hagun’s gaze shifted to Jin Haryeong.

“You can’t do it alone.”

“What?”

“If you step forward by yourself, you really will be expelled. If the two of us go together, at least you won’t be expelled.”

“You’ll help too, Big Brother?”

Jin Hagun nodded.

She had never imagined that her brother would make such a decision.

“Did you really give up your desire for power?”

“The opposite. I’m doing this to protect my power.”

Jin Hagun’s reason for helping was simple as well.

“I don’t want war either.”
