“Oh! How impressive. A woman challenging the Drunken Demon to a drinking contest.”

The interesting story continued.

“So I said, Do you even know who is standing before you, challenging him to a drinking contest? She said, I do. Then what will we wager? Let’s have the winner grant the loser’s wish. What are we, children? What kind of childish wager is that? Are you not confident? As if that could be! Fine. That’s how it went.”

I filled the Drunken Demon’s empty cup to the brim. As if recalling that day, the Drunken Demon emptied the cup with a deepened gaze.

“She could drink. Yes, she’s one of the top three masters among drinkers, so of course she could drink well. But when she set her mind to it, she drank even better than I expected. The two of us drank every bit of liquor in this pavilion. It wasn’t enough, so we opened the liquor storehouse and drank there.”

Imagining it, it must truly have been a magnificent sight.

“And the result?”

“Why ask? I won.”

I shook my head with an incredulous expression.

“You won? You heartless man. I can’t stand you.”

“I don’t know about other wagers, but can the Drunken Demon lose a drinking contest?”

“So? Did you make your wish too?”

“I did.”

“What did you ask for?”

The Drunken Demon silently looked down at his empty cup.

I knew he was not someone who would easily give his heart to a woman. Please, let it not have been a wish like, Never do this to me again!

“I told her to bring a spring greens dish she made herself as the side dish at our next drinking session.”

“!”

A bright smile spread across my lips.

“Why are you smiling?”

“Because you’re impressive, hyung.”

I filled the Drunken Demon’s cup to the brim.

“This is the most impressive you’ve been since I met you. You always looked so pitiful before. Now you finally seem like a man.”

“So you’re confessing that you looked down on me until now.”

Smiling, the Drunken Demon clinked his cup against mine.

Yeo Bin, who had liked the Drunken Demon for a long time, must have staked everything she had that day. How nervous must she have been?

I did not know how the relationship between the two of them would turn out from now on, but the Drunken Demon had understood her heart all too well.

“These days, we just drink together now and then, like friends.”

That drinking session must have given the Drunken Demon his ease.

“This is all because of you.”

“Why because of me?”

“She said the reason she found the courage was because of you and the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign. And the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign was heavily influenced by you, wasn’t she? In the end, it’s because of you. You influenced me too.”

Yes, it probably was because of me. I acknowledged it. After my regression, so many things had truly been changing. In any case, among all those changes, this was one that made me truly happy.

“When you drink with the spring greens, call me too.”

“I’ll think about it.”

Watching him act smug and show off, I laughed and emptied my cup.

Hyung, just don’t blame me later by saying, “It’s because of you!” in a different sense.

* * *

While meeting the Demon Venerables, I also devoted myself to martial training.

At the heart of my efforts there was always grueling training. I trained whether I met the Demon Venerables or not.

The reason a joke exchanged with a Demon Venerable could provide a clue to enlightenment in martial learning was because it was supported by the endless training I had accumulated every day.

The moment a single word becomes a great enlightenment is the moment one places the final ornament atop a tower painstakingly built high. Without the tower, there can be no final ornament.

I continued to cultivate the Sicheon Secret Art and gained even more time, and within that time, I practiced the Guhwa Demonic Art with all my might.

In that way, twenty-some days passed, and at last, that day arrived.

A strategist sent by Tongcheon Pavilion came to report to me.

“The date of the summit has been set.”

It was set for a date not too far away. Because the three leaders’ resolve to meet was firm, things had proceeded more quickly than expected.

I headed straight for Maga Village.

Just then, in the distance, Jo Chunbae, who had been stretching in front of Pungryu Tavern, saw me and greeted me warmly.

“Young Cult Leader, welcome!”

I walked toward him as he smiled brightly, unaware of what was to come.

Chapter 384: I Cannot Reveal the Secret Ingredients.
----------------------------------------------------
Jo Chunbae had been in an excellent mood lately.

He had taken the most precious vacation of his life, and had played the part of a fine father-in-law as well.

There was truly nothing more he could ask for.

All because of that man.

“Proprietor, have you been well?”

He was living a life in which the Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult greeted him with a bright smile.

“Young Cult Leader, what brings you here at such an early hour?”

“I came because I have something to tell you, Proprietor.”

“Please come in.”

Jo Chunbae escorted Geom Mugeuk inside.

Geom Mugeuk looked around Pungryu Tavern. It had been a long time since he had come, but nothing had changed.

“As expected! The moment I step inside, my heart feels at ease.”

“I hardly know what to do with myself when you say such things.”

Geom Mugeuk sat in his usual seat on the second floor.

“You sit too, Proprietor.”

Jo Chunbae sat across from him, inwardly tense.

‘What could he have to say to me?’

Geom Mugeuk handed him a paper with the date of the meeting written on it.

“I think I will have to rent out the entirety of Pungryu Tavern for this one day. Would that be all right?”

“Of course.”

Geom Mugeuk had saved both his family’s lives and his own. Jo Chunbae would gladly lend it for a month, not merely a day. If he asked to have the tavern, how could he refuse?

“But who is coming, for you to do this?”

Until now, everyone had simply dropped by without warning. The Heavenly Demon dropped by, the Demon Venerables dropped by. So who could be coming that he would notify him in advance and rent out the entire tavern?

“There will be a meeting of our cult. I boasted about you, Proprietor. I said I wished to host them here no matter what.”

“Thank you very much, Young Cult Leader.”

There was a slight misunderstanding then.

He thought a meeting normally held within the Divine Cult would be held here so they could sell liquor and spread the name of Pungryu Tavern far and wide.

“Please take care in preparing the liquor and dishes. I apologize that I cannot reveal in advance who the guests are. Also, I ask that you not let this meeting become known outside until the day itself.”

“Of course. Please do not worry.”

Jo Chunbae laughed heartily with confidence.

‘At last, an opportunity has come to repay even a little of the debt I owe the Young Cult Leader.’

His gaze turned toward the back wall. No matter how honored the guests were.

‘I am a man who has even received the Cult Leader!’

And that was not all. He had also hosted drinking gatherings for the Demon Venerables, as well as the Demon Lord and the Lord of Hwangcheon Pavilion.

“When it comes to receiving honored guests, have I not been through every hardship there is? Please leave it to me!”

Proprietor, these guests are people who mainly wage aerial battles.

Though it was top secret, he could have given Jo Chunbae a hint in advance about who was coming. But that would only have the opposite effect.

Jo Chunbae would not sleep a wink all night from worry, and he was the sort of man who would scale a cliff to find fine ingredients. No, Jo Chunbae’s heart might not even last until that day.

“Then I will trust only you, Proprietor.”

In truth, if Geom Mugeuk had his way, he wanted to barge in as usual. There would be customers on the first floor, and the Martial Alliance Leader would say, “Let us pass,” as he made his way through the people and went up to the second floor.

He simply wanted to show them the place as it usually was. As he always had, Jo Chunbae would handle them well too. A three-way summit among the customers!

Of course, he could not bring himself to insist that far. It would put the guards in far too awkward a position.

“I will come back a little later to eat with my brother.”

“I will prepare what you usually enjoy.”

After leaving the tavern, Geom Mugeuk walked toward a middle-aged man standing across the street, wearing a bamboo hat.

“Did you not come in because of me?”

At that, the middle-aged man removed his bamboo hat.

“You recognized me.”

Surprisingly, he was Chef Wang, the chief representative chef of the Cult Leader’s Hall.

It seemed the Chief Strategist had sent Chef Wang to ensure this meeting was flawless. Apart from trusting Geom Mugeuk, the people to be entertained were the Martial Alliance Leader and the Unorthodox Alliance Leader.

“I came to see if there might be anything the tavern needed.”

That was what he said, but he had come to inspect it. How the cooking was, how the hygiene was.

“Do not mind me. Do the work you were assigned.”

“Would that truly be all right?”

Since he knew Geom Mugeuk had chosen the venue for this meeting, Chef Wang was inwardly concerned.

“I am fine, but that tavern will not be.”

“Pardon?”

“You are none other than Chef Wang, and if you inspect that shabby tavern, how could it possibly satisfy you? From one to ten, nothing will please you.”

Chef Wang acknowledged it as well. After all, he himself had wondered whether it made any sense to hold this meeting at a tavern like that.

“Then may I ask why you chose that place?”

There were many reasons, but he gave the one that would resonate most deeply with Chef Wang.

“The food is delicious.”

Sure enough, Chef Wang immediately accepted it and nodded.

“If that is the reason, it is sufficient.”

“Then I will see you later.”

Geom Mugeuk watched Chef Wang enter Pungryu Tavern, then turned his steps toward the main compound.

The person he met at the main compound was his elder brother, Geom Muyang. He was in his office, handling work related to this meeting.

“What brings you here?”

“I came to see your face, Brother.”

“I am busy. If you have seen my face, go.”

He was acting curt for no reason, but there was a look of welcome on his face.

“Let us eat together. We can look over the meeting place as well.”

“I told you I am busy.”

“Our brother has all sorts of bad points, but I never imagined you would even become the very embodiment of armchair theorizing! If you sit in your office and make decisions without even looking at the scene, how can anything run properly? You should go yourself, see it with your own eyes, and say, Proprietor, should this not be fixed like this? Little brother, should you not yield on this as well?”

Geom Muyang covered his ears and rose from his seat.

* * *

The two of them walked side by side toward Maga Village.

“You will attend the summit too, right, Brother?”

“Why? Do you wish I would not?”

“As if that could be true. I must boast to the people of the Martial Alliance and the Unorthodox Alliance that I beat this man and became the successor.”

Geom Mugeuk had been worried that Geom Muyang might say he would not attend.

But Geom Muyang was unexpectedly堂堂.

“If I do not go, will they not say I was ashamed of being pushed out of the successor’s seat and therefore did not show myself? I must go, if only to avoid that disgrace.”

It could not have been something he wanted. He was grateful to his brother for saying he would attend like this.

“Now that I think about it, I should not let you come, Brother. Everyone might start whispering, why did he make the younger brother his successor when he had such a fine eldest son?”

“So long as you understand.”

“This time, Captain Jin and his sister from the Martial Alliance will come too. We have to show them that we are the better blood siblings.”

“Did you call them just to show that?”

“Yes. I called them to boast about what kind of people I have around me. I mean it.”

“How childish.”

As they talked about this and that, Geom Mugeuk suddenly asked.

“Do you still dream these days?”

“What dream?”

“The dream where you become the successor.”

Now that he thought about it, he had not had that dream lately. At first, he had dreamed it so often.

“I dream it every day.”

Geom Muyang finally realized that the dream had left him. No matter how busy he was, it was not something he would simply forget. Somehow, he felt both relieved and disappointed.

“I will dream it even the day before I die of old age.”

“How unsightly.”

“I will dream it even after I am in my grave.”

Perhaps he found his own words funny, for Geom Muyang gave a faint smile.

Geom Mugeuk could sense that his brother had let it go.

“If you keep dreaming, it may become reality someday. How can anyone know what will happen?”

Geom Muyang, who had been walking beside him, stopped and shouted with an angry face.

“Do not even dream it!”

Very occasionally, it occurred to him. The sight of Geom Mugeuk handing the Heavenly Demon’s position over to him and leaving. The more he came to know about his younger brother, the clearer that scene became.

“Why are you trying to awaken my dream?”

Geom Muyang deliberately looked at his younger brother even more coldly. This point had to be made clear.

“If you have taken it once, you carry it to the end. I will not forgive you for yielding it. Understood?”

“Understood. I will not yield it. Do you take me for a fool? Why would I yield something so good?”

The two of them started walking again. They did not say a word until they arrived at Pungryu Tavern.

At that moment, Chef Wang was admiring the food.

It tasted better than he had expected. It seemed ordinary, yet there was a deep flavor to it. It was a taste anyone could enjoy without strong likes or dislikes, and yet it had a distinctive flavor of its own.

“How did you make this broth?”

“It is pork broth, with secret ingredients added. Even if you are from the cult, I cannot tell you the secret ingredients.”

“That is all right.”

This elderly man had said he was from the cult and that he wanted to taste the food here before the meeting.

Having run his business here for a long time, Jo Chunbae knew better than anyone how the Divine Cult handled affairs. It was only natural that someone would come out to inspect.

After taking another sip of the soup, Chef Wang was greatly moved.

“It tastes different from the first sip.”

“Oh my, no. Not at all.”

Jo Chunbae waved his hands in humility, but his shoulders had risen as high as the corners of his mouth.

“Ah, then please try this as well.”

This is what the Cult Leader ate too!

Chef Wang tried the dish he recommended and looked very satisfied.

“This is good too. This suits my taste best.”

At that, Jo Chunbae casually said,

“Shall I pack some for you?”

“Would you do that?”

“To tell the truth, ever since I was young, I was often told I was born with a good touch for cooking. My tongue is sensitive as well, so I understand flavors. I do not know if you know this, but cooking, like martial arts, is something that requires talent.”

“Sometime, I would like to bring the chefs of the Divine Cult here and let them taste it. To show that even with such common ingredients, one can create a deep flavor.”

“Oh my, what are you saying? Not at all, not at all.”

Jo Chunbae waved his hands again. He was so pleased that his hand-waving was cheerful enough to turn into butterflies and fly off.

Jo Chunbae, who had been in high spirits, only then noticed that Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang were standing at the entrance, watching them.

“Ah, Young Cult Leader. First Young Master! When did you arrive?”

Jo Chunbae respectfully greeted the two.

“This guest here is someone who came from our cult for a preliminary inspection.”

Geom Muyang greeted Chef Wang first.

“Chef Wang.”

“First Young Master.”

At the words Chef Wang, Jo Chunbae flinched in surprise. Chef? This man was a chef? Chef Wang?

Having run his business here for so long, how could he not have heard stories about Chef Wang? Surely not? That Chef Wang?

Jo Chunbae asked Geom Mugeuk in a trembling voice.

“Do you mean this person is? No, surely not?”

Geom Mugeuk said with a smile,

“That is right. This is the head chef of the Cult Leader’s Hall.”

Jo Chunbae, startled out of his wits, nearly jumped. The butterflies that had taken flight all came crashing down.

“Oh dear!”

Not just any chef, but the head chef of the Cult Leader’s Hall—and he had bragged about how to make seasonings, how to simmer broth, and all sorts of things.

“This foolish man has swung a stick before the Sword God and lectured on martial learning.”

At that, Chef Wang shook his head.

“At times, that stick can be sharper than the Sword God’s sword.”

They said truly delicious restaurants were hidden in unexpected places, and Pungryu Tavern was such a place. Even when Geom Mugeuk had said it was delicious, he had inwardly looked down on it, thinking, how delicious could it be?

Chef Wang said to Geom Mugeuk,

“I am someone who believes there is fate even between food and people. This taste must have played a part in the fate between this tavern and the Young Cult Leader.”

It was the moment Jo Chunbae received recognition from the chef of the Cult Leader’s Hall.

“I ate well.”

“It was an honor, Chef!”

Before leaving, Chef Wang took all the food Jo Chunbae had packed for him. That sight alone moved Jo Chunbae.

“Please bring those delicious dishes to the second floor.”

Leaving him steeped in emotion, Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang went up to the second floor.

Geom Muyang sent a voice transmission.

—It is not that delicious.

—Why send it by voice transmission? Be wicked and say it aloud in front of the Proprietor.

—He is so moved right now that I doubt he would hear me even if I said it.

Jo Chunbae was endlessly staring at Chef Wang’s departing back.

“Sit over here, Brother. This spot has a good view of outside.”

Geom Mugeuk yielded the good seat.

“Since you will not let me yield the Taishi chair, I should at least yield this seat.”

Brother, do you know? To me, that seat is as precious as the Taishi chair.

* * *

When Jin Hagun came out of the conference room, Jin Haryeong was waiting.

“That took a long time.”

“They are in the middle of a pride contest.”

At Jin Hagun’s words, Jin Haryeong asked,

“What pride?”

“They are agonizing over the number of guards compared to the Unorthodox Alliance.”

“To bring more?”

At that, Jin Ha-gun shook his head.

“To take even one fewer person.”

Those words made Jin Ha-ryeong laugh. He was worried that the other side would think him frightened if the number of guards was too high.

“You worry about the strangest things.”

“Because it’s an important matter to them. But why?”

“I heard you removed me from the summit roster?”

“Yeah, I took you off.”

“Why?”

“Because we can’t both go.”

Jin Ha-ryeong made an expression of disbelief.

“So that if something unexpected happens, one of us will survive?”

Jin Ha-gun nodded.

“Then go in and persuade him. If you can’t, big brother will be the one left behind.”

He was Jin Ha-gun, who knew his younger sister’s temperament better than anyone. He also knew that she truly wanted to see Geom Mugeuk.

“I was already in the middle of persuading him.”

“Then I came here for nothing. For not trusting the best big brother in the world.”

“Am I truly the best?”

Jin Ha-gun went back inside.

Jin Ha-ryeong smiled faintly and looked up at the sky beyond the corridor window.

Though the drifting clouds were so slow they seemed frozen, time was flowing fast.

* * *

Until the moment of departure, Baek Ja-gang remained in his private training ground.

After the date of the assembly was set, he had stayed only there, like a man facing war.

Today as well, he was glaring at the iron dummy.

In his eyes now, Geom Woo-jin stood superimposed over the iron dummy.

He still stared at him with arrogant eyes.

Then someone spoke from outside.

“It is Sa-in. The departure time has come.”

“Enter.”

Into the room came Bi Sa-in.

“Do you know who stands there?”

Bi Sa-in looked at the iron dummy. Superimposed over it, he could see the figure of Geom Mugeuk.

“Who stands there?”

“The Demonic Cult Leader. Exactly as he appeared ten years ago when I met him. He has been standing there ever since the trilateral talks were decided.”

Bi Sa-in looked at the iron dummy together with his master, then suddenly asked.

“Did you cut him down?”

For a moment, Baek Ja-gang was silent. The silence was long, but Bi Sa-in did not think he had made a mistake. His master had clearly been waiting for that question.

“I could not cut him.”

Bi Sa-in liked this about his master. He did not hide the truth to save face or protect his pride.

“Here, I understood why I had resolved to meet him.”

“What was the reason?”

“He loomed too large in my heart.”

Bi Sa-in could guess what those words meant.

“This time, when you meet him, he will become smaller.”

One unease arose in Baek Ja-gang’s heart.

“What if he is a greater man than I have grown to be?”

Had he ever seen such a frank side of his master? Was it Geom Mugeuk who had changed him, or was it Geom Woo-jin?

“Then perhaps you will understand my feelings, even if only a little. The stifling feelings of a disciple who watches his sworn enemy grow larger every time he sees him.”

Baek Ja-gang laughed without a sound.

Bi Sa-in felt pleased to see his master laugh. Each time he smiled, it felt as though he took one step closer. Even if it was an illusion, it was welcome.

Soon after, attendants entered with clothes. They were the splendid garments worn by the Saber Path Alliance Leader during an official procession outside.

But Baek Ja-gang refused all of them.

Recalling the time when he first entered the Saber Path, he put on a clean black martial uniform.

The black martial uniform suited him perfectly, and it carried more dignity than the splendid attire.

“Let us go. To the Demonic Cult.”

Absolute Regression

Chapter 385: Today, the Unit Leaders Are the Youngest.
------------------------------------------------------
At last, the day of the talks dawned.

Jo Chunbae began preparing for business earlier than usual, wrote a notice on a sheet of paper, and pasted it prominently at the entrance.

Closed Today (今日休業).

Looking at the words he had posted, Jo Chunbae took a deep breath. At last, today was the day! You can do this, Chunbae.

“Is something going on today?”

Mr. Yang from the drapery shop asked worriedly.

“There’s a gathering of the Divine Cult at the tavern today.”

He had kept it secret until today, but at last he was able to reveal it.

Naturally, Mr. Yang was startled.

“A Divine Cult gathering is being held at your tavern?”

“That’s right.”

“Congratulations. Truly, congratulations.”

Everyone knew Jo Chunbae was on close terms with the Young Cult Leader, and that not only the Demon Venerables of the Divine Cult but even the Heavenly Demon himself had visited the tavern, so the fact that a gathering was being held there was not unbelievable.

“May I come take a look later?”

“If you had the courage for that, you’d have lived your life wearing those martial uniforms you sell.”

Mr. Yang burst into hearty laughter, saying he was right.

“Do well.”

“Thank you.”

Songi, who helped him in the kitchen, also arrived earlier than usual. In fact, there wasn’t much to prepare. Jo Chunbae had already gotten everything ready in advance. The dishes could go out the moment an order came in.

“Master, aren’t you scared?”

Songi was thoroughly frightened by the gathering that would take place today.

“What’s there to be scared of?”

“Won’t this place be packed with demonic martial artists today?”

“Don’t be afraid. We have the Young Cult Leader with us. The most reassuring person in the world is on our side, so what is there to fear? And it’s only half-baked nobodies who torment people. The true masters are refined.”

After reassuring Songi, Jo Chunbae went out through the back door of the kitchen.

In truth, Jo Chunbae was just as nervous. There was a world of difference between serving someone who dropped by unexpectedly and serving people after preparing like this.

Just then, an old man entered through the back door.

“Elder, we’re not open for business today. And you can’t come in this way.”

“Are you the owner of this place?”

“I am, but…”

“I have something to ask. My legs ache, so let me rest for just a moment.”

The old man sat down on the small wooden platform in the backyard.

Judging by the wrinkles on his face, he was quite old, but his build was very sturdy. His two fierce, tiger-like eyes made one think he must have had quite the temper in his youth.

Aside from that, he was ordinary. Jo Chunbae studied the old man with the intuition and eye for people he had gained through long years in business, but he did not look like a martial artist. Nor did he look like a bad person.

“I haven’t seen you around before.”

“I came from Hubei.”

“Goodness, you’ve come from far away. What brings you all the way here?”

“I came to meet someone.”

“At your age, you must have had a hard journey. Shall I bring you a cup of water?”

“I’m fine.”

“Who did you come all the way here to meet?”

“An enemy, if you call him an enemy. And someone close, if you call him close.”

At that, Jo Chunbae asked carefully.

“Did you perhaps come to see your child?”

In that instant, the old man’s face hardened, and an unmistakable shadow fell over it.

Jo Chunbae thought his guess had been right. Such cases were not uncommon. Old folks who had lived apart from their children, then traveled a long road with the thought that they might at least see the faces of their children and grandchildren before they died.

“How long has it been since you last saw them?”

“Must be more than ten years.”

Jo Chunbae felt sorry for him inwardly. Hadn’t he himself just gone through a major incident involving family?

“Where are you supposed to meet?”

“We agreed to meet at this tavern.”

“Oh dear! Of all days, you had to choose today? The tavern is closed today. What time were you supposed to meet?”

“There’s still time left, but I came early. I was also curious what scheme he had in mind, choosing this place as the meeting spot.”

Just from the word scheme, Jo Chunbae could tell. This hot-tempered old man did not have a good relationship with his child.

“Make peace, even now.”

“Should I?”

“Running a business here, I see all sorts of people. They hate each other enough to die and fight. But later, when you look, they all regret it. They say all they had to do was endure for one moment, be just a little more generous—why did they cling to their pride so much? Aren’t you and I past the age of keeping our edges sharp? No, we’re at the age when we ought to throw away blades altogether.”

The old man silently stared at Jo Chunbae.

“If you keep saying later, later, it becomes too late. If you see him, Elder, you should be the first to say you were wrong. Isn’t that what an adult does? Temper yourself a bit.”

“Why do you think I have a temper?”

“People like us can tell just by looking at a face. You had quite the temper in your younger days, didn’t you?”

The old man gave a strange smile.

“I did.”

“I’m someone with an unerring eye for people.”

For a moment, Cook Wang, whom he had completely failed to recognize, flashed through his mind, but he quickly brushed it aside.

“Anyway, we’re not open today, so wait at the inn down below. If anyone comes looking for you, I’ll make sure to tell them to go there.”

Given the atmosphere today, even conveying those words would not be easy. Should he ask one of the demonic martial artists guarding the entrance? For some reason, he worried the old man and his child might miss each other and never meet again.

Just then, the old man suddenly asked.

“I hear the main altar of the Demon Cult is near here. Have you ever seen the Heavenly Demon?”

Has this old man gone mad?

“How dare you put someone like that in your mouth? If you speak so carelessly in Maga Village, you won’t die peacefully.”

“My apologies.”

He considered throwing him out right away, but since the old man had come from far away to meet his child, he felt sorry for him and answered.

“I’ve seen him. The Cult Leader came to our tavern as well.”

“What kind of person is he? Is he as frightening as the rumors in the murim say?”

“He may be frightening to the orthodox and unorthodox factions, but to us, he is a benevolent man. And he is the person I respect most.”

“Because he protects you from the orthodox and unorthodox factions?”

“Of course, that is one reason, but…”

Jo Chunbae gave a surprising reason.

“I like him because he is the Young Cult Leader’s father.”

It was the first time Jo Chunbae had said such a thing. He had only just realized it himself. That he had been thinking this way.

‘But this old man—why is he looking at me as if he understands? What do you know about the Young Cult Leader?’

Just then, Songi’s voice came from inside.

“Master, a guest is here!”

“I’m coming.”

Jo Chunbae stood up.

“Go on, quickly. If you loiter around here today for no reason, you could be in serious trouble, so wait at the inn. Understand?”

“Thank you. I’ll see you later.”

“I’m telling you, you won’t see me today!”

Jo Chunbae hurried into the building.

The guest who had come to the tavern was an unexpected person.

“Huh? My lord.”

It was the Demon Army Lord Jang Ho. Unlike when he usually came to drink in ordinary martial attire, today he wore the Demon Army’s formal uniform.

When Jo Chunbae saw the terrifying evil-ghost pattern drawn on his chest, a fearful thought came to him naturally: Ah, this man really was the leader of that terrifying Demon Army.

“Lord Jang, what brings you here?”

“I came to arrange the seats for today’s meeting.”

“Just tell me what to do. I’ll handle it.”

“No, I’ll do it myself.”

Jang Ho went up to the second floor.

There, he arranged the tables and chairs so that three people could sit in a triangular formation, facing one another.

Jo Chunbae found it strange inwardly.

‘Are three people coming to today’s gathering?’

He had thought it would be a gathering with many people, but apparently not. But it was only a gathering of three people, and yet Cook Wang had come to inspect the food, and the Demon Army Lord himself had come to arrange the seats?

‘Gasp! Could it be? Is the Cult Leader coming?’

But he soon shook his head. If that were the case, they would not have arranged the seats like that. They would have placed the Cult Leader’s seat at the head. Right now, all three seats faced one another equally.

“But why are you doing it personally, my lord? Why not have your subordinates do it?”

“My subordinates will not be coming here today.”

Jo Chunbae did not know. Today, no martial artist below the rank of dan leader could come to Pungryu Tavern. Today, the dan leaders were the youngest ones here.

The dan leaders of each organization were stationed throughout the marketplace like ordinary martial artists.

“No one else will be allowed to enter the tavern.”

Jo Chunbae was about to mention the old man, then stopped. In any case, he had already come and gone. If he mentioned him for no reason and the old man was dragged off by the demonic martial artists for questioning, he would not be able to meet his child.

Just then, someone entered the tavern.

Astonishingly, it was the Fist Demon. When Jo Chunbae saw that terrifying face, his heart began to pound.

‘It seems Lord Fist Demon is also attending the gathering.’

The Fist Demon and Jang Ho exchanged greetings.

With the two of them standing side by side, it felt as if the entire inn had been filled. Moreover, weren’t these the two most frightening-looking men in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult? When the two imposing figures met, Jo Chunbae did not even dare look at them.

After exchanging a brief conversation with Jang Ho, the Fist Demon approached Jo Chunbae.

‘Ah, I should have gone into the kitchen earlier.’

The Fist Demon came right up to him and asked an unexpected question.

“Is the food preparation proceeding without issue?”

The Fist Demon was asking him about food preparation?

Jo Chunbae looked at the Fist Demon with a startled expression, then hastily lowered his head. His face was too frightening to look at directly.

“There is no need to be surprised. I am in charge of security for today’s talks.”

The Fist Demon was in charge of security? He was not attending as one of the main figures in today’s gathering? Why?

“There is no problem with the food preparation. Whenever an order is given, we can serve it at once.”

“Before the food is brought out, a martial artist of our Cult will come and inspect it for poison. It is not because we suspect you, but because it is part of procedure, so please understand.”

“Of course.”

Jo Chunbae went into the kitchen. He already felt as though the strength had gone out of his legs.

Why would a Demon Venerable, especially the terrifying Fist Demon, explain every little thing like this to a mere tavern owner? Normally, he would not have. He never would have. If not for the Young Cult Leader, that is.

Jo Chunbae was looking down into the broth bubbling away when it happened.

Another Demon Venerable entered the tavern.

It was the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon, carrying a great saber on his back. His attire, too, was different from usual.

He wore a red changshan symbolizing a blood-colored sky. The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon now was not the same Blood-Heaven Blade Demon who had read poetry and joked around with Geom Mugeuk.

A frost-like chill rose from his sharp, unyielding eyes.

Jo Chunbae felt as though he could not breathe and quickly turned his head to trim vegetables that did not need trimming.

Songi asked with her eyes.

‘You’re still saying you’re not scared?’

I’m scared. Truly scared.

But because of his pride, he could not bring himself to say it aloud.

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon asked the Fist Demon.

“Have you seen the Young Cult Leader?”

“He stopped by the Cult Leader’s Hall.”

“I see.”

The Blood-Heaven Blade Demon slowly moved his feet and went outside. The Heaven-Destroying Great Saber on his back gleamed more coldly than ever.

After seeing the Fist Demon and then even the Blood-Heaven Blade Demon, Jo Chunbae felt immense pressure. The aura of the Demon Venerables today was different from usual. They spoke calmly and merely stood quietly, yet somehow the feeling was different.

‘Phew, get a hold of yourself. For the Young Cult Leader’s sake, you have to get a hold of yourself.’

He opened the small window in the kitchen that faced the main street and took a deep breath.

As he looked up at the sky and steadied his heart, he saw someone.

Someone was standing with arms crossed on the roof of the building across the street. There was no mistaking who it was. The white mask was shining as sunlight struck it.

Was it because the sight of him standing alone, relying on a single mask, looked lonely? Or was it because the cherry blossom petals, carried by the spring breeze as they brushed past the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon, were beautiful? Jo Chunbae stared at him as if bewitched.

Just then, the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon’s gaze slid toward Jo Chunbae.

‘Gasp!’

Startled out of his wits, Jo Chunbae hurriedly hid himself below the window. Had their eyes met?

When he cautiously raised his head and looked over there again, the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon was no longer in that spot.

He let out a sigh and said,

“Phew. Lord Smiling Demon is the scariest person after Lord Poison King.”

When Songi gave no answer, Jo Chunbae turned around without thinking.

‘Urgh!’

He was even more startled than before.

He did not know when he had come in, but the Poison King was crouched beside Songi, staring at a snail-like bug crawling among the vegetables she had been trimming. Songi was frozen in place, her hand covering her mouth.

The Poison King, too, was dressed formally. Wearing a changshan that elegantly blended green and white, he had become a handsome young nobleman who drew the eye at a single glance.

Even in her shock, Songi found herself thinking that the drawings of the twelve zodiac animals on the poison pouch at the Poison King’s waist were cute. If one knew what was inside it, one would imagine the eyes of those adorable animals turning blood-red as they transformed into monsters and sprang out.

The Poison King placed the insect on a blade of grass and carried it outside.

Once he was gone, Jo Chunbae let out the breath he had been holding.

“I’ll admit it. I’m scared too.”

At that, Songi looked toward where the Poison King had left and said,

“No, he’s not scary.”

“What?”

“He’s amazing.”

Jo Chunbae shook his head.

Just then, he sensed someone entering the tavern. From the kitchen, Jo Chunbae raised his head slightly to peer through the small window that looked outside, checking who had come in.

The one who entered was Ilhwa Sword Venerable, dressed in pure white martial robes. On a day like this, she might have put on makeup for the first time in a while, but she had come barefaced. The traces of time were naturally revealed.

Moreover, her face was tanned from grueling training, standing in contrast to her snow-white martial robes.

Even so, Jo Chunbae found her present appearance more beautiful than when she had once adorned herself with elaborate makeup. He wanted to go and tell her so. That she looked even more magnificent and elegant now. Of course, he did not dare.

There was someone who said it in his stead.

“You’re the most beautiful Sword Venerable I’ve ever seen.”

When she turned around, the Drunken Demon stepped inside.

He, too, was properly dressed today. On the clothes he wore was an image of a drunken Wine God laughing. Among the many garments painted with the Wine God, one might have expected him to wear the one with a terrifying, rampaging Wine God on a day like this.

“I hear spring winds have been blowing through Chwimongru lately?”

“For someone who will one day become the Cult Leader, he certainly has a loose tongue.”

The Drunken Demon smiled faintly, opened the stopper of his wine bottle, took a deep sniff, and closed it again.

‘Is today so important that even the Drunken Demon is holding back from drinking?’

Just as Jo Chunbae was thinking that, golden light poured in from outside the tavern. The small giant, the Demon Buddha, had arrived, revealing a presence that could not be concealed.

On the kasaya draped over him were images of humans and evil spirits suffering in hellfire, and it looked as though the golden radiance he emitted had become flames, burning them all away.

In addition, the Soul-Seizing Demon Venerable appeared as though she had seeped into view, like someone who had been there from the very beginning. In her two eyes, which had grown even more mature in the time he had not seen her, an eerie ghostly aura flowed. She was walking the narrow path between human and ghost.

It was Jo Chunbae’s first time seeing all of the Eight Demon Venerables gathered in one place. With all of them properly dressed and assembled together, it was truly a magnificent sight.

The fact that they had gathered like this in a marketplace that did not suit them made it an even more unforgettable scene.

If he had been a painter, he would surely have painted this moment.

‘So it really was a meeting of the Eight Demon Venerables!’

Then, a question naturally arose.

Jo Chunbae’s gaze turned toward the second floor. If all the Demon Venerables had gathered like this, why were there only three seats?

‘And why is no one sitting there?’

Absolute Regression

Chapter 386: If I Had Known the Cult Leader Was Coming
------------------------------------------------------
Jo Chunbae gazed once more at the Eight Demon Lords standing in the street before the tavern.

Blood Heaven Blade Demon stood tall at the center, his great dao thrust into the ground, while One Flower Sword Venerable and Drunken Demon stood to either side conversing.

Nearby, Poison King crouched looking at something, and Demon Buddha looked down at it with him.

Extreme Evil Young Demon sat perched upon the edge of the roof. He had always preferred solitude and high places.

Soul-Seizing Demon Lord looked up at that Extreme Evil Young Demon. Perhaps because she was young and could not easily mingle with the other Demon Lords, there seemed to be a sense of kinship in her eyes.

Fist Demon, who was responsible for today's security, gazed down the street with his two massive fists hanging at his sides. He wore the gauntlets that the Young Cult Leader had given him as a gift.

The image of the eight Demon Lords guarding the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was engraved upon Jo Chunbae's heart like a painting, one he would proudly boast of to guests for years to come.

Those looking at the Eight Demon Lords were not only Jo Chunbae. The merchants of the street watched them, and standing among them were the Danju.

It was the first time for the Danju as well. All of the Danju mobilized to stand guard like ordinary martial artists—they couldn't even remember the last time they had taken a shift.

Suddenly, longing surged in Jo Chunbae's heart. For the one person who had made this sight possible.

‘Young Cult Leader, where are you? I wish to see you.’

*

Father had chosen the most splendid outfit to wear for today's meeting.

The highlight of the outfit was on the back. There, the back of the Heavenly Demon Soul was depicted, standing turned away.

An outfit where, when Father walked, it looked as though the Heavenly Demon Soul was walking as well. It was truly an outfit that declared, “I am the Heavenly Demon, Geom Ujin.”

I met Father's eyes in the mirror as he changed his clothes.

“Today you look the most magnificent of all.”

Today's protagonist is neither me, nor Jo Chunbae, nor the Murim Alliance Leader, nor the Four Paths Alliance Leader.

The protagonist of today, in my mind, is Father.

But it seems Father believes that protagonist is me.

“Now, I've gathered everyone as you wished. What do you intend to do now?”

“My wish?”

“Then was it mine?”

“Of course. Why else would the two Alliance Leaders come? They came because they wished to see Father.”

Father in the mirror looked at me and sneered. Father, please look at your own expression just once. Is it acceptable to sneer so openly at someone?

Of course, I love this sneer of Father's all too much.

Because it was both a moment when my banter worked and Father's answer.

This sneer could be interpreted in many ways. Sometimes it meant, “You rascal,” and other times, “Is that so?” or “Don't be absurd.” And right now, it meant, “You shameless brat.”

Father turned to face me.

“Let's go. If we've invited guests, we must arrive first and wait.”

Today, for once, I followed quietly behind Father without dropping any banter.

The terrifying back of the Heavenly Demon Soul drawn upon his back.

Watching it resemble Father's back, I grew curious about my own Heavenly Demon Soul.

When the Nine Splendors Demon Art reaches great accomplishment, what form will my Heavenly Demon Soul take?

My older brother, Geom Muyang, was waiting before the residence.

The gaze Father directed toward Brother was gentle. On a day like today, walking out together must make him proud.

“Father, please look at me with the same eyes you show Brother right now.”

Without answering, Father strode forward. Brother and I followed from left and right.

“Father, favoring the eldest son like this is too much.”

Naturally, I thought he would ignore my words.

“When your brother was young, he once had a fever.”

Brother and I were both startled. It was the first time Father had ever spoken of such a thing.

“At the time, the Demonic Physician was away on business outside. I held your brother and ran to her using the Heavenly Demon Flight Art.”

I wanted to crack a joke—So that's when Brother became strange!—but I held back. Because Brother was listening to Father with a look of such surprise and tension. If even I am this astonished, how must Brother feel?

“Fortunately, the fever broke quickly. The Demonic Physician laughed. Said it wasn't serious enough to rush over like that.”

Even this perfect person had moments of clumsiness. Before a sick child, he was not the Heavenly Demon but a father. These were words I would never have heard if I had not regressed and changed our relationship.

Father added one more thing.

“That is why I am sorry as well.”

Because it is the first child who makes one go through all manner of trials and errors in raising them.

Brother walked quietly behind Father without saying a word. I could feel him suppressing surging emotions. I thought that Brother would probably never forget these words Father spoke today.

Yes, the fact that Brother has endured this well is thanks to Father.

“What about me? Didn't I ever have a fever?”

“You did. You burned even hotter.”

“Then you must have run even faster.”

A faint smile formed at the corners of Father's lips. At least this smile was not a sneer, but a smile recalling that time long ago.

“I stripped you naked and wiped you down with lukewarm water to bring the fever down.”

“How could you!”

Was it Father walking ahead who laughed? I had the illusion that the Heavenly Demon Soul on his back was smiling.

All the way to Magachon, I kept picturing Father running while holding Brother, and Father soaking a cloth in lukewarm water to wipe my body and bring down my fever.

Yes, we have come this far. With each step we draw closer, we will hear more stories.

In the distance, the Pungnyu Tavern came into view.

*

Extreme Evil Young Demon, who had been on the roof, lightly leaped down to the ground.

Poison King, who had been crouching lost in thought, sprang to his feet.

Those two, who seemed least likely to emerge from their own worlds, moved first, and the remaining Demon Lords split to either side and looked toward the end of the street.

Jo Chunbae couldn't contain his curiosity. So he came out of the kitchen and poked his head out slightly.

‘Good heavens!’

Geom Ujin was walking from the end of the street. Behind him, the Young Cult Leader and the Great Young Lord fanned out to either side like wings as they followed.

‘So the Cult Leader really was attending!’

Startled, Jo Chunbae froze on the spot. He wished someone would carry his statue-like body back into the kitchen.

His feet wouldn't budge because, having seen the Cult Leader approaching, he couldn't simply slip back inside.

As Geom Ujin drew near, the Demon Lords all cupped their fists in salute at once.

“We greet the Cult Leader.”

Their voices united and rang out mightily. The merchants who had been watching outside had already gone into their buildings.

On the street, the Danju stood in a line at fixed intervals with their heads bowed, and at their head was Jang Ho.

“Rest easy, all of you.”

Geom Ujin's voice was low yet weighty, echoing to the end of the street. The Danju who had been bowing all raised their heads and presented stalwart appearances.

“Now, let us enter.”

Geom Ujin took the lead and went in, and the Demon Lords followed after him.

Jo Chunbae was prostrated flat on the ground beside the entrance.

Geom Ujin recognized him.

“Rise.”

“If I had known the Cult Leader was coming, I would have prepared with even greater care.”

Then from behind, Geom Mugeuk remarked lightly, “So you are saying you did not do your best? Innkeeper, there may be only one father, but here there are eight of us.”

Flustered, Jo Chunbae habitually waved his hands.

“No, that's not what I meant.”

Then he flinched in surprise and bowed his head again. Waving his hands before the Cult Leader—he had committed an act that would not be strange at all if someone said, “Someone should cut off that rude hand!”

Geom Ujin turned to look at Geom Mugeuk with an exasperated look, as if to say, “Must you tease the innkeeper even now?”

Seizing the opportunity, the Demon Lords all looked at Geom Mugeuk as well.

A barrage of accusatory gazes.

“No, I was just trying to ease the tension. You people are too much! You toss me aside before Father without even taking a breath!”

Of course, as if that were true.

Blood Heaven Blade Demon's frosty gaze had been saying, “You brat!” all along, and Extreme Evil Young Demon's eyes behind his mask were smiling.

Drunken Demon's eyes seemed to ask, “Did you already blab about what happened with Yeobin?” Poison King's eyes, which never retreated into his own world in front of Father, Fist Demon's stern eyes saying, “No pranks today,” Soul-Seizing Demon Lord's eyes as the vanguard of the next generation's Demon Lords, One Flower Sword Venerable's soft yet intense gaze, and Demon Buddha's unwavering eyes fixed on Brother instead of me.

Fist Demon guided Geom Ujin to the upper floor.

“Please go up.”

Geom Ujin took one of the three seats on the upper floor.

Watching Fist Demon come back down, Jo Chunbae was inwardly puzzled.

‘Why isn't anyone going up? If not the Demon Lords, who else are they meeting?’

That the other party might be the Murim Alliance Leader or the Four Paths Alliance Leader was unthinkable from the start. How could one even imagine that such a momentous meeting would take place at his tavern?

The Demon Lords stood on the first floor. None of them sat down. Moreover, they did not scatter but gathered on one side.

‘Just who is coming?’

Just then, one person entered the tavern. When Jo Chunbae saw who entered, he was shocked. It was the old man he had seen that morning.

He, who was near the entrance, hurriedly ran to the old man.

“Elder, why did you come here when I told you today wouldn't work? Hurry and leave, please hurry.”

Surprised gazes all turned to Jo Chunbae.

Jo Chunbae looked around at the nearby Demon Lords and said,

“No, no. He is someone who had an appointment at our tavern today. There was a brief misunderstanding.”

And he quickly whispered into the old man's ear.

“Please hurry and leave. Everyone here is someone frightening.”

Only then did Jo Chunbae see the martial artists lined up behind the old man. They were looking at him with cold eyes.

“Oh!”

Startled, Jo Chunbae stumbled backward.

Jo Chunbae had seen all kinds of martial artists while running a tavern. But the people lined up behind the old man were not mere experts.

An overwhelming presence—the same crushing sense of existence he felt when looking at the Demon Lords—radiated from them.

Jo Chunbae looked at the old man again in surprise.

The old man's previously ordinary clothes were now splendid and luxurious. Upon his chest, the two characters “Righteous Hero” were entwined by an ascending blue dragon.

Not only had his clothes changed; his atmosphere was different as well. From the old man emanated an absolute dignity and bearing that overwhelmed even the experts behind him, and his fierce, round eyes were truly the eyes of a tiger.

“Didn't I say we would meet later?”

Jo Chunbae answered with a trembling voice.

“So you are a guest attending today's meeting.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been observing, stepped forward and said,

“He is the Murim Alliance Leader.”

Jo Chunbae's eyes grew wider and wider. The words “Murim Alliance Leader” sounded so foreign. The Murim Alliance Leader? The leader of the orthodox Murim Alliance? Why is the Murim Alliance Leader here? Is there a war? Is this a dream?

“I invited the Alliance Leader to Pungnyu Tavern.”

The astonished Jo Chunbae stood with his mouth agape, unable to speak.

Geom Mugeuk slowly walked forward to greet Jin Paechon first.

“Thank you for coming all this way.”

“Not at all. It's been a while since I've been to this region, so the journey was quite pleasant.”

“This is the innkeeper of this place I mentioned beforehand.”

Jo Chunbae's heart pounded as if it would burst. He had mentioned him to the Murim Alliance Leader? And now he was introducing him?

“I'm counting on your finest performance, Innkeeper.”

Jo Chunbae nodded his head vigorously.

Jin Paechon said to Geom Mugeuk, “When I met the innkeeper earlier, I could see why you chose this tavern.”

He was the Murim Alliance Leader who had been scolded by Jo Chunbae. Telling him not to be so sharp and to go apologize first, since he was the elder.

“Once you've had the alcohol and the food, you will understand completely.”

Jin Paechon went up to the upper floor. He declined when Fist Demon offered to guide him and went up alone.

Among the martial artists who came with him were Jin Hagun and Jin Haryeong.

I wanted to banter with them, but since it was an official meeting, I merely cupped my fists in greeting without words.

Jin Haryeong in particular brightened upon seeing me. There was a fluttering excitement that only I could evoke.

In this world, who could stand in front of the Murim Alliance Leader just to introduce a tavern keeper?

Behind them were seven elite experts selected from the Murim Alliance.

There was an old expert with disheveled white hair, and a young expert who appeared to be in his thirties. They were the greatest experts of the past and present generations of the orthodox faction whose names were known to all.

They were people who could be honest with their emotions even before the Demon Lords. One of them openly displayed an expression of displeasure.

Great Righteous Hero Im Sanggon.

He was displeased that I had looked after Jo Chunbae before the Murim Alliance Leader earlier. Introducing some tavern innkeeper? As if they weren't being treated with contempt.

When he glared at me, a cold gaze flew toward him.

It was Blood Heaven Blade Demon. The clear warning in his eyes.

How dare you send such a look to the Young Cult Leader?

Then the old man beside Great Righteous Hero glared at Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

Taiyi Divine Sword Jin Gwang.

A sword expert who had dominated the previous generation's martial world with a single blade.

Filthy demonic cult bastards.

He too revealed his emotions plainly on his face.

Then this time, One Flower Sword Venerable looked at him coldly.

Because both were people walking toward the pinnacle of the sword, they could not help but be naturally conscious of one another.

Jo Chunbae was not watching this silent war of nerves.

He watched, as if entranced, Jin Paechon slowly walking up to the upper floor.

Even though Jin Paechon displayed no martial spirit whatsoever, the moment he stepped onto the upper floor, Jo Chunbae could feel the air of the first and second floors change.

“A meeting between the demonic and orthodox factions, at my tavern! At my tavern!”

Jo Chunbae was so happy he felt he might faint.

He took a deep breath to compose himself. Now was the time to display his greatest skill.

Jo Chunbae, who had been about to head to the kitchen, whipped around and looked up at the upper floor. The remaining empty seat caught his eye.

“Huh? Then what is that seat for?”

Chapter 387: We're Not on Smiling Terms
---------------------------------------
When he came up the stairs, he saw the Heavenly Demon, Geom Ujin, ahead of him.

Jin Paecheon felt his heart stir for the first time in ages.

‘How long has it been since I felt this excitement?’

Geom Ujin had risen from his seat and was waiting for him.

Demon and orthodox, orthodox and demon.

The leaders of two camps that had fought endlessly through the long years.

The two men stared at each other in silence. It was their first reunion in more than ten years. Normally, they should have exchanged formal greetings first, but neither of them said a word. No greeting would come from their mouths.

Jin Paecheon’s first words broke the silence.

“Your eyes are just the same.”

Only after meeting Geom Ujin again like this did Jin Paecheon truly realize it. How much he had hated this man. How deeply he hated the Demon Cult. The Geom Ujin in his imagination had been a beautified Geom Ujin.

A vast hostility surged up in him, so great that he wondered where so much hatred had been hiding.

The self-control he had cultivated over many years of meeting countless people collapsed in an instant the moment he saw those cold, arrogant eyes of Geom Ujin’s.

This man’s gaze was unchanged. His ambition to swallow the murim was unchanged as well.

Jin Paecheon’s heart turned cold.

This time, Geom Ujin spoke calmly.

“You have not changed either.”

The word “you” grated on him, and the words “not changed” grated on him too. He could have interpreted it favorably, as “You have not aged at all,” but he did not.

Jin Paecheon realized anew that he, with his tyrannical nature, had been living with quite a lot of restraint lately.

As such thoughts took hold, the air around them naturally began to grow cold.

The taut tension was not flowing only between the two of them.

The first floor was the same.

Between the orthodox masters and the Demon Venerables, a tense battle of wills was also underway.

Geom Mugeuk stood before the line of Demon Venerables.

Jin Hagun stood before the line of orthodox masters.

Beside them was Jo Chunbae, who had not yet managed to reach the kitchen. He had been watching the Martial Alliance Leader go up to the second floor, but before he could enter the kitchen, the battle of wills on the first floor had already begun.

No one was moving, and in that stillness, he did not have the courage to be the only one to move and enter the kitchen.

The one fortunate thing was that no one was releasing qi pressure that would directly affect a person’s body. It truly was nothing more than a staring contest.

But the looks being exchanged were the sort that stabbed hearts and cut throats.

Someone might have spat out a single word that would become the seed of a fight, but fortunately, no one had opened their mouth yet.

Jin Haryeong saw it. Even in this hair-trigger situation, Geom Mugeuk was utterly at ease.

—Shouldn’t you stop them?

—How am I supposed to stop these people?

Then again, when she thought of the people who had come together this time, it was absurd that she had believed Geom Mugeuk would somehow be able to stop them.

Each and every one of them was a powerhouse capable of becoming the overlord of a region.

—It’ll be fine. I don’t think your grandfather brought anyone so reckless and stupid that they’d start a fight here.

Jin Haryeong looked at the masters standing beside her. She knew who they all were, of course, but she did not know them inside and out. Yes, her grandfather must have kept them in check.

If anything, she was worried about upstairs.

Her gaze turned back to the second floor. Still, no conversation could be heard.

Were they speaking through sound transmission? Even so, shouldn’t there at least be the usual greetings—welcome, have you been well, thank you for coming? How is the Alliance these days, is the Cult running smoothly? No matter how displeased they were with each other, weren’t those the minimum courtesies?

The second floor was silent, and the first floor was silent.

Tension hung in the air as if everything might explode at any moment.

It was then.

Jo Chunbae, who had been looking up at the second floor, felt a chill run down his back.

When he turned around, everyone inside was looking toward the entrance at once.

A man was standing there.

He was dressed in neat black martial attire. He slowly looked over the masters of the Demon Cult and the orthodox faction. His eyes were small, but their light was intense.

It was nothing more than a single glance. Yet the man’s presence swallowed up all the taut tension in the room.

Right behind him stood a young man whose appearance was frighteningly hideous, and behind him again stood nine martial artists in a row.

Taking the Eight Demon Venerables as the standard, the Martial Alliance had brought one fewer person, while the Evil Dao Alliance had brought one more.

Baek Jagang, a thorough realist, was not the sort of man to reduce his numbers out of pride. If anything, he would bring more.

Jo Chunbae could feel it. They, too, possessed a presence similar to that of the Martial Alliance masters who had entered earlier.

There was no sign whatsoever that they were intimidated by the Demon Venerables or by the Martial Alliance masters.

Jo Chunbae’s gaze returned to the man in black martial attire.

Judging by his clothing alone, he looked like an ordinary martial artist, but his presence overwhelmed everyone standing behind him. It was the moment when that plain black outfit devoured all the splendid garments around it.

Geom Mugeuk shouted loudly.

“The Evil Dao Alliance Leader has arrived!”

At the words “Evil Dao Alliance Leader,” Jo Chunbae let out a groan-like scream—“Ugh!”—then hurriedly covered his mouth with his hand.

He had thought there could be nothing more surprising today.

There was.

“You have had a hard journey coming all this way.”

Baek Jagang looked at Geom Mugeuk for a moment.

That Geom Mugeuk accounted for half the reason he had come here. He had been just as curious about how Geom Ujin had raised such a son as he was about whether Geom Ujin had truly abandoned his ambition to unify the murim.

And this matter was connected not merely to winning or losing at raising children, but also to the future of the Evil Dao Alliance. The brat before him had yet to be harvested. He still could not even guess what form that harvest would take.

Baek Jagang strode toward the stairs leading to the second floor.

The Fist Demon tried to guide him, but he refused as well.

Just as Baek Jagang was about to go up to the second floor, he stopped in front of Jo Chunbae, who was standing near the stairs with his head bowed.

‘Do not come here! Please, just go upstairs!’

Jo Chunbae was having an astonishing experience. After the Heavenly Demon, he was now standing before the Martial Alliance Leader, and then the Evil Dao Alliance Leader.

“Are you the owner of this place?”

At the deep, resonant voice, Jo Chunbae raised his head. It was truly pleasant to hear.

“I am.”

When he answered in a trembling voice, Baek Jagang’s gaze shifted from him to Geom Mugeuk.

“The Young Cult Leader spoke highly of you.”

Because Geom Mugeuk knew that Baek Jagang had said that out of consideration for him, he smiled at him. Baek Jagang also gave a faint smile with his eyes and went up to the second floor.

Indeed, people had to be seen often. The more he saw those small eyes, the more he kept thinking they were impressive.

Geom Mugeuk approached Jo Chunbae, who was standing there in a daze.

“That’s right. Today’s gathering is the first tripartite conference of this generation between the Cult Leader, the Martial Alliance Leader, and the Evil Dao Alliance Leader.”

It was the moment Jo Chunbae finally learned the true nature of today’s meeting. It was also the moment the owner of the final chair was decided.

‘A Demon-Orthodox-Evil conference? They decided to hold that conference in my tavern? The Cult Leader, the Martial Alliance Leader, and the Evil Dao Alliance Leader will sit in the chairs of my tavern? In our chairs!’

Jo Chunbae lifted his head with a bright expression, then flinched in surprise and lowered it again.

Everyone on the first floor was looking this way.

Those people, with whom he would never have met eyes even once in his life, were looking at him, a mere tavern owner.

Fear suddenly rose in him, and his entire body began to tremble.

“I think this place is more splendid than any other venue suited to a tripartite conference.”

Hearing Geom Mugeuk’s calm voice and seeing his gentle gaze, Jo Chunbae’s trembling subsided.

‘Yes. If not for the Young Cult Leader, I would already be dead. What is there to fear?’

Geom Mugeuk had said he had boasted of him to both the Martial Alliance Leader and the Evil Dao Alliance Leader. Jo Chunbae gathered his strength.

“I will cook with all my heart.”

Jo Chunbae walked proudly toward the kitchen.

Then, from behind him, Geom Mugeuk spoke loudly.

“For today, the owner is the king of every tavern in the world!”

In that instant, Jo Chunbae’s legs went weak, and he nearly staggered and collapsed.

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Jin Haryeong burst out laughing. She had been caught off guard, and the laughter escaped.

The elderly master of the Martial Alliance standing beside her turned to look at her, and Jin Haryeong made an effort to erase the smile from her face.

‘The Young Cult Leader really is insane. And I’m an insane woman for coming all the way here because I wanted to see that madman.’

On the other hand, those who had only heard rumors about Geom Mugeuk were glaring at him with the thought, ‘What on earth is he doing?’ Was he mocking them?

—Be careful of that tavern owner.

They even exchanged such sound transmissions. They had misunderstood Jo Chunbae as perhaps being the hidden card prepared by the Demon Cult.

Bi Sain, Jin Hagun, and Jin Haryeong knew. That tavern owner was an utterly ordinary person. They knew that Geom Mugeuk’s demonic path lay in protecting that ordinary man. No matter how many people were in this cramped tavern, he would never let that table be broken.

—You really are pulling this off.

At Jin Hagun’s sound transmission, Geom Mugeuk answered.

—We’re pulling it off. Though I didn’t know they would hate each other this much.

Then again, it was no different from enemies meeting after ten years.

—Can you resolve this well?

—I have to try.

Now the staring contest on the first floor was no longer between two groups, but three.

With their respective successors standing before them, they lined up in rows and glared at one another.

The orthodox masters hated the unorthodox faction just as much as they hated demonkind.

The Great Righteous Hero, Im Sanggon, now glared at an unorthodox master. There was one person in particular he hated.

Thunderstrike Blade Cheon Mang.

He was a master who had recently gained fame in the unorthodox faction, and several orthodox masters had died fighting him. Im Sanggon had wanted to meet him someday, and now he was seeing him here.

When the Great Righteous Hero glared, Thunderstrike Blade reacted. He did not avert his gaze, but glared right back.

The gazes of the two men, cursing each other with their eyes, gradually grew heated.

If left alone, it seemed they would charge at each other without regard for what came before or after.

It was then that someone placed a hand on Thunderstrike Blade’s shoulder.

When Thunderstrike Blade turned, Goeak, one of the Seven Great Masters of the Evil Dao, gave a slight shake of his head, telling him to stop.

Goeak was on friendly terms with Thunderstrike Blade. Only by stopping him this visibly would there be a pretext to step back. Sure enough, Thunderstrike Blade turned his gaze away.

Bi Sain looked at Goeak and sent him a gaze that said he had done well.

The situation on the second floor was not much different.

When Baek Jagang came up to the second floor, Geom Ujin and Jin Paecheon rose from their seats to greet him.

It was the moment the three of them finally gathered in one place.

Demon, orthodox, and evil.

The first conference of this generation.

Baek Jagang could tell.

Just from the heavy atmosphere flowing through this place, he could guess what the mood had been like.

Yes, we are not the sort to meet with smiles.

If not for the special variable that was Geom Mugeuk, they might have gone their entire lives without seeing each other’s faces. Or else they would have seen one another on the day they died.

Knowing that this was not an atmosphere for exchanging greetings, Baek Jagang sat in the remaining seat.

Once Baek Jagang sat down, Geom Ujin and Jin Paecheon also sat down with him. As expected, the greetings were omitted.

Baek Jagang looked at Geom Ujin first. Seeing Geom Ujin after so long, he gave off a different impression from when Baek Jagang had last seen him.

‘You, too, are aging.’

Only those eyes that seemed to look down on people were just as they had been before.

This time, Baek Jagang looked at Jin Paecheon. He was looking out the window, avoiding the gazes of the two men.

If Jin Paecheon hated Geom Ujin, would he like Baek Jagang?

Baek Jagang could tell.

‘He’s already somewhat heated.’

It was probably because of Geom Ujin’s gaze. Even he felt this displeased by it, so how much worse must that proud, rigid old man feel?

‘Can this conference really happen?’

And then, a question arose in Baek Jagang’s mind.

‘This man raised a son like that?’

This cold, blunt man had raised that talkative, bright son? He wanted to ask at once.

‘Just how did you raise that son of yours?’

Even with all three gathered, it was quiet. Someone had to take the lead and guide the conversation, but none of the three had any intention of doing so.

The silence on the second floor heightened the tension on the first even further. At this rate, if someone on the second floor said—

Sweep them all away!

The moment those words came out, this place would become a sea of blood.

And the one to resolve this heavy atmosphere was Geom Mugeuk.

“Master.”

Everyone’s gaze focused on Geom Mugeuk.

The Demon Venerables who knew him all thought the same thing.

Good. Since the young cult leader has stepped forward, the mood will shift now and the meeting will begin in earnest.

He was relieved that he himself had not been singled out.

‘What trick is that brat trying to pull?’

The Fist Demon felt uneasy inside, but answered in a low, weighty voice.

“What is it?”

“I mean the young alliance leader of the Sado Alliance over there.”

This time, Bi Sain flinched. Why me? He had a bad feeling.

‘Please, don’t say anything unnecessary!’

Geom Mugeuk’s question shattered Bi Sain’s desperate wish in a single blow.

“Isn’t that face the sort you prefer, Master?”

It was a question that bewildered both of them. Of course, since silence had settled over the hall, Geom Mugeuk’s words were heard not only by the three on the second floor, but even by Jo Chunbae in the kitchen.

Everyone’s gaze focused on the Fist Demon.

At a three-party meeting, he was supposed to appraise the young alliance leader of the Sado Alliance’s face?

It was the sort of thing that should have made thunder rumble from the Fist Demon’s fist, but the thunder only rumbled in his heart. The thunder roared with these words.

‘We’ll see after this is over.’

He could not shout at the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult here, and ignoring him might be rude to Bi Sain.

“I like faces that look manly. Yes, that is right. It is a face I like.”

It was something the usual Fist Demon would never have said. But the Fist Demon knew. Geom Mugeuk had begun to step forward for the sake of this uneasy and perilous meeting. And so he had stepped forward for his disciple.

If someone else had said those words, Bi Sain might have taken offense.

Is this bastard mocking me?

But these words came from the Fist Demon, the strongest impressionist who, by impression alone, seemed capable of facing everyone gathered there.

“Thank you for looking on me favorably.”

Bi Sain cupped his fist and expressed his gratitude.

It would have been good if it had ended there.

“What do you think of my master’s face?”

Bi Sain was flustered. He had never imagined he would be asked about the Fist Demon’s face.

—Why me? Why not tease Unit Leader Jin over there!

—Because teasing you is more fun.

In the past, he would have been flustered and instinctively blurted out, “You look splendid,” or “You look even more manly.”

But now, things were different.

Bi Sain first looked at the Fist Demon. Setting aside his preconceptions, he calmly gazed at him.

“Of all the people I have met in the martial world, you look the most frightening.”

He spoke honestly, yet politely.

Perhaps his attitude did not grate on him, for the Fist Demon accepted it with a gentle joke.

“Fortunately, this face is quite useful in my line of work.”

The masters of the righteous and unorthodox factions there found it rather unexpected. He was very different from the Fist Demon they had heard of in rumors.

In any case, the conversation had begun with an absurd question, but in the end, it softened the explosive atmosphere on the first floor.

And then came the final bit of banter. Geom Mugeuk said to Bi Sain,

“To tell the truth, I also like faces like our young alliance leader’s.”

At that, Bi Sain asked,

“If you could trade faces with me, would you?”

“…….”

That exchange alone showed the relationship between the two. In front of the masters gathered there, they did not hide their friendship.

Just then, Geom Ujin’s voice came from the second floor.

“Mugeuk, come up.”

At that, Jin Paecheon spoke as if in competition.

“Hagun, come up.”

Baek Jagang did not stay still either.

“Sain, come up.”

And so the three went up to the second floor.

The three on the second floor knew. With only themselves, this meeting would not be easy. They disliked one another far too much. They did not even want to look at one another, so what could they possibly say?

The three successors each stood beside their cult leader or alliance leader.

Then Geom Ujin said,

“Muyang, you come up as well.”

Since he had not expected to be called too, Geom Muyang went up to the second floor in surprise.

Geom Muyang stood on the opposite side of Geom Mugeuk, as if spreading wings.

So you have two sons, is that it? Ridiculous. Jin Paecheon, the doting grandfather, could not hold back.

“Haryeong, come up.”

Jin Haryeong came up and stood on the opposite side of Jin Hagun. Jin Paecheon wore a proud expression.

Baek Jagang looked at the two of them with an incredulous expression.

‘So this is how you’re going to be?’

Absolute Regression

Chapter 388: Didn't We Form a United Front?
-------------------------------------------
It was an unthinkable joint operation between the Orthodox faction and the Demonic Cult.

Baek Jagang looked at Jin Paecheon, who was beaming with pride, before turning his gaze to Geom Ujin again.

The first person to draw his sword was that Geom Ujin. He was the very one who had summoned Geom Muyang, after all. The Heavenly Demon was bragging about his children?

"It seems the changes were not merely external."

He wondered if perhaps it was this change that had raised Geom Mugeuk.

In truth, the person more flustered than Baek Jagang at this moment was Bisain.

Everyone else was in pairs; only he was alone, so he couldn't help but feel as though he had done something wrong. He felt sorry toward his master.

He wanted to shout in his master's stead.

"You two! You are truly childish!"

He looked at Geom Mugeuk with a gaze that said, "You think so too, don't you?"

Then, as if he had been waiting, Geom Mugeuk's voice transmission flew in.

"Later, you must have many children! Have about seven and line them up! Then take your revenge."

"You call that a proper thing to say!"

He regretted looking. Geom Mugeuk had probably been scouring for an opportunity to tease him.

In truth, of everyone present, only Geom Mugeuk was guessing the underlying meaning of his father calling his elder brother here.

Father wanted to show them off. His two sons who had ended the succession struggle without shedding blood. Thus, he wanted to boast of his sons standing proudly at his left and right. This was both boasting and praise.

"Right? The joy of sparing is greater than the joy of killing, is it not? Even if others don't know, Father does, doesn't he? That this is a far more difficult thing. Because he is someone who can kill anyone if he sets his mind to it."

Anyway, Geom Mugeuk could feel it. That this moment of placing his elder brother and himself side by side was the first and last time in his father's life that he boasted about his children.

"Father, I will not let this be your last boast. I will let you keep boasting."

While this was happening, Baek Jagang's counterattack began.

"A tree with many branches knows no day without wind."

In all those years living alone, was there only loneliness? It had truly been a life lived freely and without restraint. But how could anyone boast of that arduous path before this good life?

A slight shadow swept across Jin Paecheon's face, which had been swelling with pride. Jin Paecheon, who had sent his child off before him, was the one who had been struck head-on by that wind. Because his branches had broken.

That was why he had tried so hard to raise Jin Hagun and Jin Haryeong preciously. To him, who had lived his life dedicating himself solely to the Martial Alliance and the martial world, those two were the only private desire permitted.

Just when the atmosphere was in danger of growing heavy, Geom Ujin spoke.

"From those branches, flowers bloom and birds chirp, do they not?"

Geom Mugeuk did not miss the opportunity and stepped in.

"My elder brother is the flower, and I am the chirping bird."

Then Geom Ujin looked at Geom Muyang and said,

"Flowers bloom."

And looking at Geom Mugeuk,

"The wind blows as well."

He corrected him like that.

Geom Mugeuk put on an expression that said, "You are too much."

At that sight, Jin Haryeong smiled.

In a different sense, she thought Geom Mugeuk was a wind-like person. One who suddenly blew in, raising a whirlwind, before disappearing like the wind. That was why she had come to find that wind herself.

As expected, even at today's gathering, the wind was blowing without fail.

"Truly unimaginable things are happening."

She had never dreamed she would be called up to a place where these three were gathered. She had known even less that the conversation would be broached through boasting about children.

For the first time, she saw the Heavenly Demon this closely. And surprisingly, before the thought "So that person is the Heavenly Demon" could arise, this thought came first.

"That is his father."

He looked alike, yet not alike.

Geom Ujin exuded an oppressive aura that made it difficult to even get a word in. It would have been the same even if one didn't know he was the Heavenly Demon. It was not the dignity of his position. It was not the fear born from a frightening appearance like the Demonic Lord or Bisain. There was an intense presence emanating from the very person called Geom Ujin.

She should have felt fear and revulsion.

But she did not hate it so much. Rather, she wanted to speak to him.

"I have wanted to meet you at least once."

Was it because he was Geom Mugeuk's father? Though it was their first meeting, it felt like she had seen him many times before, and she felt a strange intimacy.

Her gaze then turned to Geom Muyang. It was the same this time. Not as the Great Young Lord of the Heavenly Demon Cult.

"So that is his elder brother."

He felt completely different from Geom Mugeuk. He felt composed and orderly, and he did not seem like someone who would reveal his emotions easily. If anything, the elder brother was the one who resembled their father, not Geom Mugeuk.

Her eyes met Geom Muyang's, and she bowed her head slightly in greeting. Geom Muyang also lowered his head slightly.

"We do have something in common."

The fact that we are blood relatives yet pushed out of the succession. Of course, she had never had any ambition to become the successor in the first place.

"Whoever marries you two, the most frightening father-in-law in the world awaits."

The future father-in-law asks.

"What does your father do?"

"He is the Heavenly Demon."

Recalling such a situation, she found it amusing just to imagine it. In any case, matters related to Geom Mugeuk always made her nervous, excited, and made her laugh.

Her gaze then turned to Bisain.

It was a face that would normally be difficult to look at, but strangely, it did not feel so hideous.
