His dancing younger brother, their grandfather together with the Demonic Cult Leader and the Unorthodox Alliance Leader, and himself together with the Demonic Cult’s Grand Prince. None of it felt real.

Jinha-gun’s gaze finally turned toward the person who had created all these fantastical events.

Yes. How could even that person not feel embarrassed?

He was surely enduring it for the sake of a friend. Even if it had not been a dance floor but a deadly trap surrounded by thousands of enemies, the Geom Mugeuk he knew would have leaped in.

He had to manage the martial world together with this person. To keep pace with him, all he needed to do was remember this moment and never forget how much effort it would require.

Bisa-in, who was following along with that adorable dance despite his fierce expression, was surely making that effort as well. At least, he was one step ahead of Jinha-gun.

Geom Mugeuk shouted to the musicians.

“Now, give us the final song!”

Perhaps because they thought it would be their last, the three moved even more flamboyantly.

They had begun dancing in embarrassment, but things were different now. Fully absorbed in the dance, they were releasing all the anger that had built up within them. When would they ever have another chance to dance to music like this?

And so, the final song ended, and silence descended upon the place that had been so noisy.

Bisa-in belatedly came to his senses.

They had danced to the music, and Geom Mugeuk had leaped in. After that, he could hardly remember anything—not what dance he had performed or how he had danced it.

It felt as though a great storm had passed. A tidal wave had swept through the marketplace, while lava had set his heart ablaze.

Awkwardness came rushing over him. Should he run into the tavern? While he was debating that, Geom Mugeuk announced in a loud voice:

“That concludes the dance performance by the Unorthodox Alliance’s Young Leader, Bisa-in.”

Applause broke out from here and there, and the three performers clasped their fists and bowed. The hall leaders who had been guarding the place applauded enthusiastically. Jang Ho, in particular, shouted in a booming voice:

“Magnificent!”

Out of courtesy to Bisa-in and Jinha-ryeong, he did not single anyone out, but of course, he had been speaking about Geom Mugeuk. What he had really wanted to say was, “Young Cult Leader, you were the best!”

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly and waved toward Jang Ho and the hall leaders.

Watching him, Jinha-gun found himself thinking: Had he ever seen a leader wave at his subordinates with such a radiant expression?

Did he not know that sorrowful truth—that in the end, people betrayed not the frightening ones, but the ones who treated them well? Of course not. He must have known that better than anyone. Even knowing it, he smiled and waved like that.

The trio of dancers walked toward the entrance.

“How was it?”

In response to Geom Mugeuk’s question, Geom Muyang answered with another question.

“Tell me honestly. You wanted to dance, didn’t you?”

At his brother’s joke, Geom Mugeuk laughed.

“I think I have a talent for dancing.”

Geom Muyang turned away as though he had not heard him, and everyone followed him into the tavern.

Jo Chun-bae, who was standing at the entrance, spoke with a deeply moved expression.

“There have been countless performances in Maga Village’s marketplace, but today’s was the best of them all!”

The scene of them dancing in front of his tavern had also become a painting in Jo Chun-bae’s heart. Like the sight of the Demon Venerables standing there, this was something he would never forget.

Meanwhile, the Unorthodox masters looked at Bisa-in with displeased expressions. Derision flickered across some faces, while others looked at him as though he were pathetic. Though most did not openly show it, their expressions were overwhelmingly negative. They could hardly be otherwise. The successor of the Unorthodox Alliance had danced without the slightest regard for decorum.

In the past, Bisa-in would undoubtedly have been consumed by such reactions. He would have thought that the reason his heart was pounding as if it might burst was because of them. He would have blamed himself for dancing a dance he should never have danced.

But things were different now. His heart was not beating so quickly because of them. This throbbing came from the joy of keeping his promise. From the friendship he had shared in his embarrassment.

That was why Bisa-in paid no attention to those who were now looking at him with displeasure.

They were his master’s people, anyway. By the time he became the Unorthodox Alliance Leader, new people would be standing in those places.

‘The people I need to worry about are the ones who will be standing there then.’

He already thought he had changed and grown greatly to reach this point, but Geom Mugeuk pulled him one step further by swaying his hips playfully.

Geom Mugeuk was looking at the Demon Venerables and teasing them by demonstrating some of the dance moves they had performed moments earlier.

The Demon Venerables each reacted differently. Some shook their heads, some looked away, and some smiled. But at least none of them sneered or looked at him with contempt. The deep familiarity and bond between them could be felt plainly.

Watching that, Bisa-in realized that his thinking had been wrong.

‘I shouldn’t wait until later. I have to change things now.’

Those Demon Venerables over there were not Geom Mugeuk’s own Demon Venerables, but the Demon Venerables of the current Heavenly Demon. Even so, they reacted like that. If they were this way, then what would Geom Mugeuk’s Demon Venerables be like in the future? If Geom Mugeuk leaped in, perhaps all eight of them would leap in and dance together. If they were Geom Mugeuk’s Demon Venerables, that is.

‘I have to work until the expressions of those Unorthodox masters change to resemble the expressions of these Demon Venerables.’

Bisa-in looked at the Unorthodox masters again. Then he clasped his fists politely, wearing an expression that seemed to say, “I’m still young, after all.”

How much difference could a single gesture like that make? And yet, amid the continued displeasure, he sensed that a few gazes had softened, if only slightly.

That was right. There was no later when it came to relationships between people. If you could not do it now, you would not be able to do it later, either.

Even during this brief moment after the dance performance, as they climbed to the second floor, Bisa-in continued to draw stimulation from Geom Mugeuk. Rather than letting that stimulation pass him by, he was trying to understand it and change himself.

They went up to the second floor.

Geom Ujin, Jin Paecheon, and Baek Jagang were standing there, looking in their direction.

Bisa-in walked over to them and stopped before Baek Jagang.

“I kept my promise and returned.”

Bisa-in was inwardly tense. He had gone out to dance while swept up in the heat of the moment, but now he had returned to reality.

Come to think of it, telling his master that he was going out to dance had been madness. He had even said it with the Demonic Cult Leader and the Martial Alliance Leader standing beside him.

He had been bracing himself for a thunderous reprimand, but—

“You could use a little more practice.”

Only then did Bisa-in relax.

“Yes, Master.”

The emotion on Bisa-in’s face was unmistakable.

‘Thank you, Master. I will never forget the immense consideration you showed for your disciple today.’

Jin Paecheon shook his head at Jinha-ryeong with an expression that said she was hopeless, while she melted her grandfather’s heart with a coquettish smile.

Geom Ujin was looking at Geom Mugeuk with his characteristic sneer. Today’s sneer seemed to say, ‘Now you’re even dancing in front of your father?’

Yes, Father. If I can see this sneer of yours, this sneer that shows you are responding to something new and enjoying yourself, I would do anything.

And so, the successors gathered together once more and drank.

Bisa-in drained his cup in one go. He felt truly liberated. Had he ever felt this free in his life?

He had even danced in front of all these people. What in this world could he possibly not do now?

Bisa-in expressed his gratitude to Jinha-ryeong. He knew she had joined in for Geom Mugeuk’s sake, but thanks to her participation, they had been able to finish on such a pleasant note. It had been fun, too.

“Thank you for joining us.”

“I enjoyed it, too.”

Bisa-in deliberately did not thank Geom Mugeuk. He had been waiting for Geom Mugeuk to playfully ask why he had not thanked him.

“You were the most impressive I’ve seen since the day I met you!”

Geom Mugeuk was always shattering his expectations like this.

Bisa-in stared at him without a word. Geom Mugeuk had rushed over and danced with him during that mortifying moment. Bisa-in could have even bowed to him if asked. And yet this man was the one to tell him first that he had been impressive.

Do you know something? I’m going to live as impressively as you do. That’s why I’m having such a hard time these days. Trying to chase a stork without tearing my thighs apart is exhausting.

“Is that a compliment to a martial artist?”

“It is because you’re a martial artist. What martial artist in the world could do something like that?”

Jinha-gun, who had been listening to their conversation, agreed with those words. Neither what Geom Muyang had shown earlier nor what Bisa-in had shown was something he could do.

Geom Mugeuk asked him:

“Do you regret it? Regret not dancing with us?”

Normally, Jinha-gun would have answered that it was something he could never do, even if he died. But he said nothing. Truthfully, if he had been able to do it, part of him wondered what it would have been like to try.

Reading his thoughts, Geom Mugeuk smiled.

“Then say it out loud and get it off your chest. Say, ‘If I get another chance like this, I’ll definitely dance!’ Say it with style.”

Jinha-gun turned to Bisa-in and asked:

“So this is how you fell into the trap?”

Bisa-in nodded.

“That’s right. By the time I came to my senses, I had already made the promise.”

The others smiled at his words.

Even as they drank together, they felt a lingering regret.

They knew that once this moment ended, it would not be easy to create another gathering like this, and so they regretted every passing second.

Once they left the tavern, this moment would remain only as a memory and a longing.

They wanted to drink through the night, but eventually the time to part ways arrived.

Jin Paecheon looked at Baek Jagang. They could understand each other through their eyes alone: It was time to leave. Baek Jagang nodded.

When they had first arrived, they had not even wanted to exchange a single word, so being able to share such a glance could itself be called great progress.

Jin Paecheon and Baek Jagang rose from their seats together.

“Thank you for inviting us.”

Geom Ujin stood.

“Thank you for coming such a long way.”

They exchanged brief, but proper, farewells.

They were still enemies, but at least until they had uprooted the force behind this incident, they would join hands.

The successors also said their reluctant goodbyes.

Geom Mugeuk said to Bisa-in:

“The way you kept your promise was impressive.”

Those were the words Bisa-in most wanted to hear from Geom Mugeuk. The greatest reason he had found the courage was that he wanted to show Geom Mugeuk that he could keep his promises. How flawless this man was. Geom Mugeuk had not forgotten to mention that very point.

“Even if you say that, I’m never making another promise like that again.”

This time, he said goodbye to Jinha-gun.

“Once you officially become the successor, let’s get together again. We’ll hold a celebration and play through the night!”

Jinha-gun had not yet formally taken the position of Young Alliance Leader.

“Let’s do that.”

Finally, they parted from Jinha-ryeong. Of the three, she was the one who felt the greatest regret. But she made an effort not to show it.

“Do you think word will spread that I danced?”

“It probably will.”

“If I’d known this would happen, I would have danced a little better.”

Geom Mugeuk looked around at everyone and said:

“I can’t bear to see Miss Jin feeling disappointed. Let’s dance one more time, all five of us.”

Geom Muyang and Jinha-gun simultaneously drew their swords halfway from their scabbards.

“See? I told you those two were exactly alike.”

And so, they exchanged reluctant farewells. At that moment, they felt as though they could play together all night.

Before they went down to the first floor, Jin Paecheon asked:

“Surely, Cult Leader Geom, you weren’t the one who wrote those words?”

They had checked the names written on the wall when they first entered. At the time, he had assumed they were merely scribbles left by drunkards, but now he wondered if that might not be the case.

“I was.”

Jin Paecheon was startled by Geom Ujin’s answer. If so, that meant the words left by the Demon Venerables were all genuine as well.

In all his life, he had never seen martial artists leave their names on the walls of a tavern or an inn in the martial world. And it was not as though they were ordinary martial artists.

Then someone spoke from behind them.

“Alliance Leader, why don’t you leave a few words as well?”

When they turned around, Geom Mugeuk was standing behind them.

“Me?”

Jin Paecheon was flustered. He was a man who rarely lost his composure, even in the face of extraordinary events, yet since arriving here, he had been surprised and bewildered again and again.

“Wouldn’t people find it strange? This is where you fellows have left your names.”

“Wouldn’t it make you look even more impressive? ‘The Martial Alliance Leader proudly left his name here before departing. How magnificent.’”

No, even so, it would not do.

“People will look at it without giving it much thought. ‘Oh, I see. So he wrote his name.’”

The moment he heard those words, Jin Paecheon recalled the emotion that had occurred to him earlier—the moment he had regretted the days of his youth, when he had neither smiled so brightly nor played like this.

‘What had I been thinking so hard about? Why had I treated life so seriously?’

Yes. He would leave a few words behind.

Jin Paecheon stepped toward the wall covered in inscriptions.

He was about to write on that wall when he turned toward another one. It would not do to write above the Heavenly Demon’s inscription, nor would it feel right to write beneath it. He was the sort of person who worried even about things like this.

Jin Paecheon drew his sword. With it, he carved words into the wall. His writing flowed freely from the very first character, without the slightest hesitation.

Jin Paecheon, Martial Alliance Leader, witnessed a new path of the demonic way here.

He had left those words for Geom Mugeuk. If not for him, he would never have written anything. No, he would not have come here in the first place.

“Thank you.”

Geom Mugeuk expressed his gratitude with a respectful clasp of his fists.

Then he looked at Baek Jagang. Baek Jagang did not hesitate.

“When Zheng and Mo go, the Unorthodox must follow.”

He left his inscription below Jin Paecheon’s. He did not worry about whether it should go above or below. If Jin Paecheon’s calligraphy was powerful and grand, Baek Jagang’s was neat yet sharp.

Baek Jagang, Unorthodox Alliance Leader, watched his disciple’s dance at the Wind and Current Tavern.

It was a historic moment: the words of the Heavenly Demon, the Martial Alliance Leader, and the Unorthodox Alliance Leader were all carved into the walls of the Wind and Current Tavern.

Chapter 394: My Meeting Is Now Over.
------------------------------------
“Why has it gotten so quiet outside?”

At Songi’s words, Jo Chunbae turned his head.

Stir-frying, boiling, and grilling.

He had been wrestling with the dishes in the kitchen. Worried they might be late, worried they might not taste good, he had quite literally been pouring all his strength into cooking.

Jo Chunbae carefully poked his head out of the kitchen. The first floor, which had been noisy and bustling, had fallen silent, and everyone was looking up at the second floor.

Jo Chunbae came out of the kitchen as well and looked up at the second floor.

Astonishingly, the Sado Alliance Leader was leaving words on the wall.

‘Huk!’

Jo Chunbae clapped a hand over his mouth.

He couldn’t see what had been written, but above the words the Sado Alliance Leader was writing, there were other words already left there. They had to be the words left by the Murim Alliance Leader.

‘Ah!’

Jo Chunbae was so shocked and excited that his legs trembled. He had thought there would be nothing left to surprise him. Today, Jo Chunbae’s heart was growing stronger.

Meanwhile, Bi Sain, who had seen the words his master left on the wall, lowered his head deeply as if embarrassed. But there was a smile at the corners of his mouth.

They were words left at the tripartite talks, so they would become a topic of conversation throughout murim. They might even be passed down as history for a very long time.

There was no way his master did not know that, yet he had mentioned him. Even in this moment, his master was making him—not Geom Mugeuk, not Jin Hagun—the main character.

After leaving the words, the Murim Alliance Leader and the Sado Alliance Leader came downstairs. Behind them, the Heavenly Demon and the successors came down together. The sight of them descending the stairs was engraved deep in Jo Chunbae’s heart. To him, every scene taking place here was moving.

The experts downstairs did not know exactly what words had been exchanged above. That was because Geom Ujin had prevented their voices from leaking outside before the conversation began.

But judging from the expressions of the three leaders, and from the fact that they had even left words on the wall before coming down, they could guess that this historic meeting had ended successfully.

As Jin Paecheon was about to go outside, he smiled at Jo Chunbae, who was standing in front of the kitchen, and said,

“The liquor and the food were both delicious.”

The words “Oh my, thank you” came out of Jo Chunbae’s mouth on their own. Normally, he would have run over and bowed, but he could not do that in the presence of the Heavenly Demon. From in front of the kitchen, he bent at the waist to express his gratitude.

As Jin Paecheon walked out, Jin Hagun and Jin Haryeong followed behind him, and behind them again, the experts of the orthodox faction spread out to the left and right as they followed. As if they had never been drinking and making noise, they followed their Alliance Leader quietly and with discipline.

Before they left, there was also one incident known only to those involved.

The Tae-eul Divine Sword glared once more at the Ilhwa Sword Venerable to the very end, and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable likewise did not avoid that gaze until the last.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon, who was beside her, sent a voice transmission to the Tae-eul Divine Sword instead of saying to the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, “So young, so young.”

—Stop glaring now.

The Tae-eul Divine Sword glared at the Blood Heaven Saber Demon. Far from being frightened, another cold remark flew at him.

—Before I gouge out your eyes.

The Tae-eul Divine Sword was taken aback, never having expected the Blood Heaven Saber Demon to hurl such a crude insult. He could not flare up either. Judging from that savage force glaring at him, if he reacted, it seemed the man would immediately shout for them to fight.

Leaving aside whether he would win or lose in a fight with the Blood Heaven Saber Demon, after a successful meeting had ended and the Cult Leader and Alliance Leader had even exchanged farewells, there was no way they could start a fight.

‘He knows that, and that’s why he’s doing this.’

The Tae-eul Divine Sword, who had been glaring at the Blood Heaven Saber Demon, abruptly turned his head away.

‘These damned Demonic Cult bastards, every last one of them!’

Truly, when it came to the Demonic Cult, he was sick to death of them.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable asked the Blood Heaven Saber Demon through voice transmission.

—What did you say to him?

—I politely told him to stop staring.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable thought there was no way that was true. His earlier reaction had not been a reaction to polite words.

—Thank you, friend.

When the Blood Heaven Saber Demon looked at her with an incredulous expression, she replied,

—We’re the same age now, aren’t we?

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon smiled faintly. At the very least, his response to the Tae-eul Divine Sword just now had suited the words, “If only I were ten years younger!” The curse he had actually spat out was something he would have said only when he had been some forty years younger. If it had not been a matter involving the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, he never would have done it.

Next, the unorthodox faction left.

Before leaving, Baek Jagang glanced once at Geom Ujin and Geom Mugeuk.

‘Let’s see how things look on harvest day.’

In the battle of raising one’s children, if the seed on this side was Bi Sain, he thought it was worth trying.

Baek Jagang also went outside. Behind him, the experts of the unorthodox faction spread out and moved on.

After seeing off all the experts of the orthodox and unorthodox factions, Geom Ujin was the last to leave. Geom Muyang and the Eight Demon Venerables followed behind him, spreading out like wings.

And so everyone left that place, and the last people remaining in the Pungryu Tavern were Jo Chunbae and Geom Mugeuk.

Ah, there was one more person.

“Songi. You worked hard today.”

He paid Songi, who had been working in the kitchen, and sent her off. Since there had been a lot of work today, he gave her double the usual pay as a special gesture and packed up a great heap of the leftover food from the kitchen for her.

“Thank you! Elder, you should rest early too.”

Once even Songi had left, only Geom Mugeuk and Jo Chunbae remained there.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Jo Chunbae with gentle eyes.

“You truly worked hard.”

“Ah, it feels like a dream.”

Jo Chunbae plopped down into a seat. Then he sprang to his feet.

“May I go up to the second floor for a moment?”

“Let’s go together.”

Jo Chunbae stood before the wall on the second floor, overwhelmed.

“I never thought a day like this would come.”

On the wall of his tavern, the Heavenly Demon, the Murim Alliance Leader, and the Sado Alliance Leader had all left words behind. There was probably no tavern in the world like this one. There had never been one until now, and there would not be one in the future either.

At the same time, Jo Chunbae grew afraid.

“I am frightened.”

“What are you afraid of?”

“What if this wall is damaged?”

Just imagining it filled him with terror, and Jo Chunbae trembled all over. What if he came in the morning and someone had scribbled on the wall? What if someone broke the wall? What if someone stole the entire wall?

Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“It would make them the enemy of the Demonic, Orthodox, and Unorthodox factions. Who would dare do such a thing? Even if they tore that wall off and stole it, where would they sell it?”

Geom Mugeuk knew that such words would not completely reassure Jo Chunbae. He would go on fretting anxiously the whole time.

“Even if such a thing were to happen, that too would have its own meaning.”

“Pardon? What do you mean by that?”

“On such-and-such a day, at such-and-such an hour, someone damaged the wall for this or that reason and disappeared. I mean that would also become a moment in history. Perhaps it might even become an even more interesting piece of history.”

Jo Chunbae truly had not expected Geom Mugeuk to say such a thing.

“All the owner needs to do is remember this moment. Then this event will remain forever. Look. Among the people whose words remain on that wall, is there anyone who would get angry because the wall was damaged? They would probably come back and write something again before leaving.”

It was the moment the heavy boulder that had been weighing on Jo Chunbae’s chest was set down on the floor.

“So let us remember this moment well in our hearts.”

“Yes, Young Cult Leader.”

How could someone so young be so thoughtful? No matter how much Jo Chunbae thought about it, he could not understand.

“May I offer you a cup of wine?”

“That sounds good. I shall accept a cup from the owner.”

Jo Chunbae respectfully poured wine for Geom Mugeuk.

“As expected! The wine our owner gives me tastes the best.”

After drinking it down refreshingly, Geom Mugeuk picked up the wine bottle.

“Please accept a cup from me as well.”

He filled Jo Chunbae’s cup to the brim.

After receiving the wine respectfully, Jo Chunbae emptied the cup.

Only after drinking that wine could he finally feel that the greatest event in his life as a tavern owner had come to an end.

And Jo Chunbae saw it. The sight of Geom Mugeuk sitting quietly, looking at the street outside the tavern.

It was a quiet side of him, different from when he was laughing and joking. Jo Chunbae now knew. That calm appearance, tinged with loneliness, was the Young Cult Leader’s true self.

“Owner, then I shall be going now.”

Jo Chunbae could not think of what words he should use to bid him farewell. How should he convey this immense gratitude? And so he gave the greeting he always gave to the guests of his tavern.

“Guest, please come again!”

How could Geom Mugeuk not understand his heart? Geom Mugeuk matched the rhythm of that feeling and greeted him like a regular customer.

“Sell plenty, owner.”

When Geom Mugeuk left the tavern, the people of the marketplace who had been holding their breath until then could be seen entering the tavern. Perhaps tonight, Jo Chunbae’s boasting would continue until late. He would probably not even let them cough in front of that wall. As long as those people were alive, the wall too would live.

* * *

After returning to the main sect, I headed straight for the Cult Leader’s Hall.

It was to see the protagonist of today’s meeting. Father, who had led the tripartite talks to success, was not in the Cult Leader’s Hall.

“He told me to bring you to him when you arrived, Young Cult Leader.”

The place Hui, the guard, led me to was the hot spring reserved for the Cult Leader, located behind Father’s residence. Normally, he did not let me come this far, but for some reason, he allowed me in today.

Father was soaking in the hot spring, from which steam rose in wisps.

“A bath with Father after so long! How wonderful!”

Just as I was about to take off my clothes, Father held out his hand. The open front of my clothes closed again, and I was pushed backward by his void-seizing force.

“That is too much! Please let me in too!”

Father firmly shook his head.

“You put some elixir in that water, didn’t you? If you soak in it, your skin becomes as strong as steel and all that, right? That’s why you’re doing it alone, isn’t it?”

Father slowly closed his eyes and said,

“It is ordinary water. It has been passed down from long ago.”

“Then shouldn’t you at least add some elixir to it? It is a place where the Heavenly Demon soaks his body.”

“You buy some and put it in, then.”

“Anything is best in its natural state.”

Fine. No matter how much my father had changed, he was still not the type to bathe together with me. Letting me come this far alone was a great change.

“Then I’ll at least enjoy some of the steam here.”

I sat down in front of the hot spring.

“How was it, meeting the Murim Alliance Leader and the Sado Alliance Leader?”

“They were the same as ever.”

Even without saying it, he must have seen things I could not. To Father, they must have held another meaning.

Father, who had been silently gazing at me, spoke quietly.

“You look tired.”

At that one sentence, I felt the tension that had kept my heart stiff all day melt away.

As expected! Father noticed.

I was tired. Truly tired.

I had danced, laughed, and joked, but from the beginning of the meeting to the end, I had been tense.

In case they might be plotting something at this meeting, I had kept every nerve on edge. Even though Father was there, and even though the Murim Alliance Leader and the Sado Alliance Leader were there, because of that, I was even more on edge. Schemes were originally the sort of thing that dug into precisely that kind of carelessness.

Fortunately, this meeting ended without anything happening.

The fact that our enemies did not make a half-baked attempt was, rather, because they were strong. They had made the proper judgment. The precise calculation that if they schemed while we were gathered like this, they themselves would ultimately suffer losses.

If we directly experienced an external enemy while the Demonic, Orthodox, and Unorthodox factions were all gathered together, it would only result in making our side unite even more.

“It seems I was more worried than I realized.”

Father nodded.

“Will you not tell me I worked hard?”

“You worked hard.”

“It was praise I only got by poking Father in the side, but it still feels good.”

I looked around the hot spring.

“I remember coming in here.”

In that instant, the corners of Father’s eyes trembled slightly. The steam obscured my vision, but with the Divine Eye Art, I could see Father’s expression beyond the steam clearly.

“I remember crying because it was hot. Father carried me in. I remember my brother too. He was sitting in front of us with an expression that said it wasn’t hot at all. Now that I think about it, even when he was that young, he wanted to look good in front of Father.”

“Even after becoming a successor, you still curse your brother behind his back whenever he is not here.”

“You saw how impressive he was earlier, didn’t you? He’s a brother I can’t afford to be careless around.”

How could those words be meant to curse my brother? Father surely knew as well that I had brought it up because I wanted to say how impressive my brother had looked earlier.

After a brief pause, I spoke respectfully to Father.

“Thank you, Father.”

“For what?”

“For everything.”

My Demonic Way is blocking Father’s Demonic Way. He must have confirmed that once again today. It must certainly bother him and get on his nerves. Yet Father shows no sign of it at all.

After a brief pause, Father spoke.

“You must be tired. Go and rest.”

“Yes, Father. Please rest as well.”

Of course, I didn’t simply leave.

Splash.

After playfully splashing water at Father, I ran away.

Of course, not a single drop of that flying water would have reached him.

Next time, I’ll scrub your back for you, Father.

Though I don’t know if that day will ever come.

* * *

When I returned to my quarters, I used the Space-Time Shift Technique to create a second space of my own.

If the first place was a beach with blue skies and sea, this space was a snowy plain blanketed in fresh white snow in every direction. There, I created a small hot spring. Seeing Father’s hot spring had piqued my interest.

This place had only two things.

Snow and stars.

It was a place where only white snow and the stars of the night sky could be seen.

“What do you think, Father? Isn’t this place more elegant?”

After undressing and folding my clothes neatly, I placed Bigwe on top.

Then I immersed myself in the steaming hot spring.

“Ah! This is great!”

Looking up at the night sky where stars seemed to cascade down, I sat there feeling all my fatigue melting away.

Only then did I truly feel that the negotiations had ended. While the others’ negotiations had ended earlier, my own had only just ended now.

After speaking with Jo Chunbae, meeting Father, and confirming that everyone had passed a safe day—only then had today’s negotiations truly ended.

A pleasant sense of relief washed over me. The weariness that came with it mixed with the warm water, and instead I felt even better.

Plunge.

I submerged my head deep into the water and let myself sink completely.

Above the water, the stars of the night sky swayed as if dancing.

This time alone was wonderful. If you truly respect someone, you must respect their time alone, and right now I was freely respecting myself.

Ah, but I’m not alone right now.

After lifting my head above the water, I playfully spoke to Bigwe.

“Are you cold? Want to come in too?”

Thanks to him, I had already absorbed Heukjeong and Cheongjeong. I had no idea how many more I needed to absorb for my qi to reach completion.

If it were anyone else, they would have devoted all their attention to uncovering the secrets of Bigwe or the qi absorbed into their body, but I was relaxed.

Since it had been that way from the start, I left it to fate’s guidance. I was simply traveling along like a friend. Still, I was curious sometimes.

“How many qi do you have left to absorb?”

Bigwe was not one to answer.

“You don’t actually know either, do you?”

Bigwe simply smiled brightly, as always.

Chapter 395: Only Nice in Front of Us.
--------------------------------------
The next morning, I was in my private training hall.

I had slept soundly for the first time in ages. I had slept so well and woken so refreshed that I wondered if I had greeted a morning like this at any point recently.

When I woke after such a good sleep, feeling light as if I had finished some great task, I decided to attempt something I had been putting off.

At the center of the training hall, where no one could enter without my permission, I cast the Space-Time Transposition Art and the Secret Art of Temporal Heaven in succession.

Then time within the Space-Time Transposition Art began to flow slowly.

Now, through the Secret Art of Temporal Heaven, I could use three times more time than in reality.

Being able to use three times as much time as others was more tremendous than anything, but in a situation where my goal was the great completion of the Twelfth Star of the Nine Flowers Demonic Art, it was still insufficient. I needed to secure more time.

Even so, I spent half of my training time cultivating the Nine Flowers Demonic Art. The reason I did not devote all my training solely to the Secret Art of Temporal Heaven was that I never knew what might happen at any moment.

In the world within the Space-Time Transposition Art, I unleashed the Nine Flowers Demonic Art.

First Form: Annihilation.

Saaaaaaak!

The terrifying illusions of four evil spirits, chilling just to behold, appeared simultaneously in the east, west, south, and north, and swung their swords.

Slash! Slash! Slash! Slash!

The frightening one, the handsome one. The one that gave off a strange sense of mystery, and the one that looked cleverer than anyone else. No matter when I saw them, they were reassuring, and I had seen them so often that they now felt almost like friends.

And in the meantime, there had been changes as well. The four evil spirits in the east, west, south, and north had become faster and stronger than before. Their positions of appearance, directions of attack, even the timing of their disappearance—I could handle them with greater freedom.

If I could achieve great completion of the Nine Flowers Demonic Art and summon the Heavenly Demon Soul, would I be able to form an emotional exchange with them as well? Would the Heavenly Demon Soul and the lesser evil spirits stand before me in such a relationship, like me and the Demon Venerables? I found myself harboring such pointless expectations.

Next, I displayed the Second Form: Great Destruction.

Sssssssss.

Originally, the frightening-looking evil spirit among the four directions split into six, but now two more had been added, and it split into eight.

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwak!

The eight evil spirits swept away everything in front of them and surged forward. No matter when I used it, Great Destruction always made me feel refreshed.

Third Form: Great Demon Wall.

A massive wall of astral qi rose before my eyes. Just as Father had said—that the higher the realm, the smaller its size became—the Great Demon Wall had also become smaller than at first.

When it reached its final realm, it would become as small as a shield that blocked only one person’s body.

But I had a grand plan for this Great Demon Wall. I would take that perfected Great Demon Wall, small as a shield, and raise it at this size while preserving all of its power. It was something impossible to achieve with great completion of the Tenth Star. If I could achieve great completion of the Twelfth Star, then perhaps?

Until now, I had repeatedly trained up to the Third Form.

And today, I intended to display the Fourth Form for the first time. Having recently faced the Twelve Zodiac Kings, my perspective on martial arts had changed, and now that my realm had also risen, I felt that the time had finally come to train the Fourth Form in earnest.

Fourth Form of the Nine Flowers Demonic Art: Dark Flash.

The moment I displayed the Fourth Form, the surroundings flickered. I had the illusion that I had closed and opened my eyes.

Though it failed, it was not a bad start for a first attempt.

I recited the formula again and attempted the Fourth Form.

This time as well, the surroundings darkened and then brightened. The time they remained dark was longer than before. It was as if a lamp had been covered by a hand and then revealed again.

I repeated it, and repeated it again.

As was the case with most martial arts, the further one progressed in the Nine Flowers Demonic Art, the greater the amount of internal energy required. This Fourth Form also required an immense amount of internal energy, so it was not a martial art that could be used carelessly.

Because of that, even I, who possessed vast internal energy, had to replenish it through qi circulation and breath regulation from time to time.

And so the attempts continued.

At last!

Whoosh!

Complete darkness settled around me.

The world went black, as though the light of the sun had vanished.

It was darkness itself, as if light had never existed in the first place, a pitch-black void where one truly could not see an inch ahead. It was a darkness that even the Divine Eye Art could not pierce.

Shiiing.

Within the darkness, a single streak of light cut across.

A single streak of light said to sever anything.

After the light passed, the surroundings brightened.

For now, I had faced an imaginary enemy, but if I had unleashed it toward someone real, that person would be lying ahead with their body severed.

‘I did it!’

I unleashed Dark Flash once more.

Darkness descended in every direction.

Shiiiing.

This time, the light was drawn diagonally. The direction of the flash could be determined according to the will of the one displaying the martial art.

Filled with a sense of accomplishment at successfully displaying the Fourth Form, I stood alone in the space of the Space-Time Transposition Art and laughed loudly.

If it was going to come easily, it could happen in a single day like this; but if it was going to torment you, it could take months or even years. That was the nature of an ultimate martial art like the Nine Flowers Demonic Art.

Of course, just as the other forms continued to develop, this was not the end of the Fourth Form either.

The more I trained it, the deeper the darkness would become, and the stronger its power. It had been passed down that a Dark Flash that had reached great completion could not be blocked by any protective astral qi.

When darkness falls, the opponent must die.

That is the Fourth Form of the Nine Flowers Demonic Art: Dark Flash.

* * *

When I finished training and came out, a welcome visitor had arrived.

“Young Cult Leader!”

The great saber he wore at his waist suited him so well now that the dignity of the Lord of the Yellow Springs Pavilion could be felt naturally. It was none other than Seo Daeryong.

“Have you eaten?”

“No.”

“I’m hungry. Let’s have lunch together and talk.”

I had a maidservant bring lunch for Seo Daeryong and me. Since I had trained for far longer than the time that had passed in reality, I was extremely hungry.

“Congratulations on safely concluding the talks yesterday.”

“Thank you.”

“Right now, it’s all anyone in the cult is talking about.”

“They’re only talking about the dancing, aren’t they?”

Unable to deny it, Seo Daeryong smiled awkwardly. The young alliance leader of the Sado Alliance dancing, and the Young Cult Leader of the Divine Cult and the Murim Alliance Leader’s granddaughter joining in to dance—it had been a major incident. The rumors would soon spread not only through the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, but across all of the Central Plains.

“I should have been there.”

Yes, if it had been Seo Daeryong, he would have danced with us. He would have made a fuss about how embarrassing it was, but he would have jumped in even before Jin Haryeong.

“I thought about sneaking over to watch, but I couldn’t do that either.”

Because only the unit leaders had been mobilized to guard the surroundings, he, as a pavilion lord, had been unable to attend.

“I don’t know about anyone else, but I wanted to see Miss Jin again.”

“You should have come. You could have kicked open the tavern door and shouted, ‘I am the champion who defeated Miss Jin in the final duel of the Little Dragon Tournament!’”

“If I had done that, the orthodox masters would have beaten me up. No, actually, Master would have thrashed me first.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed at Seo Daeryong’s banter.

“What about the results of the talks? Did they go well?”

“We decided to make it our top priority for the Demonic, Righteous, and Unorthodox factions to join forces and wipe them out.”

“You truly accomplished something remarkable.”

At Seo Daeryong’s admiration, Geom Mugeuk spoke humbly.

“Father was the one who accomplished it.”

Seo Daeryong quietly stared at Geom Mugeuk’s face.

“Why are you looking at me like that?”

“I just think that at times like this, you’re allowed to brag a little. You achieved a great undertaking that will never happen again in this world, didn’t you?”

This was the reason he had come today as well—to say these words. It was what Geom Mugeuk had always done for him. He praised him without overlooking even the smallest thing, then praised him again. Thanks to the strength of those words, the gloomy, pessimistic mere investigator he had once been had come this far.

“This humility is already calculated bragging.”

“You’ve succeeded. Even when you’re humble, you look impressive.”

Seo Daeryong asked all sorts of questions about the talks. What the Murim Alliance Leader was like, what the Sado Alliance Leader was like, which masters had come with them, whether Jo Chunbae had been nervous. He asked every question he was curious about.

“Are you disappointed? That you couldn’t be there?”

“A little.”

“You should have asked. If it was a request from you, I would have granted it.”

If it had been Seo Daeryong, I might have specially placed him beside Jang Ho.

“Everyone already knows I’m close to you, Young Cult Leader.”

He had not wanted to become the subject of gossip that private favors had been exchanged. More than being criticized himself, he had wanted to avoid causing harm to Geom Mugeuk.

“So what? Times like this are when you show it off. I cherish our Pavilion Lord Seo this much, so no one but me had better bully him!”

“What do you mean, no one should bully me? That’s not praise!”

Even as a joke, Seo Daeryong felt pleased.

After the two finished eating, they came out into the yard.

“Don’t just scrape by with meals because you live alone. Make sure you eat properly.”

“I understand.”

Seo Daeryong, who had greeted him and was about to leave, stopped in his tracks.

“Oh, right. Have you heard news about Martial Artist Yi?”

“No.”

“You don’t know where she is either?”

“I’m sure she’s doing well.”

At the indifferent reply, Seo Daeryong shook his head with an expression that seemed to say, “How could you be so heartless!”

“You may not remember, but among your subordinates, Young Cult Leader, there is a person named Ian. She was the woman called the Pretty Heart.”

At Seo Daeryong’s banter, Geom Mugeuk played along.

“I feel like I might remember her.”

“That’s too much! At the very least, you should know where Martial Artist Yi is and what she’s doing!”

“Do you know?”

“I do. Martial Artist Yi is currently in the North Sea. Her heart is freezing in that cold place!”

“In the North Sea? Why there?”

“How would I know that?”

Ian’s purpose in leaving the cult had been to recruit Seojin, the younger sibling of the Ghost Spirit, as the second squad leader of the Ghost Shadow Unit. Since it was her first journey through murim alone, I had told her not to hurry and to look around many places before returning. It seemed she had gone all the way to the North Sea.

“Aren’t you worried?”

“What is there to worry about? She can use this chance to travel around and experience the jianghu. Meet villains, meet lechers.”

You fool, who’s worrying about whom?

Ian had as many as nine tails. Her Flying Heaven Sword Art was also at the Ninth Star.

She was kind Ian only in front of us. If there was anyone to worry about, it would be the enemies who stood before her.

Geom Mugeuk trusted Ian. He trusted her skill and intelligence, but there was something he trusted even more.

She was the one who had let me live and return like this. I trust the bond between us, and I trust her fate.

But Seo Daeryong did not seem to feel the same.

“Ah, our Martial Artist Yi is so innocent, I’m worried she might be getting deceived somewhere.”

“Like you during the martial tournament?”

Back then, Seo Daeryong had nearly fallen head over heels for a woman.

“Why are you bringing up something from so long ago? I won’t fall for it anymore. I’m spicy ginger now.”

“To me, you still look like a crisp green apple.”

Pretending not to hear, Seo Daeryong changed the subject back to worrying about Ian.

“She has to check everything with a silver needle whenever she eats. I wonder if she took enough hidden weapons with her. If she goes around without wearing a veil, all sorts of scoundrels will swarm around her.”

“By the way, how did you know Ian was in the North Sea?”

“I went and asked the first squad leader of the Ghost Shadow Unit. He said the last place they received contact from was the North Sea.”

It seemed he had gone to ask Cheongmyeon.

“Have you been to the North Sea?”

“Of course.”

“I’ve never been there even once. Then have you been to the Ice Palace too?”

“Naturally.”

“Don’t tell me you’ve even met the Lord of the Ice Palace?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded. Of course, it had been before his regression. He had ties with the Ice Palace back then.

“If you go to the entrance of the Ice Palace, they freeze people and put them on display in a row.”

“Oh, surely not?”

“It’s true. They freeze everyone who stood against the Ice Palace and display them. This is what happens if you oppose us! A powerful warning.”

“Then all those people must be dead.”

“Who knows? Maybe when the ice melts, they move like jiangshi.”

As if the thought alone frightened him, Seo Daeryong flinched.

“That’s not all. If you go to the snowy mountains behind the Ice Palace, snowmen live there. Those snowmen cultivate a spiritual herb called the Ice-Snow Herb, and if you consume its fruit, you can gain tremendous internal energy.”

“Really? You can’t just lie to me because I’ve never been there.”

“If you don’t want to believe it, don’t.”

Seo Daeryong was already tempted. You fool, you’re this easy to deceive, and you’re worrying about someone else?

Stories of people being frozen alive and displayed, snowmen, and Ice-Snow Herbs were all legends passed down in tales.

“If you’re that worried about Ian, go look for her.”

“What about the Yellow Springs Pavilion?”

“Take leave and go. I’ll make it so you can be away.”

“What about my monthly stipend?”

“Of course you won’t receive it. It’s a personal matter. You should be willing to make that much sacrifice for Ian.”

Seo Daeryong blinked for a moment in contemplation, then took one step back.

“We’re not quite that close. And I’m very sensitive to the cold.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed at Seo Daeryong’s banter.

“Then I’ll be going.”

After Seo Daeryong left, Geom Mugeuk looked up at the sky. Though he had woken up in such a good mood that morning, he realized he had begun the day without looking up at the sky even once.

Ian, what is the sky like over the North Sea?

* * *

After sending Seo Daeryong off, the place Geom Mugeuk headed to was the residence of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been about to stride in boldly while calling for the elder, stopped in his tracks.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was in the flower garden prepared in the corner of the yard. It was that very flower garden where he had once pruned flowering tree branches with the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber.

There, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was planting flowers together with the One Flower Sword Venerable.

The man who had stood proudly before the orthodox and unorthodox masters yesterday and the man now planting flowers felt like completely different people.

At that moment, someone whispered beside Geom Mugeuk.

“They say late-blooming passions are the most frightening.”

When he turned around, it was the Drunken Demon. It was not something he should be saying, considering that lately he and Yeobin had been in quite the good mood.

“He’s been like that all morning.”

“What are you doing here, Brother?”

“I had something to say to the Sword Venerable.”

“Then go say it.”

The Drunken Demon’s gaze turned toward the two of them.

“Would I ruin such a good mood after so long?”

It was understandable that he hesitated. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon probably had no idea just how broadly he was smiling right now as he planted flowers with the One Flower Sword Venerable.

It seemed spring had come to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s heart as well.

Then the Blood Heaven Blade Demon shouted from inside.

“What are you two whispering about like a pair of rats?”

Geom Mugeuk shouted back to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Don’t mind us! We’re passersby! Just two rats passing through!”

Together with the Drunken Demon, he walked out of the place.

“Why were you trying to meet the Sword Venerable?”

“There’s something that’s been bothering me a little, so I was going to ask for her advice.”

“What is it?”

“Forget it. It’s nothing much.”

But Geom Mugeuk did not let it pass.

“Brother, a lot of problems that happen later come about because you dismiss them as nothing now, don’t they? What is it? Tell me already!”

Since Geom Mugeuk went that far, the Drunken Demon revealed what it was.

“There’s a place that sends me wine every year. Every year, without fail, when March comes, they send wine. But this year, the wine hasn’t come.”

If it had been an ordinary sect, he would not have brought it up like this.

“Where is that place?”

An unexpected name flowed from the Drunken Demon’s mouth.

“The North Sea Ice Palace.”

Chapter 396: No! This Is a Trip!
--------------------------------
The moment I heard the words North Sea Ice Palace, this thought came to mind.

“Ian, I guess fate is telling me to go see you.”

It was a place I hadn’t expected at all, yet I had heard of it twice in a single day. And it wasn’t as if I had merely overheard it in passing. Weren’t both Ian and Chwima people deeply connected to me?

“Why are you so surprised?”

“I was just thinking, so my brother is someone who gets wine sent to him every year by the Ice Palace.”

“I may be looked down on by you, but out there, people know who I am.”

How could I not know? I knew better than anyone. I knew how terrifying Chwima became if he truly got drunk and ran wild. Only someone like the Blood Heaven Blade Demon could call him a drunkard.

“But is the Ice Palace not sending wine really something you need to discuss with the Sword Venerable?”

For an instant, a faint trace of fluster crossed Chwima’s eyes.

“You say that because you don’t know how delicious their ice wine is.”

“Then since we’re on the subject, shall we go to the North Sea Ice Palace together?”

“The two of us?”

“If they won’t send the wine, we can just go there and drink it ourselves. Surely they wouldn’t refuse us a cup of ice wine if we came in person. Let’s have a drink of ice wine on the summit of a snowy mountain.”

It would be a lie to say I didn’t feel bothered by Chwima, who had always lamented that he was pushed to the back. I wanted to spend time with him on this occasion.

Drinking ice wine with Chwima while snowflakes swirled around us on the summit of a snowy mountain?

Ah, just thinking about it made me excited. It would truly be an unforgettable moment.

Besides, hadn’t I even soaked in a hot spring on a snowfield yesterday? Yes, this was fate telling me to go to the North Sea.

“Let’s go together.”

I naturally thought he would say yes, but unexpectedly, Chwima refused.

“I’m fine.”

“What? You always said you wanted to hang out with me. Was that all talk? You said you wanted to travel.”

“I have something to do.”

Judging from his expression, it was clear there was some sort of circumstance.

Could there be something he couldn’t tell me that had to do with the Ice Palace? I was curious, but I did not press him.

Among the Demon Venerables, Chwima was the one I treated with the least formality. Precisely because of that, I could have grilled him about what it was, or teased him that he simply didn’t want to be apart from Yeobin.

But I didn’t.

When the other person signals, this far, you have to step back. We always make mistakes with those we are comfortable with, and we are rude to those we are comfortable with.

The more comfortable someone is, the more you should be considerate of them and look after them. If you think of the effort the other person must have made to become part of that comfort, that is.

So I changed the subject first.

“Brother, how did yesterday’s conference look to you?”

“Do you know why I especially liked this conference?”

“Why?”

“Because it was held in a tavern. I liked it even more that your demonic path is in a drinking house.”

Chwima’s words made me laugh of their own accord. It was the moment another meaning was attached to the Pungryu Tavern. A place where my demonic path and Chwima’s path of wine met.

After talking with him about this and that, we parted. Neither he nor I brought up the matter of going to the North Sea again until the end.

* * *

After parting with Chwima, Geom Mugeuk returned to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s residence.

The reason he had come to see the Blade Demon today was to express his gratitude for helping the conference end well. But now, the purpose of his visit had changed into saying farewell before leaving for the North Sea.

The Blade Demon and the Sword Venerable were talking in the yard while looking at the flowers they had planted.

Geom Mugeuk quietly stood beside the two of them and said,

“The flowers are truly beautiful.”

Just looking at them made him feel good.

Ilhwa Sword Venerable looked at Geom Mugeuk as he gazed at the flowers. She could feel that he was not deliberately pretending to like them to show her, but that he truly liked flowers.

“Shall I plant flowers in your yard as well, Young Cult Leader?”

“No. I wander around so much they’d die soon enough. I have to leave again this time too.”

“Where to?”

“Something came up, so I need to go to the North Sea.”

At the mention of the North Sea, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon asked with a look of surprise.

“Why there?”

“According to my right arm, my heart is freezing there.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon did not understand Geom Mugeuk’s joke.

“What are you trying to do this time?”

“Could there be anything more shocking than the Orthodox-Unorthodox-Demonic Conference?”

“If it’s you.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon sighed and shook his head.

“I thought spring had come, but another snowstorm is starting to blow!”

Ilhwa Sword Venerable took up those words.

“Blood will be scattered across a snow-white field.”

Judging only from what I had shown them so far, I deserved to be misunderstood like this, but still.

“No, please don’t misunderstand! I’m just going to meet someone! I’m going to see Ian. Laughing, chatting, sightseeing. I’m going to search the North Sea for restaurants with skilled cooks.”

The two of them pretended not to hear and looked at one another with a sigh.

“How many will die this time, I wonder.”

I said no! This is a trip to the North Sea!

* * *

Before leaving for the North Sea, I busily went around saying my farewells. When I had gone to save Jo Chunbae’s family last time, I had left the cult in such a hurry it was practically like running away from home, but this time, I said goodbye to everyone. Once I left this time, I intended to tour the Central Plains together with Ian before returning.

Father said only one thing.

“Don’t cause trouble!”

My master, the Fist Demon, added one more remark to the instruction not to cause trouble.

“The Ice Palace Lord is a cold person.”

I know. I know just how cold that person is. And I know that his temperament suits the title of Ice Palace Lord all too well.

I could not even meet the Poison King and the Demon Buddha. The two of them had gone deep into the Thousand Poisons Forest again to dig up poisonous herbs.

“This is too much! How can the two of you always have fun without me?”

The words I left with Sangseon, asking him to be sure to pass them on, merely echoed through the Thousand Poisons Forest.

Of course, although I said that, I could feel that the two of them spending time together was having a positive influence on all of them.

Unexpectedly, the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable calmly revealed her true feelings.

“I hope there will be a day when I can go out with you as well.”

It seemed she had envied the sight of me spending time with the other Demon Venerables.

I gave her the words that would strengthen her the most.

“You are the first Demon Venerable of the next generation. When the time comes, there will be many such opportunities.”

Seophona, do not forget that right now is the time to bury your life in effort. Only by having such a period can you become the Demon Venerable you are looking at now. Do you think the Fist Demon’s massive fists and the Wicked Smiling Demon’s shining mask were made for nothing?

I could only hope that she would grow well into a magnificent Demon Venerable.

And the person who moved me more than anyone else was the Wicked Smiling Demon.

When I entered the Wicked Smiling Demon’s residence, I was startled.

The room had changed. Against the wall opposite the bed I had brought in, a new bed had been placed. And astonishingly, the Wicked Smiling Demon was sitting on that bed. He, who had always stood in the middle of the empty room, was sitting on a bed.

“Wicked Smiling Demon!”

“You have come.”

“So you finally have a bed of your own.”

“I took the opportunity to acquire one.”

I was truly happy. He had understood the heart with which I had moved my bed into his room. My wish for him to sleep comfortably in a bed.

“Sleeping on a bed is more comfortable and pleasant than I expected.”

I sat on my own bed and looked at the Wicked Smiling Demon sitting on his bed across from me.

Because his place had changed, the view he looked upon would also change. This change, in turn, would change him as well. Just as I was, the Smiling Demon was also growing stronger little by little.

“I intend to go to the North Sea.”

“For what matter are you going?”

“I am going to see a pretty heart.”

When I first used the expression pretty heart, the Wicked Smiling Demon had been there as well. So he would know whom it referred to.

“I thought she would become stronger if I didn’t look after her, that she would become stronger if I left her alone, but it seems I’ve reached my limit now. I must go see her.”

The two eyes behind the white mask smiled attractively. Recalling the joke with which he had once teased Ian, he said,

“If the heart you speak of is that ugly heart I know, then it should be beating well even in a cold place.”

* * *

That night, Chwima came to see me.

“Shall we have a drink?”

“Sounds good.”

Whenever I drank with Chwima, we had always drunk at the Chwimong Pavilion, but today we sat facing each other in the yard before my residence.

“I’m going to the North Sea, even if I have to go alone. You know Ian, the one who used to be my guard? She’s there right now. And just then, you brought up the Ice Palace, so I suddenly wanted to go see her.”

“The reason I said I wouldn’t go with you is…”

It seemed that had been weighing on his mind. He had always grumbled that I never spent time with him, but when I actually suggested going, he had ended up refusing.

“There is a place inside the Ice Palace where they brew wine. It is called the Hundred Wines Snow Garden. I know the Garden Master there.”

Just in case, I asked,

“Is the Garden Master a woman?”

Chwima flinched and nodded. Brother, don’t be surprised already. I can roughly guess what the situation is.

“She likes you, doesn’t she?”

Chwima did not answer and drank his wine. It was a silent affirmation.

An affection between Chwima and a master brewer of the North Sea Ice Palace. Chwima must not have accepted those feelings for a long time. And by coincidence, the wine had stopped coming at this very time when he had opened his heart to Yeobin, so he must have been thinking that the woman was hurt.

“You think she heard that things had gotten good between you and Yeobin and stopped sending the wine, don’t you?”

But this time, he firmly shook his head.

“No. If she had sent wine out of such private feelings, I would not have accepted her wine.”

I could feel the sincerity in Chwima’s words.

“The wine the Garden Master sends is truly excellent. We have interacted as one who makes wine and one who drinks it.”

A master of brewing and the finest drinker.

That was the sort of relationship the two had.

“Then why did you say you wouldn’t go?”

At that, Chwima said something unexpected.

“The wine of the Wine Essence spoiled again.”

Once before, I had helped Chwima clear away a Wine Essence that had rotted. Since it had never happened even once after he became a Demon Venerable, Chwima had called it ominous.

That Wine Essence had spoiled again.

“So I have brewed it again and am watching over it. It didn’t feel right to leave at a time like this, so that’s why I said I wouldn’t go. I was going to tell you earlier, but I didn’t because I thought you might worry for no reason.”

I could tell. Though many things had changed because I had regressed, I had still not yet changed the fate in which Hwa Mugi was approaching.

“Was it the same as before?”

“What do you mean?”

“Did the stench of rot spread as badly as it did back then?”

Fortunately, Chwima shook his head.

“It spoiled less than before. Back then, it was terrible, wasn’t it?”

I did not know what meaning there could be in this difference, but if I had given meaning to it in the first place, then I could place meaning on this difference too. I would believe that the situation had improved.

The wine brewed this time would surely spoil less. This conference must have changed our fates a little more as well.

“Brother, don’t worry too much. If it spoils, you can brew it again. If it spoils again, you can brew it again after that.”

Brew it, and brew it again. That, after all, was exactly my attitude toward facing Hwa Mugi and toward evil. I would not attach great meaning to it.

Hwa Mugi, I will not let you become my everything. You are the one who should fret and obsess over me. Why doesn’t it work? Why doesn’t it take? Be anxious and burn with frustration.

Perhaps the greatest revenge was not killing him, but living happily and well myself.

Hwa Mugi? Who was that again?

* * *

Early the next day, as I was about to leave the main cult, Chwima came to see me.

“Let’s go together.”

Chwima had already come prepared for travel.

Even his clothing was different from usual. It was the same outfit he had worn on the day of the conference, but the painting was different.

Back then, it had been a robe showing a drunken Wine God laughing, but today, the Wine God had floated a boat on a lakeside beneath the moon and was drinking alone. It was like a scene from a poem and painting.

“That outfit is really stylish. If you have any spare clothes, give me a set too.”

“I’ll give you one when we return.”

I did not ask why he had changed his mind and decided to go. There are things you can know without asking.

“Let’s run to the North Sea with lightness skill. I’ll match your speed, brother.”

At that, Chwima looked dumbfounded.

“You’ll match my speed?”

“Of course. The faster person has to match the slower person’s speed.”

“You’ve never properly run with me, have you?”

“That goes for you too, brother.”

“Shall we make a bet?”

A lightness skill bet with me? That was almost like suggesting to my father that we bet on who could sneer better.

“We are people who drink and fight. What do we do if we drink and cause trouble? We run, of course. We are people with expertise in running away.”

Chwima was full of confidence.

“If I lose, I’ll give you this.”

Chwima took a small wine bottle from his robes.

“It is Fire Dragon Wine.”

Among spirit medicines made into wine, Fire Dragon Wine belonged to the very best. It was truly precious wine.

“You’ll give me something this precious?”

“I only kept it because it doesn’t suit my internal energy or temperament.”

“You’re doing this because you think I’d feel burdened if you just gave it to me as a gift, aren’t you?”

“Just don’t fall over and cry!”

Chwima had wagered something truly big. The fact that he had first put up the Fire Dragon Wine meant there was something he wanted from me as well.

“And if you win, brother? Is there something you want?”

The condition Chwima put forward was unexpected.

“If I win, grant me one request.”

“What is it?”

“I’ll tell you if I win.”

“What a pity. It seems I won’t even get to hear that request.”

After deciding on our destination, we began to run.

Chwima certainly was fast. Among the Demon Venerables, he might well be the fastest in lightness skill. If he was this fast, then he had not intended to give me the Fire Dragon Wine. This was a bet he had meant to win.

Unfortunately for him, however, he was not as fast as my perfected Swift Steps.

Brother, since I am at least a man with a conscience, I’ll listen to what your request is while drinking the Fire Dragon Wine.

Fast, and faster still. Like that, we sped swiftly northward.

Chapter 397: They Said It Was a Pretty Heart.
---------------------------------------------
The Drunken Demon’s qinggong was fast.

The speed he had first shown had been only a fraction of his true ability. The more he ran, the faster he became.

‘Just how much trouble did he get into while drunk to become this fast?’

He was fast enough to make one want to joke like that. That was how the Demon Venerables were. They would drink together, crack every kind of joke, even leap into a lake and swim together, yet never once had he mentioned that his qinggong was this swift.

The other Demon Venerables were the same. Far from boasting about their martial arts, they almost never even brought them up. That was the truly frightening thing about real masters.

So? Did an unexpected reversal occur?

Unfortunately, no such thing happened. No matter how fast the Drunken Demon was, Geom Mugeuk’s Swift Step had surpassed the perfected tenth stage and reached the eleventh.

“I’m going on ahead!”

They had been running at about the same pace, but as they neared their destination, Geom Mugeuk increased his speed. He did not hold back, nor did he let his guard down. With Fire Dragon Wine on the line, how could he?

Geom Mugeuk unleashed the Swift Step to its utmost limit and arrived at the appointed place first.

A short while later, the Drunken Demon arrived, panting for breath.

“What are you? Why are you so fast?”

“I saved my strength for the final moment.”

The Drunken Demon knew that was not why he had lost. He was gasping for breath, while Geom Mugeuk was breathing as calmly as usual. In other words, he had never been a match for him to begin with.

For all he knew, if Geom Mugeuk had shown his true ability from the start, he might have gone to a nearby village, bought snacks to go with the wine, laid them out, and waited.

“I’ve never lost in qinggong before.”

“I’m the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

Geom Mugeuk looked around with an air of smugness.

“Now then, where would be a good place to drink our Fire Dragon?”

He wondered if the Drunken Demon would truly give him the Fire Dragon Wine.

“Follow me.”

The Drunken Demon flew off somewhere using qinggong.

Geom Mugeuk followed after him. This time, he used the Heavenly Demon Flight Art he had been practicing whenever he found the chance. With his back straight and even his arms crossed, he flew after him in an openly lofty posture.

Truly, it was a qinggong that suited Father all too well. To fly in with such an aloof stance, then descend slowly by walking through the air! Ah, this was it!

No matter how much faster the Swift Step was, this was the reason he could not give up on the Heavenly Demon Flight Art.

The place the Drunken Demon brought Geom Mugeuk to was a small rock jutting out from a cliff overlooking magnificent scenery.

“On my way back to the cult, I sometimes have a drink here.”

Far off in the distance, Dacheonsan could be seen, and the river flowing before it shattered the sunlight and glittered. Reed fields spread out again around the river, so beautiful it truly seemed like a scene from a painting.

“If you’re going to drink Fire Dragon Wine, you should drink it in a place like this.”

The Drunken Demon took the Fire Dragon Wine from his robe and held it out to Geom Mugeuk.

“You’re really giving it to me?”

“Then should I not? We made a wager.”

“But it’s Fire Dragon Wine.”

“I still have to give it to you.”

“You joined hands with my brother, didn’t you? So you laced it with Formless Deadly Poison here, didn’t you?”

“Looks like you don’t want to drink it. Then never mind.”

“As if!”

As the Drunken Demon tried to put it back into his robe, Geom Mugeuk quickly snatched the Fire Dragon Wine from him.

The Drunken Demon took a cup from his sleeve.

“This cup will yield exactly three drinks. It is more effective to drink it in portions than all at once, so divide it into three and drink it.”

The Drunken Demon had truly run with all his might, but seeing that he had even brought a cup, Geom Mugeuk thought perhaps he had brought this wine intending to give it to him as a gift after all.

The Drunken Demon took out another cup and poured liquor from the gourd-shaped blood flask hanging at his waist. When outside, he drank any liquor just fine, but when it came to cups, he tried to drink from his own whenever possible.

“Now then, I admit defeat.”

After clinking his cup vigorously against the one the Drunken Demon held out, Geom Mugeuk drank the Fire Dragon Wine. A scorching energy, as if it would burn his insides, slid down his throat. He understood why he had been told to divide it into three portions.

Geom Mugeuk immediately began circulating his qi and dissolving that blazing energy. It was an extreme yang energy among extreme yang energies, but Geom Mugeuk began absorbing it into the meridians throughout his body without much strain.

When he dissolved all of the energy with a single full circulation, the Drunken Demon was astonished. He knew Geom Mugeuk’s martial arts were powerful, but he had not expected him to dissolve one cup of Fire Dragon Wine with a single circulation.

They toasted again and he drank the second cup as well.

“Ugh, that’s good!”

The second cup also set his entire body ablaze. The more he drank, the more fiercely it burned, but not to the point his body could not endure it.

In that way, Geom Mugeuk drank all of the Fire Dragon Wine in three portions and dissolved all the energy contained within it.

The pure and majestic energy that had been circulating through the meridians of his entire body became internal energy and gathered in his dantian.

It was a greater amount than he had expected.

Even before, Geom Mugeuk had possessed more internal energy than anyone, but he needed even more. These days, he was cultivating the Space-Time Transposition Art, the Sicheon Secret Art, and even the Nine Transformations Demon Art.

The more internal energy he had, the shorter his training time became, so even a handful of internal energy was welcome without question.

After completing a circulation of true qi, Geom Mugeuk’s two eyes held an even clearer and deeper light than before, which then faded away.

Watching him, the Drunken Demon shook his head and said,

“I’ll have to rewrite the part about the Fire Dragon Wine.”

“What do you mean?”

“The secret manual passed down through the generations says it takes three days to absorb all of the Fire Dragon Wine.”

The Drunken Demon had expected that, even if not three days, Geom Mugeuk would at least spend a day here circulating his qi. And yet Geom Mugeuk had absorbed all of the Fire Dragon Wine’s energy before even half a shichen had passed.

“What are you? Just what are you that you keep startling people like this?”

At the question, half jest and half sincerity, Geom Mugeuk spoke in a voice full of dignity.

“I am the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. And who might you be?”

The Drunken Demon played along with Geom Mugeuk’s banter.

“I am a drunkard who lost a wager and was stripped clean of his Fire Dragon Wine. Future generations! Never wager on qinggong with the Heavenly Demon Hall!”

The two looked at each other and laughed.

“Brother, thank you.”

“What’s there to thank me for? You won it in a wager.”

“What’s your request?”

“Why so suddenly?”

“Even if it hadn’t been a wager, I should grant your request. Say it! I’ll do anything.”

“Anything?”

“Even if you tell me to go spill your liquor on Father’s bed, I’ll do it.”

“That’s not granting a request, that’s assassination.”

They bantered like that for a while, but in the end the Drunken Demon did not state his request.

“It’s fine. I’ll tell you later if the chance comes.”

Since he seemed unwilling to say it now, Geom Mugeuk did not press him further.

“Let’s have another qinggong wager next time.”

“Next time, I request Longevity Wine.”

Longevity Wine, too, was a rare wine that could grant internal energy no less valuable than Fire Dragon Wine.

“Shall we go to the Ice Palace first?”

Geom Mugeuk was being considerate of the Drunken Demon, but he did not accept that either.

“If not for you, it was a place I wouldn’t even have thought of going. Meet your heart first. You do know where that heart is right now, don’t you?”

“If I knew where she was, I wouldn’t go. I’m going because I don’t know where she is or what she’s doing, and I’m worried.”

“How are you going to find her?”

Geom Mugeuk gazed at the river far away, sparkling like jewels in the sunlight.

“Don’t worry. That girl… if anything, it’ll be harder not to find her.”

* * *

The two ran and ran toward the North Sea.

If they came across an inn along the way, they stayed there; if not, they camped outdoors.

Geom Mugeuk displayed his skill at camping. When he expertly dressed the beast he had hunted, sprinkled it with a secret seasoning, and served it, the Drunken Demon was greatly impressed.

Seeing him rejoice that it was perfect as a drinking snack, Geom Mugeuk felt apologetic. It was a dish he had made several times for the other Demon Venerables. He had even taught his guards how to camp and cook.

And so, sharing the stories they had not yet told, they ran north.

They ran and camped, ran and drank. After running without pause like that, the two finally arrived at the North Sea.

“Snow.”

Spring had come to the Central Plains, but snow lay piled in the North Sea.

“I was disappointed that there wasn’t much snow this year. Let’s enjoy it to our hearts’ content before we go back.”

Watching the excited Geom Mugeuk, the Drunken Demon thought inwardly,

‘Will we really be able to enjoy ourselves and go back?’

For some reason, being with Geom Mugeuk made him feel an instinctive sense of crisis, as if something was bound to happen. It was just his imagination, right?

The place the two arrived at was the village from which the last news of Ian had come. It was a little removed from the center of the North Sea, so there were not many outsiders.

The villagers wore fur hats and clothes made from animal hides. Trees and grasses that could not be seen in the Central Plains grew here and there. The North Sea certainly had its own unique sense of mystery.

‘Why did Ian come all the way to this distant place?’

She must have come here for a reason.

At the appearance of the two men in thin martial robes, the villagers’ gazes gathered on them. Passersby, shop clerks, even people who had stuck their heads out by the windows, all silently watched the two strangers.

“Let’s go there.”

It was, unusually, a tavern made from a cave.

When the two entered the tavern, several customers drinking inside looked at them all at once. Every gaze was wary.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk and the Drunken Demon paid them no mind.

When they sat down, a waiter came over and greeted them.

“Welcome!”

“Bring us liquor and something simple to eat.”

“Please wait a moment!”

The Drunken Demon, who had been looking around the interior, glanced up at the ceiling and said,

“I don’t think I’ll even get drunk. Not when I don’t know when those things above my head might fall.”

At the Drunken Demon’s words, Geom Mugeuk raised his head. From various places in the cave ceiling, icicles stretched down long and sharp like spears.

“If we break those off and put them in the liquor, it’d be nice and cool.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Drunken Demon laughed and said they should try it.

A short while later, the waiter brought the liquor and dishes. There was no need to break any icicles. The cups were frozen solid.

“If you drink from these, the liquor will be cold.”

Though it was cold outside, everyone was drinking chilled liquor rather than warmed wine.

“See here.”

“Yes, sir.”

“Let me ask you something. Has a woman wearing a veil and carrying a sword come here?”

“No. No one like that has come.”

But Geom Mugeuk saw it. In that fleeting instant, the waiter had flinched. No matter how seasoned a waiter he was, he could not avoid Geom Mugeuk’s sharp eyes.

“We’re friends of that lady xia. We’re not bad people, so tell us.”

The Drunken Demon cut in and added a word.

“Look at our faces. What part of these faces looks villainous?”

“In my experience, whether someone is a villain or not has nothing to do with how they look. In any case, I have never seen such a person.”

After the waiter withdrew, the Drunken Demon said,

“Why did he say he didn’t know when he clearly did?”

The Drunken Demon had also noticed that the waiter was lying.

“He tried to hide it because we looked like villains.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Drunken Demon touched his own face.

“This face? This handsome face? What part of it!”

The Drunken Demon was indeed handsome. Wherever he went, he had the face of a handsome middle-aged man.

The Poison King, the Extreme Evil Young Demon with his mask off, the Drunken Demon, and me. If the four of us walked around together, I’m certain we’d be called the Four Handsome Men or the Four Heavenly Kings.
