“If that’s not it, then she’s involved with dangerous people whose names he can’t even speak.”

That was likely the case. Otherwise, there would be no need to hide the fact that he had seen Ian.

When the two had drunk a few cups, the waiter brought the food. It was a dish from the North Sea region that could not be seen in the Central Plains.

As the waiter set down the food, Geom Mugeuk casually placed a silver piece on the table. The waiter flinched in surprise. It was far too large a sum to be a tip from a customer thanking him for his trouble. It was money he would have to work at least ten days to earn.

Quick-witted as he was, the waiter understood. He was to tell them about that lady xia and take it.

“This is your first time here, isn’t it?”

“It is.”

“I’ll tell you some places nearby worth seeing.”

The waiter told them of places worth visiting in the area.

“If you go five li east, there is a place where snow blossoms bloom. It’s a magnificent sight you won’t see in the Central Plains. Surinsa Temple to the south is also worth visiting. Be careful not to slip when you climb the stairs.”

After mentioning a few more places, he added at the end,

“Ah, and do not go near the manor to the west under any circumstances. It is a dangerous place.”

That was enough. He had told them in this way that Ian had come here and gone to that manor.

The waiter pocketed the money and went toward the kitchen. It was a clever answer. Even if trouble arose later, he had not said a single word about Ian.

After leaving the tavern, Geom Mugeuk and the Drunken Demon headed west.

Following the waiter’s directions, they found that there truly was a manor there.

As they entered through the main gate, the Drunken Demon looked around the interior and said,

“Didn’t you say the person you’re looking for was a pretty heart?”

A single line entered their eyes.

The trees, the stone lantern standing in the courtyard, even the rear wall. They had all been sliced in a long, straight line. As if measured with a ruler and cut with perfect precision, it was clear a clean single strike had been unleashed.

And on the ground remained bloodstains that had not yet been cleaned away. Presumably, those caught along that line had been cut apart in the same way.

The Drunken Demon pulled his collar closed as if he felt cold and said,

“That’s not a pretty heart. That’s a terrifying heart, isn’t it?”

Chapter 398: One Who Opens Their Heart to No One in the World.
--------------------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk examined the cross-section of the severed stone lantern.

Up close, the cut was not as smooth as he had expected. And yet, a smile formed on Geom Mugeuk’s lips.

‘The Bicheon Sword Art is trying to go beyond nine-tenths mastery.’

It was like the final period of confusion that came just before reaching Great Completion. It was a natural phenomenon that usually occurred in those who had advanced their martial arts too quickly; if they failed to reach Great Completion in this state, most ended up regressing below their original level. In this way, martial arts tested warriors through all sorts of methods.

Geom Mugeuk believed in Ian. He knew well how much blood, sweat, and effort she had poured in for this result. Ian’s efforts would never betray her.

Next, he examined the bloodstains on the floor.

‘Ian was angry.’

She had cut down those who attacked her without mercy. With Ian’s personality, she would never commit unnecessary slaughter, so they must have been people who deserved to die.

As Geom Mugeuk looked around, studying the traces left behind and the bloodstains, he pictured that day’s battle in his mind. It was possible because his martial realm was so high, and because he knew Ian’s swordsmanship better than anyone.

He imagined them rushing at her from every direction to kill her, and Ian drawing her sword and cutting them down. The imagined blood sprayed by the falling enemies landed precisely upon the bloodstains left in reality, merging with them.

Geom Mugeuk moved, following Ian’s movements exactly.

“She cut down two here, then stabbed the one charging from this side. Turned back, blocked an attack, then stabbed the heart.”

When Ian turned her head, she saw those who were about to throw hidden weapons.

As she unleashed sword energy, the ones about to throw the hidden weapons fell, and even the tree and stone lantern behind them were cut through. The stone lantern in Geom Mugeuk’s imagination merged with the stone lantern now lying on the ground.

The Drunken Demon was amazed by Geom Mugeuk’s explanation. He too could roughly guess what had happened, but to reconstruct the scene so precisely?

Geom Mugeuk slowly entered the building.

The interior was empty. There were traces of fighting there as well. Bloodstains remained here and there along the corridor floor.

When they finally arrived at the main hall, Geom Mugeuk said,

“The last fight happened here. She cut down the two who blocked her path. She deflected the hidden weapon that flew from behind that pillar and eliminated the person standing here.”

Geom Mugeuk walked slowly inward.

“She faced the leader here.”

Geom Mugeuk quietly examined the area around the chair.

“She didn’t kill him. The fact that she didn’t kill him means she was trying to find something out from him. In other words, the man here wasn’t her final target. There’s probably trouble at one of the major sects around the North Sea. We’ll have to find that place.”

Though he was implying that the problem had grown even larger, Geom Mugeuk did not seem particularly worried.

“Aren’t you worried?”

“About whom? Ian? Or her opponent?”

After joking like that, Geom Mugeuk revealed his true thoughts.

“I would’ve been worried if she’d gotten caught up in something vague. If there hadn’t been any particular traces here, I’d have been more worried instead. But right now, I can feel a clear objective. Ian is moving according to a plan.”

The Drunken Demon shook his head with an expression that said, I admit defeat. Geom Mugeuk was certainly different.

And was it only today? He had experienced this uniqueness many times on the journey here, which was why the time he spent with Geom Mugeuk was so interesting and enjoyable. Was that why? He even felt a strange sense of stability.

He used to feel this stability when they drank together at Chimong Pavilion, but now that they had left the cult, he understood exactly what that feeling was.

Normally, he should have been a Demon Sovereign on high alert, responsible for the Young Cult Leader’s safety, but instead, he felt as though he were the one being protected. It felt like traveling comfortably with a close younger brother. Yes, Geom Mugeuk turned this perilous journey through the jianghu into a trip.

Following Geom Mugeuk as he strode out, the Drunken Demon shouted,

“You were there that day too, weren’t you? You saw everything, didn’t you? And now you’re acting all impressive about it, right?”

* * *

When the Black Fan Gang Leader, Zhou Kui, came to his senses, he was in a place like a dark warehouse. His immobilizing acupoint had been sealed, so he could not move at all. Fortunately, his mute acupoint had not been sealed, so he could still speak.

‘Why am I...?’

Then he suddenly remembered.

‘Ah! I was kidnapped, wasn’t I?’

He could not believe he had been kidnapped. A woman wearing a bamboo hat had stormed into the manor, cut down his subordinates in an instant, and subdued him.

Because she had been wearing even a veil with the bamboo hat, he had not been able to confirm her face. No, even if she had not worn the veil, her movements had been so fast that he likely would not have seen her face anyway.

If killing him had not been her purpose, she should have at least asked him something. But the woman had asked nothing, merely subdued him and thrown him here.

‘What is this about?’

He had committed so many evil deeds in his life that he could not guess which grudge this was about. What mattered now was that, in any case, he had survived.

‘You shouldn’t have left me alive. I’ll make you understand just who you dared to touch.’

He had reliable backing. In particular, he was currently carrying out an important matter with one party. Once they learned that his manor had been attacked, they would release experts to find him.

The Northern Blood Sect.

The Northern Blood Sect was one of the Three Great Powers of the North Sea. Among them, the North Sea Ice Palace boasted by far the most overwhelming strength, while the remaining two, the Northern Blood Sect and the Ice Sword Sect, stood as rivals on equal footing.

The three factions were both friends and enemies; internally, they fought for supremacy, but when pressure came from the Central Plains, they joined hands and became friends.

In particular, the Northern Blood Sect was a gathering place for warriors of cruel and ferocious temperament, a place even the North Sea Ice Palace did not touch recklessly.

The person Zhou Kui had joined hands with was Yang Zhong, the second son of the Northern Blood Sect Master.

‘Before you even try to touch Yang Zhong, your own head will fall first, wench.’

He believed Yang Zhong would soon come to save him. To Yang Zhong, he was a business partner who absolutely had to be rescued. For Yang Zhong, the second son, to become the successor of the Northern Blood Sect, he had to make this venture succeed.

It was right then.

“Uuugh.”

A groan came from beside him. Startled, he turned his head and saw someone sitting in a chair a short distance away. It had been dark, he could not use his internal energy, and on top of that, he had been focused on the fact that he had been captured, so he had not realized someone else was there with him.

Zhou Kui carefully examined the other person. The outline of the person’s face emerged.

“Hrk!”

After confirming who it was, Zhou Kui let out a short scream.

“Young Master Yang!”

Astonishingly, it was Yang Zhong. It would have been wonderful if he had come to rescue him, but he too had been captured and brought here.

How can you, the one who’s supposed to save me, be captured here?

“Come to your senses! Young Master Yang!”

Only after he shouted several times did Yang Zhong wake from sleep.

“Zhou Kui! How dare you!”

He misunderstood, thinking Zhou Kui had captured him. He had just come to his senses, and the surroundings were dark, so he had only recognized Zhou Kui’s voice.

“You misunderstand! I was captured too.”

To avoid an unnecessary misunderstanding, Zhou Kui shouted.

In response to the silence of distrust, Zhou Kui spoke quickly.

“The person who kidnapped Young Master Yang was a woman wearing a bamboo hat, wasn’t she?”

At the words “a woman wearing a bamboo hat,” Yang Zhong recalled the one who had kidnapped him.

That was right. It had been a woman wearing a bamboo hat. The woman had subdued his escort warriors in an instant, and her movements had been so fast that he had not been able to tell what method she used. He had not even properly seen her face.

“Then you too?”

“My manor was wiped out.”

Yang Zhong did not guess who was behind this. He was certain.

“It was my elder brother. This is definitely his doing.”

There was no one here in the North Sea who would dare touch him. If he had to name exceptions, they would be the North Sea Ice Palace and the Ice Sword Sect.

If it were the North Sea Ice Palace, they would not need to kidnap him in this manner to achieve what they wanted. A single sentence telling him to come to the North Sea Ice Palace by a certain date would have been enough for him to obey. Above all, kidnapping was not their way.

The possibility of the Ice Sword Sect was also slim. They were the ones who had joined hands with them to keep the North Sea Ice Palace in check. Their relationship was like teeth and lips. Both sides knew well that without the lips, the teeth would be cold.

But Zhou Kui seemed doubtful that the one who had done this was the eldest young master of the Northern Blood Sect.

“He would know very well that if Young Master Yang were kidnapped, he himself would be suspected immediately. I don’t think he would do something like this. Besides, I’ve never heard of a female expert like that under the eldest young master.”

“Then who on earth do you think did this?”

After pondering for a moment, Zhou Kui lowered his voice and spoke in secret.

“There is the matter you and I are carrying out, young master, is there not?”

Yang Zhong’s expression hardened. There was indeed a matter he had been carrying out in partnership with Zhou Kui. If they had been captured together, it occurred to him that it might be because of that matter.

“No matter who did this, we’ll be released soon. The sect will know that I’ve been kidnapped and will have begun searching. And we also have Huang Chu.”

Zhou Kui knew well of Huang Chu. He was Yang Zhong’s right hand and an expert in tracking arts. Not only was he skilled in tracking, but his martial ability was also outstanding, so when something like this happened, Huang Chu would step forward and resolve it.

“We can trust Hero Huang.”

“So do not worry. They’ll soon learn that leaving us alive was a grave mistake. Once I get out of here later, I’ll flay every bone from that wench while she’s still alive.”

It was right then that a groan came from behind them, as if someone had awakened from sleep.

Startled by the fact that there was yet another person there, the two turned around. It felt as though someone were hiding in the darkness, waking them one by one at perfectly timed moments.

As anxiety surged in, Zhou Kui pleaded inwardly.

‘Surely not? It can’t be, right? Please!’

By now, the two had grown accustomed to the darkness. Their shock immediately turned into a sigh.

The person leaning against the wall in the rear corner, his immobilizing acupoint sealed, was none other than Huang Chu.

Seeing that he had been captured here, it seemed he had been searching for them. Though that was precisely the problem.

Then who’s supposed to find you?

* * *

Geom Mugeuk and the Drunken Demon were sitting in a tavern in a village of the North Sea where many people came and went. The two had moved here from the first village where the manor had been. It was because they predicted that the next incident would surely happen in this region where the major sects were located.

Sure enough, they were able to hear the news they wanted here. Many people were talking about it.

“They say the second young master of the Northern Blood Sect has gone missing.”

“I heard too. Apparently, the Northern Blood Sect has been turned upside down because of it.”

Geom Mugeuk and the Drunken Demon listened to their conversation from a short distance away.

Pouring wine into Geom Mugeuk’s cup, the Drunken Demon said,

“To take on the Northern Blood Sect alone. She really has guts, doesn’t she?”

Both Geom Mugeuk and the Drunken Demon knew of the Northern Blood Sect. It was one of the three representative powers of the North Sea, after all.

“Ian takes after me, so she’s fearless.”

“What on earth is going on for her to even kidnap the bloodline of the Northern Blood Sect?”

“Maybe she wanted to make her first journey through the jianghu a proper one.”

The Drunken Demon sipped his wine and asked with interest,

“So? Are you just going to watch like this?”

Now that she had touched the bloodline of the Northern Blood Sect Master, they would not simply let this matter pass. In terms of the number of experts and their overall scale, they were absolutely not an opponent to be taken lightly.

“For now.”

Even so, he intended to watch how she handled it. He knew better than anyone just how important this experience was.

Ian, how will you resolve this?

Just then, the people on the street began to stir.

“They’re experts from the Ice Palace!”

Looking outside, they saw warriors dressed in white walking toward them from afar.

The North Sea Ice Palace.

They were said to be the foremost among the mysterious forces of the murim. They belonged neither to the orthodox, unorthodox, nor demonic factions, and had walked their own independent path. The fact that they had not fallen throughout that long history proved their strength.

The aura emitted by the Ice Palace warriors was clear and cold.

In particular, the young woman at the very front showed what cold beauty truly was. Geom Mugeuk knew who she was.

Han Xue, the only daughter of the North Sea Ice Palace Lord.

She was said to have inherited the merciless temperament of the current Ice Palace Lord. It was said that she had never opened her heart to anyone in her life. Rumor had it that she trusted no one in the world, not even her own parents. All rumors related to her were this fierce and cold.

Behind her followed more than ten experts. Every one of them was an Ice Palace expert who exuded an extraordinary presence.

As they walked, drawing everyone’s gaze, they stopped in front of the tavern.

They had been walking toward the tavern from the very beginning. After leaving her subordinates waiting outside, Han Xue entered the tavern alone.

When she entered, the guests lowered their heads and did not even make a sound as they breathed. With each step she took, a chill settled over the room.

Han Xue stopped before Geom Mugeuk. Whether her nature was as the rumors said, he could not know, but at least one thing was certain. If the phrase “cold charm” existed, it was surely meant for her.

Gazing at Geom Mugeuk with clear, cold eyes, Han Xue spoke instead of offering a greeting.

“Young Cult Leader, how about a cup of ice wine with me?”

Chapter 399: I Came to See You After So Long.
---------------------------------------------
It was a provocative first greeting.

The suggestion that they drink ice wine was something that, under normal circumstances, should have come only after she had formally greeted Geom Mugeuk and then paid her respects to Chwima as well.

But she said it as her very first words. She must have calculated that Chwima would not take it as rude. After all, it was a greeting related to alcohol. In other words, she had come knowing that the person with him was Chwima.

It was also a moment that showed just how excellent the Ice Palace’s intelligence network was here in the North Sea.

If they knew about them, there was a chance they also knew about Ian’s movements. Geom Mugeuk knew that would never work in Ian’s favor.

The North Sea Ice Palace was certainly not an enemy of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, but neither was it a friend.

Geom Mugeuk silently gazed at Han Seol. How different was the woman in the rumors from the woman before him?

“Do you like wine?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Han Seol looked slightly surprised, then answered calmly.

“I like drinking alone.”

At her frank answer, a faint smile formed at the corners of Chwima’s mouth. After all, there was no one who enjoyed drinking alone more than he did.

“Pay your respects. This is Chwima, the Demon Venerable of our cult.”

Han Seol cupped her fist toward Chwima with proper courtesy.

“It is an honor to meet such an esteemed person.”

“So the rumors that the precious daughter of the Ice Palace is truly beautiful were true.”

“You flatter me.”

Unlike the rumors that called her heartless and cold, Han Seol did not seem very different from an ordinary woman her age. But Geom Mugeuk knew.

Coldness and heartlessness were not revealed in outward appearance, but in decisions and choices.

That was why he could not hold any preconceptions about her yet. He did not yet know what kind of person she was.

Han Seol’s gaze turned back to Geom Mugeuk.

“You still haven’t answered my question.”

Geom Mugeuk left the decision to Chwima.

“Would you care for a drink?”

For a moment, Han Seol found it strange. If the Young Cult Leader and a Demon Venerable were present, then naturally the Young Cult Leader should be the one to decide. Just as she, not the experts waiting outside, made all decisions.

Yet this Young Cult Leader, instead of deciding himself, was asking Chwima for his decision.

This time, Chwima looked at Han Seol.

Geom Mugeuk could feel it. Chwima at this moment was not the pathetic elder brother Chwima whom he usually scolded.

There was a world seen by a man who had reached the ultimate realm of martial arts and life through alcohol, and there was a life as seen by him. What kind of person did she appear to him now?

“If it is ice wine, I cannot refuse.”

At Chwima’s answer, Han Seol signaled to the martial artists standing outside. Then her trusted attendant, Chan, entered carrying a pure white wine bottle and handed it to her.

“This is ice wine brewed in our palace.”

When the stopper was removed from the bottle, an enchanting fragrance spread all around. The drinkers in the tavern, who had been holding their breath, wore moved expressions.

There were many kinds of ice wine in the North Sea, but the ice wine brewed by the North Sea Ice Palace was regarded as the very best. This fragrant, bewitching scent was precisely the aroma of that North Sea Ice Palace ice wine.

Drip, drip.

Han Seol poured a cup for Geom Mugeuk first.

As she poured the wine, she recalled what her mother, the Palace Master of the North Sea Ice Palace, had told her.

—The Young Cult Leader is no ordinary man.

Han Seol had also heard about Geom Mugeuk several times. They said he was changing the landscape of the Murim.

—You must deal with him carefully. If you do not wish to be devoured by him.

Her mother had never said such words to her before when giving her a task. She felt that her mother regarded the Young Cult Leader more highly than herself.

‘Is this man really that remarkable?’

The sight of him grinning and saying how wonderful the wine’s aroma was made him seem almost frivolous instead. No matter how she looked at him, she could not see any authority in him that overwhelmed a Demon Venerable.

Han Seol next poured wine for Chwima.

Finally, it was Chwima who filled her cup.

“I, too, mostly drink alone.”

And so the three raised their cups and drank the ice wine.

“Ah, it really is delicious.”

Geom Mugeuk was sincerely impressed. He had drunk ice wine before his regression as well, but it had not tasted this good then. As expected, even the same wine could taste so different depending on whom one drank it with and under what circumstances.

“How is it that you do not suspect it might be poisoned?”

At Han Seol’s question, Geom Mugeuk looked at Chwima. Fine, in my case, I can say it is because I am immune to all poisons. But is it all right for you to drink so carelessly, Brother?

Chwima answered Han Seol.

“If you put poison in food or water, I might fail to notice. But poison mixed into wine, even if it were a formless poison, I would know. I could tell just by smelling it.”

“As expected, your reputation was not undeserved.”

This time, Geom Mugeuk filled Han Seol’s cup.

“It is good to meet you.”

He did not know much about this woman. But he knew what kind of life she had lived in his life before regression. No, to be precise, he knew what kind of death she had met.

Geom Mugeuk asked Han Seol,

“I imagine there is a reason you came looking for us.”

“Yes, there is.”

Han Seol revealed the reason she had come.

“The Northern Blood Sect is a famous sect, so I trust you know of it well. The second son of the Northern Blood Sect Master has been abducted. Have you heard the news?”

“I heard people talking about it earlier.”

“I believe the one who did it is a martial artist of the Divine Cult.”

Naturally, the atmosphere turned cold and heavy.

“Why do you think so?”

“The abductor is so elusive that the Northern Blood Sect’s martial artists have been completely unable to trace him. Our palace has also stepped in, but we have not found him easily either. He is either extremely clever or someone who has received specialized training.”

You said you were anxious because she was a child left by the water? The child is building a levee and fishing by the water. Ian, well done.

“Even so, you cannot say it was done by our cult, can you?”

“There is decisive evidence.”

Han Seol stared fixedly at Geom Mugeuk and added,

“You, Young Cult Leader. You are the evidence.”

Even at these provocative words, Geom Mugeuk remained calm.

“Why am I the evidence?”

“Because you appeared in the North Sea at such a coincidental time.”

“Circumstantial evidence, then.”

“That’s right. Evidence that cannot be verified if the Young Cult Leader denies it.”

Geom Mugeuk could understand why Han Seol was evaluated as cold and heartless. When she spoke, she merely conveyed facts, without putting any emotion into them.

Though she said she was suspicious, she did not send him a single suspicious look or gaze.

It was not a matter of speaking much or little. Rather, because she said everything she had to say without emotion, she felt even colder.

“You are right. I also believe it was done by my subordinate.”

Geom Mugeuk readily admitted it.

“Then this will be easier to explain. This place is clearly our palace’s territory. Have your subordinate release the Northern Blood Sect Master’s son, and hand your subordinate over to us. If the abducted son of the Northern Blood Sect returns safely, we will mediate the matter properly and return your subordinate to you as well.”

Geom Mugeuk’s expression was gentle, but his refusal was firm.

“I cannot do that.”

“What do you mean?”

“If my subordinate abducted the Northern Blood Sect Master’s son, then there must have been a reason for it.”

“Yes. There must have been a reason. But the Murim is a place where an organization’s justification is more important than an individual’s reasons.”

“Could you still say that if you were the one who had to be sacrificed for the organization’s justification?”

Han Seol looked at Geom Mugeuk and said calmly,

“Of course. I would gladly give myself up.”

He felt that her words were sincere.

But the emotion he sensed in them was not a spirit of sacrifice or loyalty toward an organization. It was emptiness. A pitch-black hole that felt all the larger because she was young and beautiful.

This woman has no attachment to life.

* * *

Yang Jung could not understand this situation.

“When were you captured?”

An astonishing answer flowed from Hwang Chu’s mouth.

“Two days ago.”

“Then are you saying you were captured before us?”

“It seems so.”

In that instant, chills ran down the spines of Yang Jung and Ju Gyu, who had been listening. That meant they had first captured Hwang Chu, a master of tracking arts, and then plotted this affair. It was something they had never imagined.

“This matter was meticulously planned in advance.”

“Who on earth did this? Against the great Northern Blood Sect, who would dare!”

It was then.

Someone spoke from within the darkness.

“The important thing is not ‘who,’ but ‘why,’ is it not?”

It was a woman’s voice. It was truly the first time he had heard such a pleasant voice.

Yang Jung shouted angrily,

“Who are you?”

They had been in the same space, yet he had not even sensed her presence.

Before Yang Jung could spew curses, Ju Gyu quickly spoke.

“If you release us now, we will pretend this never happened. Let us each go our own way.”

The woman’s laughter was heard. The sound of her laughter grew louder and louder, and then the woman walking out of the darkness revealed herself. Wearing a bamboo hat and a veil, she was none other than Ian.

“Who are you, heroine?”

When Ju Gyu asked politely, Ian answered,

“I am someone who received a woman’s request and came to save her husband. I came from truly far away to get here. To think I would end up coming all the way to the North Sea.”

“Who exactly did you come to save?”

“Why? If I say his name, would you even know him?”

Ju Gyu could not answer. How could there be only one or two people who had been taken because of them?

“You people deceived people all across the Central Plains, saying you would let them earn a great deal of money, and brought them here.”

Ian had conducted a great deal of investigation in the process. This matter was not simply a case of deceiving and abducting people. They had brought only special individuals.

“All of those people had unusual constitutions, didn’t they? What exactly were you gathering them for? Were you using them as materials for some great ritual? Or were you trying to practice sorcery?”

Yang Jung and Ju Gyu gave no answer.

“I sent word to the Northern Blood Sect. If they release all the people you brought here, I will release you as well.”

Yang Jung’s expression stiffened. This affair had been carried out in utmost secrecy. To protect the secret, his father might not release the people. After all, his father valued the family more than his own blood.

Gripped by the fear that he might not be released, Yang Jung spoke as if thrashing in desperation.

“Shall I tell you what will happen if you kill us? In the end, our sect will find you, and they will kill you. Not just you, but your family as well. Your in-laws and your distant relatives, every last one of them will die because of you. And that’s not all. Even your neighbors will all be killed. Anyone who has exchanged even a single word with you will be found and killed. Only then will something like this never happen again. So go on, kill us!”

Ian quietly stared at Yang Jung. When she said nothing, he thought his threat had worked, but that was his mistake.

Ian removed her bamboo hat and took off her veil.

“Do not take it off!”

Ju Gyu shouted and squeezed his eyes shut.

It was because he feared that if he saw her face, he would be killed to silence him. Hwang Chu also closed his eyes with him.

But Yang Jung kept his eyes wide open and glared at Ian.

He had been about to hurl abuse at her, but his mouth fell open and no words came out. This damned bitch who had abducted him was far too beautiful.

From her mouth flowed words the three of them could not understand.

“I am so grateful to the young master and the people around him. Because they are people who would not be fazed in the slightest, even by a threat like this.”

Several scenes surfaced in her mind.

—You say you’re the closest person to Ian? Who are you?

—I am the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

—And you!

—I’m the Master of the Yellow Springs Pavilion.

—And you? I hear you even had a drinking gathering with her?

—I am the Demon Lord.

—And you!

—I’m the granddaughter of the Murim Alliance Leader.

—And you said you were her new father?

—……

At the absurd imagination, she laughed to herself.

“And I am so grateful that you have allowed me to say something like this.”

The smile vanished from Ian’s face. She looked coldly at Yang Jung and said,

“Make sure to tell them to come kill me. Please, be certain to leave behind a will begging them to take revenge.”

* * *

“I am someone who regards my subordinate’s reasons as more important than the organization.”

Han Seol did not believe him. No matter how plausible his words sounded, in the end, the organization would come first. And this was not just anyone, but the Young Cult Leader saying such a thing. Did he think I looked foolish enough to believe that?

“My subordinate is not the kind of person who would do something like this simply because she suffered humiliation. Someone must have asked her for help, or she must have witnessed some injustice.”

Han Seol could not understand it. Neither Geom Mugeuk, who defended his subordinate, nor the subordinate who had done such a thing to help someone else. Why did they live so hypocritically?

“You mean you cannot accept it because you believe your subordinate is in the right.”

“That is correct.”

Geom Mugeuk remained calm.

“My subordinate has spent his whole life worn out and exhausted from serving the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. At times like this, there ought to be some reward in serving the Young Cult Leader.”

Han Seol said calmly.

“The North Blood Sect has asked our palace for help. We have our own position to consider.”

“Help them.”

“Then that precious subordinate of yours may die. Would that still be all right?”

Geom Mugeuk stared intently at Han Seol.

“If my subordinate is in the right, and the scion of the North Blood Sect is in the wrong, will you still kill my subordinate?”

Han Seol stared back at Geom Mugeuk expressionlessly, then asked in return.

“Then what if the Young Cult Leader’s subordinate is in the wrong, and the scion of the North Blood Sect is in the right? In that case, would it be all right to kill your subordinate?”

Geom Mugeuk smiled faintly and said,

“In that case, I’d have to save my subordinate and escape this place.”

Han Seol looked slightly dumbfounded.

“I believe I’ve said all I have to say. Then, please talk among yourselves.”

Han Seol rose from her seat. The meeting had been meaningful if only because she had learned that this incident had been caused by a demonic practitioner.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk spoke.

“In truth, there is another reason we came to the North Sea. There is someone we very much wished to meet.”

“Who is it?”

“The Mistress of Baekju Snowfield. The Drunken Demon told me many times that she was an extraordinary person, so I have long wanted to meet her.”

Since that was the reason the Drunken Demon had come, he was trying to mention it in advance.

At that, Han Seol’s expression hardened.

“What is it?”

Han Seol looked at Geom Mugeuk and the Drunken Demon with a strange expression, then delivered shocking news.

“The Mistress of Baekju Snowfield has passed away.”

For an instant, the Drunken Demon flinched in shock. Soon after, sorrow spread across his stiffened face. He had come to meet her after all these years, and she was dead? When had he last seen her?

“When and how did she pass away?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Han Seol did not answer and only stared fixedly at the two of them. Suspicion flowed through her cold gaze.

“Why have the two of you come looking for the Mistress now, of all times?”

It was certain that one wrong word would invite misunderstanding. Since the Drunken Demon said nothing, Geom Mugeuk answered in his stead.

“Every year, the Mistress sent liquor to the Drunken Demon. This year, it did not arrive. So we came to see if something had happened to her.”

After a brief pause, Han Seol said in a low voice,

“The Mistress took her own life not long ago.”

Crash.

The cup in the Drunken Demon’s hand shattered, and liquor spilled across the table.

Han Seol said to Geom Mugeuk,

“Tell your subordinate to send back the scion of the North Blood Sect.”

Leaving those words behind, Han Seol departed.

Silence settled over the place where only the two of them remained. The liquor flowing across the table dripped down to the floor. At length, the Drunken Demon, who had been watching it without a word, broke the silence.

“There is no way she killed herself.”

There was a reason he was so certain.

“She had greater pride in brewing liquor than anyone, and she was satisfied with that work. She was someone who knew just how many people found happiness in the liquor she made. There’s no way.”

The Drunken Demon would know the Mistress better than anyone. If so, then she had not taken her own life, but had been killed by someone.

Geom Mugeuk nodded and emptied the remaining cup of liquor.

“To think I can no longer taste such fine liquor.”

Setting down the empty cup, Geom Mugeuk clasped his hands together in mourning.

The Drunken Demon emptied the liquor in the Blood Tear hanging at his waist, then poured the remaining ice wine into it. He did so with care, not spilling a single drop.

Beyond the flowing, transparent ice wine, the Drunken Demon’s face seemed to waver. The sorrow and rage in his eyes were being poured into the Blood Tear along with the liquor.

Chapter 400: Had We Met, He Would Have Just Made a Drunken Scene
----------------------------------------------------------------
The Palace Lord’s residence of the North Sea Ice Palace stood at the very top of the tower.

The tower was shaped like an icicle leaning as it soared into the sky, and it was mysterious at a mere glance.

At its entrance, Hanseol suddenly recalled something Geommugeuk had once said.

“I am someone who values the reasons of a subordinate more than the organization.”

She abruptly posed a question to Chan, a subordinate who had followed her to the entrance.

“Do you have a reason too?”

Chan asked back, his expression asking what she meant.

“What sort of reason do you mean?”

“I’m asking if there is something I need to understand.”

To begin with, she could not understand this question herself, but—

“There is not.”

Chan answered curtly, and Hanseol left him behind and entered the tower.

Chan stared at her retreating figure with a puzzled expression. At the question she had never once asked before, this thought unexpectedly rose in his mind instead.

‘Young Palace Lord, has something I need to understand come to pass?’

Hanseol walked up the ice stairs. Everything in sight looked like ice. It was a structure built with the unique architecture of the North Sea Ice Palace—ice, yet not ice. Light entering through the windows reflected in various colors, adding to the mystery.

Yet even as she ascended that fantastic space, Hanseol was thinking in an extremely practical manner.

‘This is what’s normal. Why on earth would the word “understanding” be necessary between a leader and a subordinate? Even parents and children fail to understand one another.’

At the top of that tower, her mother was waiting.

The Ice Palace Lord, Hanseogyeong (寒曙景).

An iron woman said to have a heart made of ice.

She was also known as the most outstanding in martial talent among the Ice Palace Lords of recent generations. Of course, since she had never revealed her martial arts to outsiders, no one knew the exact extent of her prowess.

Thus, the strength of the Ice Palace lay in its secrecy and mystery, its power left unrevealed.

“Palace Lord.”

To Hanseol, she was not a mother, but the mother; not the mother, but the Ice Palace Lord.

It had been that way since childhood. She had never embraced her warmly. If Hanseol took one step forward, her mother took two steps back. Hanseol had been raised thoroughly as the Young Palace Lord, and to such a Hanseol, she was a strict Ice Palace Lord.

Had their faces not borne such resemblance that anyone could tell they were mother and daughter, Hanseol might have suspected this was not her flesh and blood.

Now the two stood separated by less than five steps, but the depth of the chasm between them was unfathomable. From opposite ends of that cliff, they gazed at one another.

“The one who kidnapped the second son of the Northern Blood Sect was indeed a martial artist of the Demon Cult, as expected.”

Even though this was an important report revealing the Demon Cult’s intervention in the affairs of the North Sea, the Ice Palace Lord was not greatly agitated.

“It was an act a subordinate committed arbitrarily, and the Young Cult Leader appeared to shield the matter instead.”

The Ice Palace Lord asked for the reason with her eyes, but Hanseol could not answer. Because it was something she herself could not understand.

“He appeared to cherish the subordinate.”

At that, the Ice Palace Lord spoke in a calm tone.

“Even so, we cannot let the bloodline of the Northern Blood Sect die by their hands.”

The Northern Blood Sect had requested aid, and her mother was attempting to honor their request.

Hanseol did not know what transaction had passed between her mother and the Lord of the Northern Blood Sect.

“I have told the Young Cult Leader to hand over the subordinate’s whereabouts for now.”

“If he is indeed a cherished subordinate as you say, would he hand the subordinate over easily?”

“He would not.”

A look telling her to speak the answer she was thinking flew to Hanseol once more.

At times like this, did her mother—no, the Palace Lord—know how suffocating this was?

It had been that way since childhood. She had to find the answer herself. That was the only way to grow stronger, her mother believed. As if that were the one correct answer.

‘Mother, that answer is wrong.’

She had wished that a mother’s embrace would be a place to rest, however briefly, from the world’s gaze urging her to find answers. She had wished that at the end of this road, her mother would be waiting with open arms.

And to the present Hanseol, even such regrets she once harbored had vanished without a trace.

“I will find a way.”

She bowed respectfully and was about to turn away when she spoke as if she had just remembered.

“Ah, the reason the Young Cult Leader came with Chwima was because of the late Lord of Baekju Snowfield.”

“What was Chwima’s reaction to her death?”

“He seemed unable to believe that she took her own life.”

“Chwima will not leave easily, then.”

The Ice Palace Lord knew what relationship Chwima and the Lord of Baekju Snowfield had shared.

“Then I shall take my leave.”

Once more Hanseol bowed respectfully and turned around. Though she was a daughter, she was no different from a subordinate’s report.

Though either might have had reason to show regret at that fact, neither displayed the slightest hint of it.

From behind her, the Ice Palace Lord called.

“Young Palace Lord.”

Hanseol turned around again.

“The Young Cult Leader is the one who achieved the Jeongsama Summit. Do not underestimate him.”

When her mother spoke in such a respectful tone, she was emphasizing that the matter was of true importance.

Hanseol had not yet perceived Geommugeuk to be such a person, but she bowed her head respectfully.

“I shall keep it in mind.”

Through the transparent ice floor, the Ice Palace Lord watched the faint outline of Hanseol descending the tower stairs.

* * *

Geommugeuk and Chwima stood upon a snow-covered field.

Chwima, who had been standing there for some time, abruptly spoke.

“It was here. The place where the Lord first told me she liked me.”

It had been before she became a Demon Lord, in the prime of her youth.

“Why didn’t you accept her feelings?”

Chwima turned his head to look at Geommugeuk.

“Back then, even a single drink was enough to overwhelm my life.”

“What do you mean?”

“The competitors to become the Chwima were obsessed with alcohol. On the other hand, I…”

“You didn’t love it as much as they did.”

Chwima nodded meekly.

It was the first time Geommugeuk had heard this. Chwima had seemed like someone who loved alcohol more than anyone from the very start.

Yes, sometimes life pushes one in directions never imagined. A person who could not kill an ant might become an assassin, and a person who shuddered at the mere sight of a blade might become a famed swordsman.

And before one knows it, one adapts all too well.

“But you like alcohol now, don’t you?”

Chwima cracked a smile.

“Like it? It’s my whole life.”

Geommugeuk laughed along with him.

“I owe much to her for learning to love alcohol. At first the relationship was awkward, but gradually we exchanged letters purely about drink. Stories of alcohol she sent me, and we talked about all manner of spirits in the Central Plains.”

Geommugeuk could feel it. She had been Chwima’s soulmate, bound through drink.

“That I rose to this position, that my martial arts could reach accomplishment—if you trace it back, I owe much to her. I knew she was someone to be grateful for, yet I am only coming now. That is how selfish a man I am.”

That was the source of Chwima’s rage. The sorrow of losing a soulmate, and rage at a reality that could no longer be undone.

That is how life is.

Geommugeuk had been about to say this, but stopped. Those were not the words Chwima wished to hear right now.

“You’re a bastard!”

Chwima nodded.

“Yes, I’m a bastard.”

As if that confession had been awaited, snowflakes began to fall. In the Central Plains, flower petals would be scattering; here, it snowed.

Chwima looked up at the sky.

“I’m a bastard!”

Chwima’s cry echoed and spread.

Geommugeuk looked up at the falling sky with him.

“Where in the world is there a good Demon Lord? They are all bastards. She will understand you too, Hyung. She must have been happy, sending you alcohol. Perhaps it was better that you corresponded without meeting. Had you met, you would have simply caused a drunken scene. You did well. That would have been better for her.”

Chwima looked at Geommugeuk. He would never know what great comfort those words were.

Seeing those trembling eyes, Geommugeuk nodded silently once and moved to the next step.

“Still, we must catch whoever did this, yes?”

Since Chwima was convinced it was not suicide, there must be someone who drove her to her death.

“To investigate that matter, we must enter the Ice Palace.”

To Chwima’s worry, Geommugeuk had already formed a plan.

“The fact that the North Sea Ice Palace stepped into the Northern Blood Sect’s affairs means there are circumstances compelling them to honor that request.”

“That must be it.”

“Then if we resolve that matter for them, they will grant us leave to investigate this as well.”

Chwima understood Geommugeuk’s intent. He intended to negotiate: return the second son of the Northern Blood Sect’s Lord in exchange for permission to investigate.

“What if they have already killed him?”

“If they wanted to kill him, they would have killed him from the start rather than kidnapping him. The fact that they kidnapped him means it is to save someone.”

“Do you know where they are now?”

As expected, the answer came without hesitation.

“Having carried out a matter this important, they would have informed Gowol of their whereabouts through Eunwol, if no one else. The fact that they have not been discovered by the Northern Blood Sect’s or the Ice Palace’s search parties proves it. They are likely at Eunwol’s private residence in the North Sea right now.”

Geommugeuk knew better than anyone how meticulously Gowol handled matters. Gowol’s private residence would truly be a hidden safe haven.

“Don’t you know your subordinate too well?”

“What else would a heart be but a heart? Let’s go.”

Geommugeuk and Chwima began walking slowly through the thickening snow.

* * *

“No word came from the Northern Blood Sect before the appointed time.”

Ian had not thought the Lord of the Northern Blood Sect would abandon his own child.

‘They say the martial world is heartless, but this is truly too much.’

If it were me, I would have cast aside my life for my child. Is he truly doing this?

Though he had expected it, when reality crashed down upon him, Yangjung’s heart sank to the bottom. His father had traded the honor of the Northern Blood Sect for his son’s life.

‘Kill me!’

He had wanted to shout it boldly, but could not get the words out. He wanted to live.

“If you kill me, the main sect will surely take revenge.”

He was still begging for his life in the wrong manner.

Meanwhile, Jugyu and Hwangchu displayed no useless pride.

“Please spare us!”

The two still had their eyes tightly shut. Their desire to live was fierce.

“Now, you two open your eyes too. It is time for us all to think together.”

Eventually Jugyu and Hwangchu opened their eyes. Ian had lit a lantern in the dark room, so they could see her face.

The two were startled. They had never dreamed that the woman who had captured them could be so beautiful.

‘You crazy bitch! Who the hell are you?’

So much so that such words nearly burst out. Jugyu truly could not understand. With such beauty, she could have lived a truly thrilling life without resorting to kidnapping the bloodline of the Northern Blood Sect.

“The threats did not work. What should we do now?”

Ian approached close to them. Up close, she was even more beautiful.

“Shall I cut off a hand and send it?”

Jugyu, who had been staring blankly at her, unconsciously nodded and then flinched.

“Whose hand?”

At that, Yangjung glared at him. Whose hand? His own hand, obviously! How dare he ask when he knew full well!

“It won’t work! Since they have already given up on my life, would sending a severed hand make them yield?”

Before he knew it, Yangjung had hidden both hands behind his back. Ian had frightened him.

“It could be a bluff, couldn’t it? Thinking, surely they wouldn’t actually kill me.”

Ian leaned in and whispered softly to Yangjung.

“I will cut off your left hand, the one you do not use for your sword.”

When she gripped the sword hilt as if to strike immediately, Yangjung screamed in terror.

“I told you it won’t work!”

Ian took her hand from the sword.

“Right? Then what to do? Shall I really cut off a head and send it?”

The words from her pleasant, gentle voice were each and every one horrifying.

“So? What do you gain?”

“At least my mood would improve.”

When Yangjung trembled in fear and could say nothing, Jugyu stepped in.

“Then you will kill all the people you are trying to save.”

Ian stared at him and said.

“What can I do? What? Did you think I would feel guilty over that? Even if it were not me, they would kill those people miserably anyway, would they not? No? Did you truly think I brought you here to earn you money?”

Naturally, they could say nothing.

“Now, I have given my answer, so you give me yours. Otherwise I will simply kill you all and send you to the Northern Blood Sect, and I will disappear like smoke.”

There was a reason Ian was frightening them.

She thought that if they tossed out various answers, a solution might be found. Even a single clue would do.

‘What would the Young Master have done?’

She thought on it, but only felt that she was still far from his level.

Undoubtedly, a solution to this problem existed. So that years later, when she looked back on this moment, there would be a clear correct answer to make her kick her blanket in frustration, lamenting that she should have done it that way.

But for now, she did not know what to do. She was suffocated, as if a blanket covered her face.

“Please release me! I will go and persuade the Sect Lord.”

Jugyu was struggling to survive by any means, even if only he survived.

But Yangjung cut him off, though they could not persuade anyone together.

“Would that work?”

Ignoring Jugyu’s opinion, he presented a new plan.

“Kidnap my older brother. Then my father will surrender.”

Ian was speechless. Like father, like son, these people truly—! That suggestion must be half sincere. The other half must be for such a reason.

“There must be experts swarming around your brother right now. Are you telling me to get caught?”

Struck to the core, Yangjung flinched.

“I was merely stating my thoughts.”

“Wrong answer. Now, next answer?”

The three captives racked their brains.

In fact, what Ian would attempt was already decided.

‘I will have to make a different proposal and entice them.’

Because there were people she needed to save.

But the moment she made a new proposal, she would lose the initiative to them. These were people who did not even blink at the prospect of killing their children.

‘I miss the days when I simply guarded the Young Master’s side without a thought in my head.’

It was at that moment, as she searched for an answer, that someone knocked on the door from outside.

Ian, as well as the three men, were all startled.

Yangjung turned to the other two with a triumphant smile. Jugyu and Hwangchu’s expressions brightened as well.

‘We’re saved! They’ve come to rescue us!’

‘You’re dead now!’

They smiled coldly. Jugyu signaled with his eyes. Do not act rashly and keep your mouths shut. She might kill them all and face the rescuers outside.

But it was an unnecessary warning. Yangjung wanted to live just as much as Jugyu. Yangjung was not even breathing.

With sword drawn, Ian asked in a low voice.

“Who is it?”

Then, from beyond the door, came a most welcome voice.

“The answer.”
